# Development and verification

Cabal is the canonical package description. Nix supplies a reproducible development environment; it does not replace the Cabal build or impose a Nix dependency on SDK users.

Development resumed on 2026-09-08. Operational recovery state is kept in the repository-only `docs/HANDOFF.md`; `docs/evidence/2026-09-08/README.md` describes the preserved stopping-point receipts. Those recovery artifacts are not shipped in the Cabal archive. Checkpoint narratives below retain their original scope and counts rather than claiming current overall completion.

## Functional delivery order and verification cadence

Local/daemon sessions, external and hosted MCP with native validation and resolved redistribution rights, REST/advanced resources, coordinated state, native transports, discovery, configuration, stream/helpers and observability have verified implementations. Timestamp and attribution contracts follow the approved choices documented below. GHC 9.12.4 macOS/GNU/Linux ARM64 suites, bounded local/daemon interoperability and separate source-distribution builds provide the scoped release evidence. Unexecuted platforms and live resource mutations are not implicitly certified.

Do not extend or reharden accepted capabilities except for a demonstrated bug or a named remaining requirement. Saved-session discovery and configuration are now accepted offline, including native Mac/Linux timestamp evidence. Required codecs and operation contracts belong to their capability slices, not a preceding exhaustive inventory phase. Final conformance still requires all functional work and explicit resolution of its open gates.

Use focused compilation and behavior regressions while implementing. Run broader build/test/lint/format checks at coherent milestone boundaries or after consequential shared-code changes. Reserve repeated stress runs for demonstrated concurrency risks, and full Haddock/source-distribution cycles for release/sign-off boundaries or packaging changes. Do not repeat them after each wrapper or minor adjustment. Progress reports identify capabilities finished, missing and under active delivery; test counts are supporting evidence, not a completion measure.

## Current local SDK delivery

The local prompt/stream/result/session path is implemented; exhaustive codec coverage remains an opt-in backlog. Its frozen CLI reference is 0.212.1 / protocol 1.201.1, with later live CLI versions recorded in their own receipts. `Factory.Droid` uses the established stream-JSON-RPC arguments. Minimal initialization supplies `machineId`, absolute `cwd`, optional `modelId` and `autoRejectPermissionRequests = true`. Optional SDK-language attribution is left unset; CLI authentication is reused rather than reimplemented.

At the initial usable-path checkpoint, eleven native offline-peer tests exercised one-shot output, streaming and same-session follow-up, prefilled messages, untagged local notifications, rejected permission/question requests, timeouts, callback failure, cancellation, malformed events and structured startup errors. The API and `droid-example` built with `-Werror`; all 2,691 Haskell tests and five reference-audit tests passed, as did HLint, Ormolu and actionlint.

The first authorized live attempt exposed a real interoperability defect: local `droid.session_notification` parameters may omit `sessionId`, but the initial high-level parser required it. The installed 1.201.1 declaration and the supplied reference schema both make that field optional. The corrected parser accepts untagged notifications within the owned local session while still filtering explicit foreign session IDs; the native peer now covers this case.

The second authorized attempt used a small compiled Haskell check against CLI 0.212.1 in a fresh temporary directory, with existing authentication and permission rejection enabled. It streamed `HELLO` at 4.476 seconds, asserted matching final text and session ID after explicit completion, and returned from the session scope with exit code zero at 6.771 seconds. The monitored direct child no longer existed afterward. Two prompt attempts were used in total, including the failed first attempt. Same-session follow-up is verified offline; a complete live two-turn run is not claimed.

MVP evidence is retained at `/tmp/droid-local-final.z239ap7y`: `live-once.hs`, `live-second-evidence.json`, the live output logs, `focused-tests.log` and `final-checks.log`. Logs contain the public test response and process/check metadata, not credentials or raw initialization payloads. Verification used the pinned native GHC 9.10.3 and Cabal 3.16.1.0 tools directly after a transient macOS loader stall delayed `nix develop` and compiler subprocess startup; no SDK workaround or disabled check was introduced.

`withResumedDroidSession` now loads an existing identifier through the same owned process/channel/dispatcher scope. It sends `droid.load_session` directly with permission rejection enabled, checks the required session/history/settings containers, and retains the requested identifier. It does not replay saved history into the new turn's text callback. Stored model settings remain unchanged unless an explicit `droidModel` override is applied through `droid.update_session_settings` before exposing the session. The launch directory does not overwrite the CLI's restored working directory.

The load and model-update shapes were checked against Python `client.py:468–526`, the published TypeScript client `dist/node.mjs:902–935,1082–1100`, and the selected CLI's embedded declarations near bytes 204402133, 204403610 and 204407546. These are focused operational projections, not newly claimed exhaustive 1.205.0 codecs. Eight additional native-peer cases cover resumed turns/history isolation, stored/overridden model behavior, missing sessions, rejected overrides, three malformed load-result shapes and cancellation/reaping. Permission defaults are exercised on the resumed path as well. At this checkpoint, nineteen high-level cases and the full 2,699-test suite passed, as did build, HLint and Ormolu. Evidence: `/tmp/droid-resume-check.zV8gwl/checks.log`. No additional live model request or repeated full distribution pass was made for this increment.

`listDroidModels`, `getDroidContextStats` and `getDroidContextBreakdown` expose existing typed client operations on an owned session without caller-managed request IDs or envelopes. Startup, prompts and queries share one atomic ID source. Queries use a thirty-second exchange deadline without acquiring the prompt lock, so a text callback can request metadata. A handled ordinary query/result error leaves the session usable; uncaught callback errors and transport failures retain their existing behavior.

Five new native-peer cases verify model options/extensions/availability, typed context snapshots on resumed sessions, callback reentrancy, nonterminal query errors and rejection of invalidated handles. Consumed result shapes were checked in the selected CLI bundle near bytes 204348947, 204399247 and 204412874. At this checkpoint, twenty-four high-level cases and the full 2,704-test suite passed with `-Werror`, HLint and Ormolu. Evidence: `/tmp/droid-session-queries.LPH5aG/checks.log`. These wrappers add no codecs or dependencies, and no new live call is claimed.

`renameDroidSession`, `changeDroidWorkingDirectory` and `getDroidRewindInfo` now reuse the same ordinary session-request mechanism. Rename preserves the actual Boolean success result; directory changes return the peer's path without mutating the SDK process directory; rewind information injects the owned session ID without accessing files. The baselined Python/TypeScript lifecycle guards permit ordinary non-replacing calls during a turn, while replacement-producing operations require additional coordination. The three consumed wire shapes were checked near CLI bundle bytes 204414484, 204408212 and 204413474.

Five native cases cover success/false/empty-title behavior, resolved paths and unchanged caller directory, callback use on resumed sessions, structured/invalid-result failures, and invalidated handles. Callback tests also assert that the callback actually ran. At this checkpoint, twenty-nine high-level cases and the full 2,709-test suite passed with `-Werror`, HLint and Ormolu. Evidence: `/tmp/droid-session-controls.u7j0Kk/checks.log`. No new codec, dependency, live call or distribution rebuild was introduced.

Local `forkDroidSession`, `compactDroidSession` and `rewindDroidSession` share one replacement path. A connection owns the channel, dispatcher, ID allocator and availability signal; each handle owns lifecycle state, in-flight admission and exclusive turn admission. The later independent-attachment work moved the original connection-wide turn lock onto the handle; [current overlap calibration](evidence/2026-09-17/lifecycle-turns/README.md) makes competing prompts fail fast rather than queue implicitly. Replacement rejects active turns, blocks new source-handle requests and drains existing ordinary requests before switching the CLI's active session. Successful attachment atomically retires the source and publishes a successor owned by the original scope. Failed attachment reloads the source; failed rollback or uncertain operation outcomes invalidate it. Compaction uses the reference 240-second exchange deadline; rewind and loading use 60 seconds.

Retirement is the linearization point, not a promise of atomic caller receipt. Cancellation delivered before commit is handled by the attachment/rollback path; cancellation arriving while masked may be delivered after a successful commit. The old handle then remains replaced, records its target ID and does not own a new process. The original scope still owns the connection. A review initially treated this as a transport handoff defect, then withdrew that finding after distinguishing committed-operation ambiguity from resource leakage. No additional successor registry or artificial pre-commit checkpoint was added.

Review did identify a real admission defect: cancellation of an ordinary mutating RPC could release its local lease while remote work remained pending, allowing replacement to cross it. The native regression failed with a peer trace of rename → fork → successor load. Ordinary request effects are now explicit: uncertain mutation outcomes invalidate under a mask before releasing their lease. The drain rechecks lifecycle state, and its error cleanup restores readiness only while still replacing. A further double-cancellation review tightened the classification/state-transition mask while leaving the RPC and interruption wait interruptible.

RPC response delivery is independent of queued notification dispatch. `synchronizeRpcEvents` adds an internal event-prefix boundary, acknowledged when the sole consumer advances past prior events; it sends no wire frame and does not join asynchronously spawned server-request workers. Startup/load operations include that boundary in their existing 60-second budget before exposing a handle. Four dispatcher tests cover reply-before-callback ordering, completion without another event, cancelled boundaries and terminal wakeup. Native replacement tests replay history on both successor and rollback loads.

The replacement peer retains saved session state and independent request traces. Gates use private temporary files; load/mutation gates complete a server-request response first so the initiating write is known to have released the channel writer before cancellation. The drain gate observes/rejects premature requests rather than masking them with serial processing; temporarily removing the drain check made its regression fail, after which the guard was restored. Tests require healthy cancelled loads to attempt and complete rollback, separately cover rollback cancellation and unknown mutating outcomes, and retain post-commit ownership checks. The standard `directory` package was added only as a direct test dependency for owned gate cleanup.

At the replacement checkpoint, 48 high-level cases, 20 dispatcher cases and the full 2,732-test suite passed with `-Werror`, HLint and Ormolu. Fifty parallel repetitions of the 94 channel/dispatcher/high-level cases passed with four test workers and two GHC capabilities. Evidence is retained at `/tmp/droid-replacement-check.R1qMK9`, including `mutation-before.log`, `drain-mutant.log`, `verified-checks.log` and `verified-stress-*.log`. These are offline results, not new live CLI/daemon verification or complete functional SDK sign-off.

`sendDroidTurn` now returns explicit successful, interrupted and failed outcomes through `DroidResult.resultCompletion`, reusing `AgentTurnCompleted` and `ErrorNotification` codecs. It preserves partial text, reported usage/duration and ordered error events until matching completion. The success-checking `sendPrompt` wrapper accepts completed/spec-handoff outcomes and otherwise throws `DroidTurnFailed` with a typed completion reason after the session settles. Known terminal failures therefore permit reuse; tests that exercise truly unusable handles now use malformed events rather than completed model failures.

Turn IDs are generated with `Data.UUID.V4.nextRandom` from `uuid-1.3.16.1`, rather than connection-local request counters that could repeat after resume. The [documented UUID API](https://hackage.haskell.org/package/uuid-1.3.16/docs/Data-UUID-V4.html) uses system entropy; no credential helper is involved. The fresh ID is sent as `messageId`; completion requires a matching `turnId`, following Python `streaming.py:359–379` and `session.py:694–746`. The selected CLI declares the field near byte 204388975 and forwards it in its completion event near byte 218887723. Missing IDs fail this operational correlation contract even though the general wire type permits omission in other contexts.

`interruptDroidSession` registers a lease only for a running turn, waits for submission acknowledgement, and sends an ordinary interruption request. Separate pending-interrupt accounting fences the following prompt until the stop request settles. The existing masked mutation policy protects uncertain sent stops; cancellation while waiting to send releases the fence without invalidating the active turn. Idle interruption sends no frame. The same leases prevent pending stops from crossing a replacement boundary.

Fourteen new native cases cover typed terminal metadata/errors, settled failure reuse, spec/permission outcomes, durable IDs, foreign/missing completion IDs, callback interruption, idle/retired behavior, delayed stop/submission acknowledgements, rejection/retry and both cancellation stages. Temporarily disabling either ordering guard made its targeted test fail; both guards were restored. All 62 high-level cases and all 2,746 tests passed with `-Werror`; HLint and Ormolu passed. Thirty parallel runs of 108 high-level/channel/dispatcher cases passed. Evidence: `/tmp/droid-turn-outcomes.6buQQQ` (`verified-checks.log`, `stress-*.log`, `next-turn-mutant.log`, `submission-mutant.log`). Independent review found no issues. The README terminal example compiled without execution. Haddock required dependency documentation builds and an intermediate timed-out pass; the final offline pass completed with link warnings (`haddock-retry.log`, exit zero). `cabal check` retained its no-repository advisory. New dependency archives were fetched for the native build; no Factory/model request was made. That checkpoint left full message/event streaming and structured-output handling for subsequent work.

`sendDroidEvents` now exposes complete-message and all-event callback modes through the same correlated turn engine. The internal stream reducer replaces the former text-only accumulator; it adds no transport reader, lifecycle owner, dependency or unused codec. Existing notification/message types retain full payloads, while unadapted inner notifications remain explicit raw events. Both modes accumulate the same terminal result, including complete-event history and capture-only structured output. Full messages require `id`, `role`, `content`, `createdAt` and `updatedAt`; the selected CLI's declaration was checked near byte 204323447. This is a static 1.201.1 contract check, not a live run or a claim that the separate 1.205.0 corpus is exhaustive.

Independent review exposed stale snapshot preference: a complete earlier message could hide a later partial answer, including same-message deltas after a snapshot. Terminal text now reads current buffers in message observation order rather than cached snapshot text. A second review exposed duplicate callback suffixes after snapshot shortening/regrowth. Delivered prefixes now remain separate from authoritative buffers and survive corrections/retractions; both deltas and snapshots use the same delivery rule. Divergent blocks cannot be repaired through append-only callbacks; rich events remain available for a replaceable display, as documented in the README. Twelve targeted cases failed before their respective fixes (`snapshot-before.log`, `delivery-before.log`). The final focused review found no remaining issues; it inspected source and logs without independently executing tests.

Twenty-five additional native cases covered event order/filtering, tool/hook/message delivery, raw and malformed payloads, output null/retraction, callback failures, interruption/reuse and the reducer regressions. At that checkpoint, all 87 high-level cases and all 2,771 tests passed with `-Werror`; HLint and Ormolu passed. Twenty parallel runs of 133 high-level/channel/dispatcher cases passed. The README event example compiled without execution; Haddock completed with link warnings, and `cabal check` retained its no-repository advisory. Evidence: `/tmp/droid-event-stream.DNWgK9` (`rechecked-final.log`, `rechecked-stress-*.log`, `haddock-final.log`, `message-runtime-declaration.txt`, `EventExample.hs`). No additional Factory/model call or whole source-distribution cycle was performed. That checkpoint left requested output formats, local output validation and attachment submission for subsequent work.

`rawDroidOutput` and `jsonDroidOutput` now prepare explicit object-rooted schemas. `sendDroidOutput` and `sendDroidOutputEvents` request those formats and return the unchanged CLI completion outcome alongside local object/`FromJSON` decoding results. Preparation rejects non-object schema roots before submission. Existing `OutputFormat`, `AddUserMessageParams` and `Client.addUserMessage` now supply the common turn request; ordinary text/event entry points use the same path with the output option absent. No new dependency or wire codec was added.

The operational comparison follows Python `_high_level/output.py:45–122` and `streaming.py:300–313,621–690`: raw mode checks objects, typed mode applies a decoder, notification data takes precedence and absent/null output falls back to the entire plain JSON text. Markdown fences and non-object text are not accepted. Local validation does not rewrite the peer's completion reason; a decoded value can accompany an interrupted/failed turn, and a completed turn can have missing/invalid output. Haskell schemas are supplied explicitly, and local adaptation follows the settled turn outside its deadline. The README records these API choices rather than claiming automatic schema derivation, full JSON Schema keyword validation or decoder/schema equivalence. CLI 1.201.1 output-format and add-message declarations were checked near bytes 204400126 and 204402684 (`runtime-contract.txt`).

Twenty-four new checks covered schema preparation, exact wire fields/extensions, notification precedence, plain/null fallback, missing/fenced/non-object/retracted output, structural/custom decoding, raw-mode limits, failed-turn preservation, reuse, callback exceptions, interruption, malformed notifications and redaction. At that checkpoint, all 111 high-level cases and all 2,795 tests passed with `-Werror`; HLint and Ormolu passed. Twenty parallel runs of 157 high-level/channel/dispatcher cases passed. The typed README example compiled without execution, and Haddock completed with link warnings. The no-repository Cabal advisory remained. Evidence: `/tmp/droid-structured-output.yazP1L` (`verified-checks.log`, `stress-*.log`, `StructuredExample.hs`, `haddock.log`, `runtime-contract.txt`). No new live call or complete source-distribution cycle was performed; live structured-output interoperability remains unverified.

`Factory.Droid.Input` now supplies private validated image and document values plus `DroidInput`. Four input entry points combine attachments with normalized text callbacks, rich events and requested typed/raw output; the original text APIs remain wrappers around that same request/turn path. Payload bounds follow Python `_high_level/attachments.py`: 5 MiB for image bytes and UTF-8 text, 3 MiB for PDF bytes. Image MIME/signature checks and PDF signatures are not full format parsing. Source constructors retain metadata/extensions and do not open metadata paths. `base64-bytestring-1.2.1.0` supplies [strict RFC 4648 decoding](https://hackage.haskell.org/package/base64-bytestring-1.2.1.0/docs/Data-ByteString-Base64.html); its canonical pad-bit requirement deliberately rejects aliases accepted by CPython's validating decoder. `filepath` is also a direct library dependency. No new wire codec was added.

File reads reject NUL paths and non-regular files, compare pre-open and descriptor identity/size/high-resolution modification/change timestamps, and read no more than the checked size plus one byte. Nonblocking, close-on-exec opens prevent a checked pathname's replacement by a FIFO from waiting for a writer; one bracket owns the descriptor. Post-read metadata/length checks detect observed changes, not an atomic or tamper-proof snapshot. No filesystem-latency deadline is claimed. `droidFrameLimitBytes` makes the existing 10 MiB encoded JSONL limit explicit and configurable. Native peers verify two individually valid 5 MiB images fail at the default ceiling and succeed at 20 MiB, without truncation. Frame failures retain the established unusable-channel/session policy.

Review found the implementation coherent but identified missing race/FD regression evidence. `scripts/check_attachment_files.py` now loads the actual source into GHCi and uses controlled breakpoints, not sleeps or SDK test hooks. It checks pre-open FIFO and inode replacement, mutation after acquisition, `ThreadKilled` identity and observed descriptor closure, and a forced short read followed by continuation within the byte budget. The driver requires all markers, rejects interpreter errors and terminates its owned process group on timeout. An always-true snapshot predicate made mutation and identity-replacement checks fail; the predicate was restored. These interpreted-source probes supplement compiled native tests; they do not count close syscalls or cover cancellation within every acquisition/release syscall. CI invokes them after ordinary and packaged-source tests. Focused review of the final code, probe driver and logs found no issues, without independent execution by the reviewer.

At this checkpoint, 34 input cases and 123 high-level cases contribute to all 2,841 passing Haskell tests; five audit tests also pass. Twenty parallel repetitions of 203 attachment/high-level/channel/dispatcher cases pass, as do `-Werror` builds, HLint, Ormolu, actionlint and the controlled FD/race probes. The attachment example compiles without execution. Haddock completed after fetching the previously uncached Base64 source archive; link warnings and the no-repository Cabal advisory remain. Evidence: `/tmp/droid-attachments.5GtLNM` (`verified-checks-final.log`, `stress-*.log`, `file-probes.log`, `file-snapshot-mutant.log`, `AttachmentExample.hs`, `haddock-online.log`). The selected CLI's image/document declarations were checked near bytes 204321767 and 204322841 (`runtime-source-contracts.txt`). `cabal sdist --list-only` confirms the input module/tests, FD probe and optional codec-backlog document are included; it is not an independent distribution rebuild. These checks use Apple Silicon macOS/GHC 9.10.3; no new Factory/model request, full source-distribution rebuild or broader platform verification is claimed.

`updateDroidSettings`, `listDroidTools`, `listDroidCommands`, `listDroidSkills` and `setDroidSkillDisabled` now expose scoped settings/discovery controls. Settings support model/reasoning, legacy/current mode, autonomy, nullable spec overrides, mission models, tags, compaction and tool-ID lists. Two new typed client bindings reuse the existing executor; resumed model overrides now use that same settings binding. Queries are read-only; mutations reuse masked invalidation and lease release. Acknowledgements and allow-state flags remain peer data. No optimistic settings snapshot is maintained.

`Schema.Settings` supplied only required operation bodies at this checkpoint. The selected CLI declares invalid mode/autonomy fallbacks to absence near bytes 204403610 and 204410200; other optional fields remain strict, and explicit spec null resets survive encoding. The request shapes match the supplied schema, including number-domain compaction limits and nonnegative integer query depth. Python `client.py:626–703,1258–1323` and `_session_operations.py:100–240,469–494` supply operation references. Haskell preserves ordered/duplicate tool IDs from the wire rather than adopting the Python high-level frozenset conversion. System-prompt configuration, observed settings snapshots and customizable interaction handlers were left for subsequent work.

Twenty-three codec cases, eight binding checks and seven new native cases cover field ownership, runtime fallbacks, absent/null/value resets, nested validation, exact requests, hypothetical queries, metadata-only discovery, false acknowledgements, callback reentrancy, rejection/malformed-result reuse, retired handles and cancellation before replacement admission. All 130 high-level cases and all 2,879 tests pass with `-Werror`; HLint and Ormolu pass. Twenty parallel repetitions of 199 settings/high-level/channel/dispatcher cases pass. The README settings example compiles without execution; Haddock completes with existing link warnings, and the no-repository advisory remains. Evidence: `/tmp/droid-settings.4BGpSJ` (`focused-retry.log`, `verified-checks.log`, `stress-*.log`, `SettingsExample.hs`, `runtime-contracts.txt`). A fixture compile timeout was followed by a successful pinned-toolchain retry; no check was weakened.

Post-Haddock verification exposed a probe-driver package-resolution defect: broad GHCi dependency-name flags could expose a second Base64 unit beside Cabal's selected unit. The driver now exposes only the SDK and retains Cabal's exact dependency environment. Attachment FD/race probes pass again, including after Haddock (`file-probe-final.log`, `file-probe-after-haddock.log`). No new dependency, live Factory/model call, full source-distribution cycle or broader platform execution is claimed.

`droidSystemPrompt` now configures new sessions with validated custom text or built-in-plus-append instructions. `Schema.SystemPrompt` implements the selected CLI's ECMAScript non-whitespace rule and preset preprocessing (`Ixh`/`bAR` near bytes 204378316/204378395). Preset tags are normalized even if absent or invalid; unknown fields, missing append text and whitespace-only strings fail. Text is preserved, not trimmed. A development-only Node regex probe enumerated the whitespace set, and Haskell tests include NEL/BOM distinctions. This is not a Node runtime dependency. Load and update request declarations do not support prompt overrides: explicit resume overrides raise `DroidSystemPromptRequiresNewSession` before launch, and successor/rollback load frames remain unchanged.

`SettingsUpdatedEvent` added a typed `SettingsUpdated`/`SettingsChange` payload through `AllEvents`, retaining request correlation metadata and offered autonomy levels. The selected notification declaration (`wet`, near byte 204386523) is partial and non-nullable for spec values, unlike a mutation patch. Invalid available-autonomy arrays fall back as a whole to absence; an empty valid array remains explicit. At that checkpoint, events did not populate a cache and were not a complete settings snapshot. Complete-message callbacks still omit settings metadata; malformed adapted events fail in either mode.

Thirty-two prompt tests, four additional settings-codec cases and nine new native cases cover Unicode whitespace, preset normalization, exact initialization encoding, omission, pre-launch resume rejection, unchanged replacement loads, typed event payloads and malformed settings notifications. All 139 high-level cases and all 2,924 tests pass with `-Werror`, HLint and Ormolu. Twenty parallel repetitions of 244 prompt/settings/high-level/channel/dispatcher cases pass; attachment FD/race probes remain green. The README prompt example compiles without execution. Haddock completes with existing link warnings; `cabal check` retains the no-repository advisory. Evidence: `/tmp/droid-system-prompts.umQ4a1` (`verified-checks.log`, `stress-*.log`, `SystemPromptExample.hs`, `runtime-contracts.txt`, `ecmascript-whitespace.txt`). No new dependency, Factory/model call, source-distribution rebuild or broader platform verification is claimed.

`withDroidSessionHandlers` and `withResumedDroidSessionHandlers` now accept connection-scoped permission/question callbacks and optional payload-free failure reporting. Unconfigured callbacks preserve quiet cancellation. Only a supplied permission handler disables automatic permission rejection, and that flag is retained on resumed/successor loads. Registration precedes initialization/loading. Existing dispatcher workers remain the sole callback owners; their cancellation/join behavior is unchanged.

The refined `RequestPermissionResult` codec requires edited text for `proceed_edit`, including the empty string, following the selected CLI declaration near byte 204394882. The adapter separately checks offered choices. Question responses must match index/question identities, contain no duplicate indices and contain no answers when cancelled; free text and comma-space multi-select match the reference. Existing typed confirmation details are delivered without executing or reinterpreting them. Ordinary malformed/invalid/callback failures yield safe cancellation with optional failure classifications, not exception payloads. `deepseq` is now a direct boot-library dependency so outgoing JSON is fully evaluated inside recovery. `trySync` moved unchanged into a shared internal module, preserving asynchronous exception classification in both the adapter and dispatcher.

Thirty-four handler/codec checks and thirteen native cases cover all permission outcomes, edits, offered-option enforcement, deep latent callback failures, answer validation, observer failures, asynchronous cancellation, initialization/load flags, concurrent callbacks and nested scoped queries. Native tests assert that workers remain pending after turn cancellation and source retirement, that the original RPC response is received after a successor is active, and that both finalizers complete before the original scope returns. This is intentional connection lifetime, not per-turn ownership. Focused read-only review found no issues; it inspected source/tests rather than independently executing them.

All 152 high-level cases and all 2,971 Haskell tests pass with `-Werror`, HLint and Ormolu. Thirty parallel repetitions of 232 interaction/high-level/channel/dispatcher cases pass. The rejecting-handler README example compiles without execution; Haddock completes with existing link warnings and the no-repository Cabal advisory remains. Attachment FD/race probes passed on retry; two intervening tool runs stalled before probe output, including one recorded timeout, and `/usr/bin/python3` with the same pinned Haskell toolchain completed the unchanged probe deadline. No SDK workaround or suppressed failure was introduced. Evidence: `/tmp/droid-handlers.CHrudH` (`lifetime-checks.log`, `verified-final.log`, `stress-*.log`, `file-probe-system-python.log`, `HandlerExample.hs`, `haddock.log`). No new live Factory/model request, full source-distribution rebuild or broader platform verification is claimed.

`onDroidSessionEvent` now exposes per-handle typed session notifications outside turns through the existing dispatcher intake. It adds no transport reader, worker or settings cache. Observation accepts the wire completion shape without imposing an active turn ID; the correlated turn decoder keeps its mandatory matching-ID rule. Malformed adapted payloads and channel failure are explicit error values. Notification admission checks handle lifecycle, so replacing/retired/unavailable handles do not follow successor traffic.

The callback contract remains the dispatcher's: ordinary exceptions are isolated, callbacks must be brief, and operations that wait for the same intake (new turns, replacement/load or event synchronization) cannot run inside them. Ordinary metadata queries are supported. Unsubscribe removes later admission but does not join already admitted callbacks; connection scope still bounds all registrations. Nine new native cases verify outside-turn settings delivery, query reentrancy, coexistence with streams, uncorrelated completion, optional/foreign routing, malformed/callback failures, unsubscribe and admitted-work completion, retired filtering and channel failure. All 161 high-level cases and all 2,980 tests pass with `-Werror`, HLint and Ormolu. Thirty parallel runs of 207 high-level/channel/dispatcher checks pass, as do attachment FD/race probes and five audit tests. The observer example compiles without execution; Haddock completes with existing link warnings, and the no-repository advisory remains. Evidence: `/tmp/droid-observers.pg0vql` (`final-checks.log`, `stress-*.log`, `file-probes.log`, `ObserverExample.hs`, `haddock.log`). No new dependency, codec, live call or independent source-distribution cycle was introduced.

`getDroidSettings` now returns typed last dispatcher-observed `SessionSettings`. The existing channel queues an optional `requestReplyObserved` action atomically with first response acceptance while preserving immediate TMVar correlation. Dequeue and observation commit in one STM transaction; accepted observations survive waiter cancellation, and unknown, duplicate, malformed or synthetic failure responses add none. A connection-owned notification reducer registers before startup and user observers. Full replies and notifications therefore share one receive-order fold; no second reader, worker, epoch counter or dependency was introduced.

The snapshot codec follows `SessionSettingsSchema` and the selected CLI's `O8H` declaration at byte 204404190. Static inspection of `emitSettingsUpdatedNotification` at byte 218901203 establishes a mixed patch/snapshot contract: every event includes current spec-model/spec-reasoning/mission values when present, so omission clears those observations. Mission objects replace rather than recursively merge. Other fields are patches; `False`, empty lists and empty spec text remain values. Invalid enum fallbacks supply no new observation. The selected emitter omits tags, so tag changes can remain unobserved until a full reply. Snapshot-only extensions, including emitted `sandbox`, are validated through the full codec before exposure. Older Python `_high_level/_convert.py:364–437` instead relies on explicit transmitted null for clearable fields; Haskell follows the selected runtime emitter without relaxing the notification codec.

Getter lifecycle and stored-session-ID checks share one transaction. Replacement hides provisional target state; successful successor and rollback loads replace the whole baseline before publication/restoration. Malformed settings events invalidate only the observed view until a valid full load; partial updates cannot reconstruct it. The getter neither waits nor issues an RPC, so it can be used from a session observer and sees that callback's settings event. Acknowledgements do not install settings or guarantee dispatcher freshness. Receive order does not establish peer snapshot capture time; untagged and same-ID notifications crossing a peer attachment epoch remain ambiguous.

Seven channel tests, one dispatcher test, five schema checks and twelve native cases cover ordered observations, first-response deduplication, cancellation before and after reply return, terminal drain, callback reentrancy, initialization/resume/model overrides, absent/empty/reset semantics, full mission replacement, extensions, malformed recovery and replacement ownership. Disabling spec/mission clearing and first-response admission independently made their regressions fail; both changes were restored. All 3,005 tests pass, including 173 high-level cases; `-Werror` builds, HLint and Ormolu pass. Thirty parallel repetitions of 227 high-level/channel/dispatcher cases pass. Independent read-only review found no issues and judged the change architecturally aligned; it inspected source/tests/logs without executing tests or independently rechecking the CLI binary. Accepted-reply cancellation followed by rollback is covered compositionally, not by a dedicated end-to-end settings assertion. Evidence: `/tmp/droid-observed-settings.pfpTXj` (`verified-final.log`, `stress-*.log`, `reset-mutant.log`, `duplicate-mutant.log`, `runtime-contract.txt`).

The getter/subscription example compiles with `-fno-code -Wall -Werror` and explicit `-package droid-sdk`; it was not executed. Haddock completes with the existing dependency/Generic link warnings, and `cabal check` retains the no-repository advisory. The post-Haddock attachment FD/race probes, five Python audit tests, schema fingerprint/reference audit and actionlint pass (`example-final.log`, `haddock.log`, `file-probes.log`, `audit-tests.log`, `schema-audit.log`). These checks remain Apple Silicon macOS/GHC 9.10.3 evidence. No additional Factory/model call or broader platform execution is claimed.

The original local MVP excluded an unfinished WebSocket experiment, preserved at `/tmp/droid-websocket-deferred.nr56Lv`. The current packaged transport is described below; the archived prototype and earlier package hashes do not certify it. The [exhaustive codec report](exhaustive-codec-backlog.md) remains separate deferred work, not a completion gate.

## Existing-daemon session delivery

`Factory.Droid.Daemon` now provides caller-authenticated new/resumed sessions, shared input/output and text/event turns, interruption, subscriptions and restored pending interactions over `Factory.Droid.Transport.WebSocket`. Ordinary exit owns only disconnection and worker cleanup, not external daemon termination or saved-session closure/deletion. The [README](../README.md#existing-daemon-sessions) supplies the API example and callback/security boundaries; the [operation map](parity.md#existing-daemon-core-operation-contracts) records request, ACK, routing, error and lifecycle contracts.

The local implementation was extracted into private `Internal.Session`, with `Factory.Droid` retained as its public compatibility facade. A small backend record supplies envelope context, notification decoding, submission and interruption. Both backends retain one channel, dispatcher, session/turn ownership path, stream reducer and interaction adapters. Restored load-snapshot requests use the dispatcher's existing worker admission and cleanup rather than a separate callback mechanism.

Each WebSocket socket remains owned throughout connection setup and scoped cleanup. Default certificate/hostname verification remains enabled; private roots and plaintext are explicit policies. DNS/TCP/TLS/HTTP setup shares one 60-second deadline; HTTP headers have a 64-KiB budget, text objects a configurable 10-MiB default limit and graceful shutdown a one-second default budget. Binary messages, invalid UTF-8 and non-object JSON fail explicitly; compression is not negotiated. Daemon authentication has a 30-second request budget and initialization/load a 60-second boundary. No overall turn deadline is imposed unless configured.

An initial TLS 2.4.3 build failed at native linking with `ld: library not found for -lz`: the installed Haskell zlib package configuration lacked a native library search path. A compiler probe and the successful build establish the local correction, without disabling TLS or certificate validation: `NIX_LDFLAGS_arm64_apple_darwin=-L/nix/store/008518i9kpvzsvs6d9rgcwgicjp5l4zb-zlib-1.3.2/lib`. The Nix shell now declares pkg-config, zlib and OpenSSL; execution inside that updated shell remains unverified. This is a toolchain-specific path, not an SDK runtime requirement.

Read-only review identified two defects that were fixed before milestone validation: final worker admission now rechecks duplicate IDs atomically with insertion, and daemon handlers enforce permission association versus owned-only question scope. The unrelated-interaction regression failed before its fix and passes afterward. The concurrent admission case checks one start/finalizer under competing restored/live requests, but did not deterministically reproduce the previous interleaving. Follow-up review found no remaining issues; the reviewer inspected source and logs rather than executing the tests.

Milestone evidence is `/tmp/droid-daemon-milestone.nd8zgR`: `build.log` records the library/example/test build with `-Werror`; `tests.log` records all 3,051 tests passing, including the existing local lifecycle/replacement/settings regressions and native daemon/WebSocket cases. `format.log`, `lint.log` and `audits.log` record clean Ormolu, HLint, reference audits and actionlint. `DaemonExample.hs` matches the README block exactly and compiles with `-fno-code -Wall -Werror` (`example.log`); it was not executed. `cabal-check.log` exits zero with the existing no-repository advisory. `review-initial.md`, `review-final.md`, `interaction-before.log` and `focused-after.log` retain the review/fix trail.

These results establish Apple Silicon macOS/GHC 9.10.3 offline behavior. No new live Factory/daemon/model request, stress batch, attachment fault-probe repetition, Haddock/source-distribution cycle or broader platform execution was performed for this checkpoint. Daemon controls beyond the session/turn core, external/hosted MCP, REST resources, advanced transports, remaining configuration/discovery, attribution/observability and final live/platform/distribution conformance remain open.

## External MCP management increment

Local and daemon session APIs now expose eleven existing MCP report, remove/toggle and authentication operations. The local functions reuse `Client` bindings; daemon functions use explicit method-indexed `daemon.*` calls and overwrite any extension-supplied session ID. `sessionRequestWithTimeout` generalizes the existing leased request path without changing its thirty-second default or uncertain-mutation invalidation. Authentication selects 300,000,000 microseconds. No new auth lock, worker, state cache or automatic OAuth cancellation was introduced.

Three `DroidEvent` constructors adapt the existing status/auth codecs. They retain ordinary metadata classification and session routing; malformed known events remain errors. RPC acknowledgement and authentication completion are separate observations. A completion notification has a server name but no state/request ID, so the SDK does not manufacture per-attempt correlation. The [README](../README.md#external-mcp-management) records the public API and ownership/privacy boundaries.

A required privacy regression exposed `SuccessResult`'s derived `Show` printing arbitrary peer extensions, including potentially sensitive auth data. The explicit redacted instance fixes display only; Boolean, extension and JSON behavior remain unchanged. `/tmp/droid-external-mcp.PxXH8D/before.log` records the failing test. Native JSONL and WebSocket fixtures share `test/McpPeer.hs` and cover all newly exposed operations, state/code/error submission, false flags, malformed/foreign events, remote rejection, escaped handles, concurrent explicit cancellation and preserved `AsyncCancelled` identity.

Evidence: `/tmp/droid-external-mcp.PxXH8D/static-build.log` and `static-tests.log` record warning-free focused compilation and fifteen passing new checks. `session-regressions.log` records all 208 local/daemon session cases passing. The library builds with `-Werror`; HLint and Ormolu pass. Follow-up read-only review found no issues in this increment and inspected source/logs without executing checks. This is not a fresh full-suite, platform, release-package or live verification result.

Some dynamic-driver starts stalled before Haskell entry: `query-sample.txt` records `dyld` loading/signature work. A separate dynamic run timed out in one daemon query test; that failure is retained in `after-recompile.log`, not inferred away from the sample. Recompiling the focused driver with ordinary static Haskell linking, as used by Cabal tests, passed the unchanged test deadlines. No security check, SDK guard or runtime limit was disabled.

That increment left configuration, add-server, reload policy and global daemon configuration unfinished. They are addressed by the following delivery; the earlier evidence and hashes remain historical.

## External MCP configuration delivery

`Schema.MCP.Config` now represents startup stdio/HTTP/SSE configurations, OAuth options/opt-out, add-server parameters and stored/global-update configurations. Startup headers remain ordered arrays; add/global headers remain maps, with last exact-name value winning in explicit conversion. Omitted/empty lists and omitted/false OAuth remain distinct. Configuration normalization occurs before launch/connection or mutation: scope/client-ID text uses the existing ECMAScript whitespace predicate, secrets are checked without trimming their value, callback ports are bounded and credential/metadata/issuer constraints are enforced. URI fields use `network-uri` RFC 3986 syntax rather than claiming WHATWG equivalence. Generic add/global URL strings retain their separately declared wire contract.

`droidMcpOptions` and `daemonMcpOptions` feed initialization and initial loading. The existing connection retains normalized load policy, so local fork, compaction, rewind and rollback replay server/callback settings. The initialization-only readiness override is omitted on replacement loads and rejected when explicitly supplied for resume. Add-server uses the existing typed local client or scoped daemon method. `Daemon.withConnection` factors the existing authenticated channel/dispatcher ownership without initializing a session; global get/update use this connection and no implicit session ID. Read results permit OAuth `false`, while update configuration is typed with options only, matching the distinct daemon contract.

Review found that startup/load events previously drained before any public handle could subscribe. `DroidHandlers.onDroidMcpEvent` now installs connection-scoped observation before init/load and retains it through replacement/rollback. It recognizes MCP payloads, then reuses the backend decoder and existing dispatcher. Local notifications report their optional source ID across connection-owned sessions; daemon observations filter the attached ID. Per-handle observer admission is unchanged. Malformed events and channel failures remain typed; ordinary observer exceptions are isolated, and cancellation joins admitted callbacks. There is no additional reader, worker or replay cache.

Native fixtures emit MCP auth/status events before replies and cover normalization, empty/omitted configuration, header conversion, local replacement/rollback policy, sessionless global requests, remote failures, callback exceptions and scope cleanup. Disabling local observer installation made the prefix regression fail with an empty event list; it was restored. One mutation-check command timed out during linking; the retained compiled mutation then failed as expected, and the fixed library was rebuilt before broad validation. Follow-up read-only review found no remaining issue in the observer fix and executed no tests.

Selected CLI source establishes important limits on configuration claims. `yci` at byte 218915657 constructs inline entries in input order, then overlays user/filesystem entries, which win same-name conflicts. Initialization/load only call `setMergedMcpConfigs` for a nonempty supplied list and then stop watching (`218949036`, `218951745`); an empty list therefore does not disable all servers. The initialization override is reset on load (`218950410`). `awaitMcpReadinessBeforeAgentTurnIfEnabled` at `218965372` implements a bounded pre-turn gate and may proceed after startup failure or readiness timeout; its flag is not proof that all tools became available. The Haskell layer does not write configuration files or promise persistence by the external engine. Static source observations are not live interoperability evidence.

Evidence: `/tmp/droid-mcp-config.b7UVda` contains `milestone-build.log` (library, test suite and example with `-Werror`), `milestone-tests.log` (all 3,092 tests passing), `observer-tests.log` (41 focused MCP checks), `observer-mutant.log`, `config-initial-review.md`, `config-final-review.md`, `runtime-locations.txt` and `runtime-behavior.txt`. Ormolu passes (`format-2.diff` is empty); HLint passes with one RTS capability (`lint-final-one-capability.log`). The README MCP example matches its source and compiles without execution; Cabal check retains the no-repository advisory (`example-and-package.log`). Reference audits and actionlint pass (`audits.log`). Some earlier linter/linker invocations exhausted tool deadlines; no check or security policy was disabled. Results remain Apple Silicon macOS/GHC 9.10.3 only. No new live Factory/auth/model call or release-package cycle is claimed.

## Hosted MCP native core

`MCP.Tool` and `MCP.Server` now execute Haskell handlers over authenticated loopback HTTP. Raw handlers receive only argument objects; typed decoding occurs once, and structured output has separate schema validation. Content validation covers text, image, audio, resource links and embedded resources, including Base64 acceptance and declared metadata. Handler/argument failures produce sanitized tool errors; malformed result envelopes remain protocol failures. Unlike the TypeScript wrapper's schema-less path, no HTTP request context or authorization header is supplied to handlers.

The server uses WAI/Warp for HTTP and existing `async` ownership facilities for listener/request workers. Manual starts pin ownership; scoped leases share the runtime. Cleanup records a shared Closing completion, runs outside the state lock and joins admitted workers; concurrent starts wait for it, and finalizers may inspect configuration. Atomic masked publication closes the reviewed manual-start cancellation gap. `droidHostedMcpServers` and loopback-only `daemonHostedMcpServers` feed the existing configuration path before initialization. Native peers consume the advertised endpoint and invoke the Haskell echo tool during startup/loading, including local fork/rollback and daemon resume. Borrowed manual ownership is retained.

Boundary checks cover Host, optional Origin, bearer authentication, accepted media types, JSON content type, body size, response size and flat batch length. Oversized IDs fail before dispatch; size-error fallback uses bounded null-ID output. Valid notifications/client responses receive 202; malformed envelopes are rejected. The stateless profile provides JSON replies and no cross-POST cancellation promise. Handler deadlines and scope closure cancel owned work; disconnect is not rollback of side effects.

Read-only review identified publication, batch/response-bound, unchecked-reference and envelope defects. Corrections preserve the existing ownership model. Follow-up exposed the dependency's incorrect `~01` pointer decoding and incomplete successful-response validation; exact regressions failed before those corrections (`review-before-2-tests.log`). The affected escape is now rejected as unsupported and successful responses require object results with valid optional metadata. Subsequent review found no remaining defect in those corrections; reviewers inspected source and logs without executing tests.

The accepted JSON Schema profile is deliberately narrower than unrestricted 2020-12. The native `jsonschema` dependency has POSIX rather than ECMA pattern semantics, approximate unevaluated-keyword handling and local-reference limits. Unsupported constructs and ambiguous escapes are rejected rather than advertised as validated. `$ref` siblings are normalized to conjunction while the original advertised schema is retained; targets outside checked schema locations are rejected. Full reference-supported schema validation remains open under `hsdk-zpf`, so the hosted milestone is not complete.

Evidence resides in `/tmp/droid-hosted-mcp.w7Hdht`. `broad-build.log` records warning-free library/example/test compilation and `broad-tests.log` records all 3,110 tests passing. Focused hosted/daemon lifecycle checks passed before the final two regressions (`lifecycle-tests.log`); `review-before-2-tests.log` retains their failing-before evidence. HLint and Ormolu pass after cleanup. Pre-fix linking attempts that timed out are retained and are not regression evidence. This remains macOS ARM64/GHC 9.10.3 offline verification; no Factory/model/auth request, publication or broader platform execution occurred.

References inspected: Factory TypeScript 0.7.0 wrapper `node.mjs:3261–3481`, verified published MCP SDK 1.29.0 sources under `/tmp/droid-mcp-sdk-1.29.0.g_xdzsrs`, and Python `mcp.py`/`test_v5_mcp.py`. The wrapper declares a dependency range, so inspected 1.29.0 behavior is not a claim about every satisfying version. Resolved native dependencies include WAI 3.2.5, Warp 3.4.15, jsonschema 0.3.0.1 and http-media 0.8.1.1. The jsonschema base bound excludes the proposed GHC 9.14 line; the broader compiler matrix remains an unresolved sign-off requirement.

### Resumed hosted output-schema composition

`withToolOutputSchema` exposes the existing output-schema field to raw and typed handlers returning `McpToolResult`; `structuredTool` now uses the same combinator. No second validator or handler path was introduced. Native HTTP checks verify schema advertisement, preservation of rich content/metadata and explicit tool errors, and rejection of invalid or absent structured success. The feature test initially failed to compile because the API was absent; an identity mutant subsequently failed on missing advertised schema. After restoration, all eighteen hosted checks and all 3,111 tests passed with `-Werror`; library/examples build, focused Ormolu and HLint also pass. No package, platform or live verification cycle was repeated for this increment.

Python's baselined `extensions.py:169–188` permits `DroidTool.output_schema` independently of its typed-callable helper. An offline invocation of the actual Factory-created server, with socket connections disabled, advertised an integer/minimum-one schema and nevertheless returned `{"total":0}` as a successful prebuilt result. Inspected Python MCP 1.29.0 `server/lowlevel/server.py:548–549` returns `CallToolResult` before its output-validation block. This bypass is not copied: Haskell checks successful structured output and retains the explicit-error exception used by TypeScript MCP 1.29.0 `server/mcp.js:185–205`. The Python SDK checkout was unchanged; the reference check made no Factory or network request.

Evidence for this increment is under `/var/folders/1q/mmxrlqw15hvd4gvfc86kjwhh0000gn/T/droid-hosted-resume.97zto82w`: `output-schema-before.log`, `output-schema-mutant.log`, `output-schema-after.log`, `final-checks.log` and `python-output-contract.py`/`.log`. The permanent native regression is in `test/HostedMcpSpec.hs`. An initial test-filter command failed to parse `-p=/SDK-hosted/`; corrected `--pattern Hosted` selected seventeen baseline cases, not an empty success.

### Validator replacement assessment

The `jsonschema-0.3.0.1` license conflict remains unresolved; no clarification or replacement was established by current public-source inspection. A research child produced a conditional shortlist but its run failed on unavailable provider tools; its report is not an independent audit or executed conformance result. No source SDK baseline, dependency or validation guard was changed.

A parent-compiled C++14 probe inspected [jsoncons v1.9.0](https://github.com/danielaparker/jsoncons/releases/tag/v1.9.0), whose tagged LICENSE is Boost Software License 1.0. The [source archive](https://codeload.github.com/danielaparker/jsoncons/tar.gz/refs/tags/v1.9.0) has SHA-256 `f1017b36e4e034acd5c0f5f616bacf5d7a161d6d3a43ff9ddb73fd8dca4d3cd9`. Its `~01` and evaluated-property cases passed, but both normal and `lossless_number(true)` modes accepted `0.1` against `const: 0.100000000000000000001`; `(?<=a)b` threw a regex error; and `b$` rejected the valid string `a\u0000b`. Each case used an object with property `v` and an applicable number/string schema. These are specific failed acceptance probes, not a full JSON Schema suite or an approved native bridge.

The probe source and receipts are `jsoncons-probe.cpp`, `jsoncons-build.log`, `jsoncons-default.log` and `jsoncons-lossless.log` in the increment's evidence directory. It built with `c++ -std=c++14 -O0 -Wall -Wextra -Werror`; both executions exited 1. jsoncons was not added to the SDK.

The Rust assessment then pinned `jsonschema` 0.55.1, whose crate metadata and included LICENSE both state MIT. Its [crate archive](https://static.crates.io/crates/jsonschema/jsonschema-0.55.1.crate) has SHA-256 `b68339c3d874e48151d74ffe256d93a58cffa240983cb0967d3cbaea083a44fe`. The earlier indexed 0.49.9 candidate was superseded after inspecting published validation fixes. The prototype disabled default HTTP/file retrieval and enabled arbitrary precision. Default `serde_json` 1.0.151 decoding retyped `{"$serde_json::private::Number":"1"}` as a number, admitting an invalid object argument. Explicit structural conversion through its standard `RawValue` API preserved object kinds, including both private marker names; twelve valid/invalid fixture pairs then passed, covering the earlier precision, reference and regex failures. HTTP/file references were refused without retrieval. This was prototype validation, not a production parser or full conformance suite.

Direct native FFI remained unsuitable for the existing cancellation contract. A valid 2,304-byte schema with thirty-five duplicated-reference `allOf` levels compiled in about 1 ms but ran beyond three seconds. A GHC 9.10.3 `safe` FFI call with `timeout 100000` likewise did not return within three seconds; the parent killed and reaped the probe process. The twenty-level control returned in about 20 ms. Native-entry markers distinguish this from a loader stall. A cancellable evaluation boundary or cooperative validator changes would be needed; no Rust dependency, parser, FFI or additional runtime was added to the SDK. Permission to contact the Haskell validator's maintainers was requested but not supplied, and no outreach occurred. `hsdk-zpf` and `hsdk-4po` remain open while independent resource work proceeds.

Rust receipts are `rust-probe.log` (failed decoder case), `rust-rawvalue-probe.log`, the prototype's `Cargo.lock`, `rust-stress-20.log`/`rust-stress-35.log`, `RustCancellation.hs` and `rust-ghc-cancellation-20.log`/`rust-ghc-cancellation-35.log`, all under the increment's evidence directory. The initial all-platform offline Cargo metadata query required an absent `js-sys` archive; filtering to the native platform succeeded without that download. No replacement licensing, platform or cancellation sign-off is claimed.

## Native Factory REST delivery

`Factory.Droid.REST` implements the thirteen helpers in baselined TypeScript 0.7.0 `chunk-5UXINOXG.mjs:23904–24018`: computer list/get/by-name/create/update/delete/restart/refresh, metrics, dependency-install retry, template list/get and remote-session listing. `Schema.REST` provides their parameter/result contracts. References are the corresponding runtime schemas at `23766–23842` and public declarations near `108093–108399`. Required/optional/null distinctions, false/empty arguments, pagination, computer passthrough fields and other records' extension stripping are preserved. Numeric timestamps/counters use `Integer`; metrics and query limits preserve finite `Scientific` values rather than narrowing to machine floats.

The client uses `http-client` 0.7.19 and `http-client-tls` 0.4.0, not the session/RPC runtime or a custom HTTP stack. Credentials and endpoints are explicit. Secure defaults retain certificate verification; plaintext requires an HTTP base URL. Base prefixes are preserved and identifiers/query values are encoded separately. Automatic redirects, proxies and retries are disabled deliberately: endpoint changes cannot forward credentials, and possibly completed mutations are not replayed. A standard reusable manager owns its pool; `withResponse` closes each active response. The dependency's deprecated `closeManager` is a no-op, so no fictitious deterministic pool-close API is exposed.

A whole-exchange deadline covers connection, headers and body; response consumption is bounded after decompression. `204` permits void operations but fails typed-result decoding. Non-JSON errors remain distinct from valid-JSON API/authentication failures, matching reference parse order. Raw API error strings are accessible explicitly, but `Show` and transport/format failures do not expose headers or body diagnostics. The TLS backend's existing exception wrapper covers the entire exchange, including body reads, while asynchronous exceptions retain their identity.

Read-only review found two concrete bugs: unbounded URI ports could be narrowed by the TLS backend (`65979` to `443`), and body-read socket/TLS exceptions escaped the REST error type. Native regressions failed before correction. Explicit port text now parses as `Integer` and must be in 1–65535 before manager creation; the backend exception wrapper now encloses body consumption as well as acquisition. The reset regression uses a short after-header interval and abortive socket close; its observed pre-fix failure is evidence, not a universal scheduler guarantee. The reviewer inspected source only; fixes were verified by parent-executed tests.

Fourteen native cases cover all helper routes, verbs, encoded path/query values, authenticated headers and bodies; sparse/empty values; JSON/status/shape errors and response limits; optional/null/extension behavior; error recovery; certificate rejection; no redirect/retry; timeout/cancellation and connection closure; and both review corrections. An initial cancellation fixture assumed closure meant EOF only; it now accepts TCP reset as well, propagates unrelated errors, and still asserts `AsyncCancelled` and closure. No runtime cancellation rule was loosened. The initial offline build lacked the HTTP TLS package's available build source; an ordinary dependency build succeeded. A private manager field was corrected to the public `managerSetProxy` helper rather than importing internals or disabling checks.

At this checkpoint, warning-free library/example/test builds and all 3,125 tests pass, including fourteen REST cases. Focused Ormolu/HLint, five reference-audit tests and actionlint pass. Logs in `/var/folders/1q/mmxrlqw15hvd4gvfc86kjwhh0000gn/T/droid-hosted-resume.97zto82w` include `rest-first.log`, `rest-build.log`, `rest-build-2.log`, `rest-build-3.log`, `rest-review-before.log`, `rest-review-after.log` and `rest-milestone.log`. This completes the REST helper slice, not advanced daemon resources, observability or overall parity. No Factory resource, live authentication or model operation was exercised.

The README REST example was extracted verbatim and compiled with `-fno-code -Wall -Werror`, not executed (`rest-example.log`). Haddock completed after ordinary public dependency source downloads; the initial offline attempt failed, and link warnings remain (`rest-haddock.log`, `rest-haddock-complete.log`). Cabal check retains `[no-repository]` (`rest-package-check.log`). A newly unpacked source distribution matched all 110 packaged files at packaging time, built with `-Werror`, passed all 3,125 tests and all six attachment FD/race markers (`rest-distribution.log`, `rest-distribution-sha256.txt`). These checks precede final verification-note edits; `rest-source-sha256.txt` identifies the checked implementation and package configuration. Platform execution remains macOS ARM64/GHC 9.10.3 only.

## Daemon session catalog delivery

`Schema.Daemon.Session`, `Client` and `Daemon` now expose opened/available catalogs, stored messages, cross-session search, archive/unarchive and archive-state observation. The six low-level bindings use the same `Client.call`; connection-scoped calls share `Core.connectionRequest` and its ID allocation, envelope, correlation and deadline. Existing sessionless MCP operations use the same small adapter. No new transport, session owner, reader, cache, load guard or automatic mutation retry was introduced.

Contracts were checked against TypeScript 0.7.0 `chunk-5UXINOXG.mjs:6910–7205,10620–10650,11008–11025`, supplied daemon schemas and the SHA256-verified CLI 0.212.1. Selected CLI schema code around bytes `204447950–204452000` establishes optional force-archive, organization metadata, `worktree.optional().catch(undefined)` and null-to-absence mission preprocessing. `SessionWorktreeMetadata` at byte `204378077` matches the existing shared codec. Newer archive-event metadata follows the supplied 1.205.0 shape. Optional additions do not imply automatic version negotiation or verified support from every older daemon.

Limit values use an opaque 1–100 `Scientific` type; default list/message limits are 50/20 and are sent explicitly. Listing cursors/timestamps preserve seconds, while search filters/results preserve milliseconds. Existing host, message, tag, worktree and mission-phase types are reused. Worktree fallback is field-local; mission null normalization does not relax required state or other catalog fields. Archive false-success and timestamp data are returned intact. Result displays redact payloads, while explicit fields and JSON remain accessible.

`onArchiveStateChanged` registers through the existing dispatcher and terminal-error listener, under masked registration with cleanup if the second registration fails. It preserves connection-wide event identity rather than filtering to an implicit loaded session. Queries from callbacks work through the independent correlation reader. Invalid payloads become `InvalidDaemonEvent`, channel failures remain typed, and existing callback-exception isolation, token-safe unsubscribe and scoped joining are retained.

Thirteen cases verify exact authenticated requests and result values; default/bound/filter/role/schema literals; null/fallback/extension semantics; query-error recovery; connection-wide events and unsubscribe barriers; reversed concurrent replies; malformed events; asynchronous cancellation; callback finalization and terminal failure. The initial negative fixture used undeclared Factory error code `-32000`; the channel correctly rejected it. The fixture now uses typed `RpcInvalidParams`, without weakening the decoder. Read-only source/diff review found no issues; the reviewer did not execute tests or establish no-history/sentinel independence. The detached workflow wrapper could not persist its continuation, but the child review completed.

Fresh `-Werror` library/example/test builds and all 3,138 tests pass, with focused Ormolu/HLint and five reference-audit checks. The preserved `catalog-runtime-contract.txt` binds static excerpts to the selected CLI digest; `catalog-first.log`, `catalog-focused.log`, `catalog-milestone.log`, `catalog-review.diff` and `catalog-review-status.txt` are under `/var/folders/1q/mmxrlqw15hvd4gvfc86kjwhh0000gn/T/droid-hosted-resume.97zto82w`. README catalog code is compiled, not executed. No live call, publication or repeated Haddock/source-distribution cycle is claimed for this API-only increment. Catalog operations do not finish queued-message workflows, state managers, workspace/terminal/worktree resources or the full daemon-resource milestone.

## Daemon workspace and file delivery

Nine typed low-level/connection-scoped bindings now cover folder trust check/grant, directory validation/change, file list/search/read and daemon-side URL push/pull. Existing `Schema.Daemon.Workspace` and `Schema.Control` codecs are reused without changing their omission-preserving decode behavior. `Client` materializes operational list/search defaults; high-level search supplies the controller's 50 before the low-level schema's 60 default. Explicit values win. The shared connection adapter now accepts a deadline, retaining thirty seconds for ordinary calls and using 900,000,000 microseconds for transfers. This is configured/source-checked; tests do not spend fifteen minutes waiting.

The contract comes from TypeScript 0.7.0 `chunk-5UXINOXG.mjs:10668–10710,10849–10866,11715–11769,17062–17119`, its workspace/transfer schemas at `7668–7741`, and the fifteen-minute expiry constant at `8118`. Upstream workspace-named transfer methods alias the same session-scoped wire operation, so no separate Haskell runtime or duplicate alias API is added. The newer supplied write-file codec remains intact, but a write-file operation absent from the baselined public SDK was not added merely for schema coverage.

No local path resolution, trust mutation, cwd change, file transfer or state-manager update is performed by these client functions. Remote containment, access checks, content metadata and transfer effects remain the daemon's responsibility. Permission/trust checks are not conflated. Existing session leases, scoped request construction and `SuccessOrErrorResult` are reused; list/update wrappers are parameterized only where their wire keys are identical. There is no installer, Git client, policy cache or retry mechanism. Source variants use opaque validated ref/SHA scalars and remain distinct from reported sources; reported local sources cannot carry a path. Source objects strip undeclared fields as the baselined SDK does, while surrounding open records preserve extensions.

References: TS 0.7.0 `chunk-5UXINOXG.mjs:6004–6221,11072–11161,17203–17242` and supplied daemon schemas. The SDK trims refs and accepts case-insensitive SHA pins; the supplied schema retained the pattern but lost its flag. Haskell follows the runtime and preserves case. An offline JavaScript probe confirmed BOM/line-space trimming, NEL preservation and rejection of a trailing newline after a SHA. Installed active/managed/reason and marketplace provenance/removable values stay optional data; absence does not authorize removal. Paths/scopes/timestamps are not strengthened into unrequested filesystem/date rules.

Eight grouped cases cover all source variants and boundaries, private-path projection, metadata unknown/false distinctions, input/result records and mixed batches, all ten high/low request paths, errors, deadlines and read-versus-install cancellation. Fixtures verify the SDK protocol boundary, not repository existence, plugin validity or real installation. The first build passed within thirteen minutes; subsequent tests caught a shadowed test variable and formatting, corrected without suppression. All 3,224 tests, warning-free builds, Ormolu/HLint and five reference audits pass. Receipts: `plugins-first.log`, `plugins-focused.log`, `plugins-focused-2.log`, `plugins-final.log` under `/var/folders/1q/mmxrlqw15hvd4gvfc86kjwhh0000gn/T/droid-hosted-resume.97zto82w`. ## Diagnostic submission and runtime limits

`hsdk-diagnostic-text-limits-81n` is resolved by separating wire representation from submission validation. Existing `BugReportSource`/`BoundedText` codecs retain the supplied schema's code-point bounds and all prior tests. New `validateBugReportSource` applies the baselined UTF-16 bounds, using the standard text encoder and reflected type-level limits. It returns the original source or `BugReportSourceTooLong field limit`, never the supplied value. Both named local and daemon report bindings invoke one shared preflight; generic `Client.call` remains a caller-controlled primitive. The new daemon wrapper uses existing connection ownership and the ordinary thirty-second budget, with no automatic diagnostics collection, attribution, local file reads, retry or session loading.

Evidence: published TS 0.7.0 `chunk-5UXINOXG.mjs:1992–2000,5784–5796,11164–11171,17249–17257`; a static read of the pinned CLI 0.212.1 near byte 204339270 confirms the same bounds. Python's baseline request exposes only comment/logs, not source metadata (`schemas/client.py:1279–1288`). A local probe evaluates the extracted published source schema with Zod 3.24.0: all 45 ASCII/BMP/non-BMP/mixed/combining boundary vectors and five null rejections agree with the contract. This reference probe is not a runtime dependency or a live CLI/daemon call.

The first native regression ran within seven minutes and failed: a schema-valid source containing 51 emoji was dispatched despite the runtime's 100-unit bound. It now raises the typed source error before any report frame. Eight grouped tests cover that regression; all five Unicode limits without codec mutation; both low-level preflights; exact daemon optional/raw/source payloads; remote/malformed results and reuse; cancellation identity/no retry; channel loss; and caller envelopes/deadlines. Native report receipts are synthetic and prove neither upload nor persistence. One compiler error identified the Integer-valued `GHC.TypeLits.natVal`; using the inspected Natural-valued `GHC.TypeNats.natVal` fixed it. Formatting and an HLint composition hint were corrected without suppression.

All 3,276 tests, warning-free build, touched-file Ormolu/HLint, five audit regressions, reference integrity checks and the compiled-only README example pass. Receipts: `diagnostic-source-probe.log`, `diagnostic-before.log`, `diagnostic-after.log`, `diagnostic-after-2.log`, `diagnostic-focused.log`, `diagnostic-final.log`, `diagnostic-example.log` under `/var/folders/1q/mmxrlqw15hvd4gvfc86kjwhh0000gn/T/droid-hosted-resume.97zto82w`. ## Mission snapshots and immutable state delivery

`hsdk-tok` adds `MissionSnapshot` and `LoadedSessionState.loadedMissionSnapshot`, validating full mission reports before resumed-handle publication. Raw receipts retain directory/update metadata, separate aggregate/per-session usage, ordered rosters and extensions. `Factory.Droid.Mission` implements the store transformations as immutable values: caller-owned storage and explicit observation times replace mutable caches/hidden clocks. Existing session observers provide event delivery; no new callback registry, background task or automatic cross-session association is introduced.

The model retains field presence independently of rendered defaults, so pristine merges cannot erase destination state, while explicit default phase/null title/empty lists can. Worker/history and per-session usage maps merge with source precedence. Progress replacement retains historical workers/start times and missing failure exit codes; explicit completion replaces a worker report instead of patching it. Roster ordering matches reference insertion behavior, including numeric object-key ordering for newly derived workers. Typed worker variants drive derivation, and native maps do not copy JavaScript prototype-property behavior. Totals use exact `Scientific` arithmetic and missing credits contribute zero. Restoration follows the reference load sequence rather than assuming reported aggregate usage equals the per-session sum.

## Owned mission observation and getters

`hsdk-wv2` adds `getDroidMissionSnapshot` and `Daemon.getMissionSnapshot`, returning the connection-owned immutable model's current snapshot without RPC or waiting. Full initialize/load replies seed the model on ordered intake; selected mission mutations apply before existing callbacks. The getter checks lifecycle and owner ID atomically, distinguishing no observed mission from invalid/unavailable state. Routine usage or heartbeat events do not create a model. A malformed mission mutation invalidates the mission view until a full successful baseline; settings remain independent.

The existing reply-observer API gains `requestReplyObservedAt`: local UTC time is captured after envelope decoding and before acceptance, then supplied to the already-queued STM observer. No clock IO occurs inside STM, no new reader/queue/worker is introduced, and dequeue/observation atomicity remains unchanged. Worker fallback times are local observation samples, not actual worker-start evidence. The `time` dependency was already in the library and is now declared directly for the tests.

Source inspection of TS 0.7.0 `chunk-5UXINOXG.mjs:14882–14900` found a prior contract mistake: mission event application used base usage, but the reference prefers inclusive usage for the owner or a known worker. The old assertion encoded that wrong assumption. A corrected regression failed with 3 instead of 999, then passed after fixing the shared model rather than compensating in the observer. Explicit inclusive zero and unrelated-worker exclusion are verified.

Five new grouped cases plus expanded local tests cover captured reply time despite delayed consumption/duplicates, routine-session absence and scope closure, full-load/update ordering and fallback timestamps, callback getter coherence and scoped usage, malformed-view isolation/fresh-baseline recovery, and local callback/replacement retirement behavior.
