## Independent daemon session attachments

`hsdk-daemon-resources-observability-mxt.4.1` adds `withResumedSessionOn`, its handler variant, `detachSession` and `closeAttachedSession`. Multiple saved sessions borrow one authenticated connection without another socket, reader or dispatcher. The original owned creation/resume path now uses the same attachment registration and cleanup. A private binding retains the original credential context, core handle, handlers and unique lease token; it does not expose credentials through public receipts.

The shared core now indexes observed settings by session and gives each handle its own turn lock, closure signal and cleanup registrations. Local replacement continues to drain and retire its source correctly. Temporary lifetime cancellation uses the existing async facilities for waits; stream consumption checks closure without moving user turn callbacks off the caller's thread. Explicit detach retires local admission, releases observers and joins admitted local operations without remote interruption. Ordinary explicit cancellation retains the prior uncertainty/interruption rules; a cancelled partial transport write can still fail the physical channel.

One dispatcher router prefers the execution session, then permission associations in supplied order. Questions remain exact-session only, and detached/unregistered requests cancel. Scope closure joins its blocked interaction callback without cancelling another attachment. Session lifecycle notices retire only the named binding. Unique binding ownership prevents a stale detach/finalizer from deleting a newer lease for the same ID. The reference rejects duplicate active handles, and the native API does likewise before loading. Local detach remains distinct from explicit remote close: `closeAttachedSession` retains admission through its own RPC reply, then retires the handle under masking, with or without a lifecycle notice. The connection-level close operation retains its separate contract.

References: TS 0.7.0 `chunk-5UXINOXG.mjs:23333–23340,23389–23394,23590–23629,23635–23730`, existing daemon load/control schemas, and the established native lifecycle/dispatcher contracts. Both an early lifecycle notice and an acknowledgment without one are covered. Failed API compilation, a mistaken fixture getter and its unused import were corrected without weakening checks. Review also corrected an initial callback-thread migration and removed a synthetic cleanup binding before sign-off.

## Daemon load coordination

`hsdk-daemon-resources-observability-mxt.4.2` adds explicit `loadSessionInfo`, coalesced `ensureSessionLoaded`, readonly readiness snapshots/STM change waits, and not-loaded/not-found/pre-init controls. The existing daemon context owns per-session generations and shared completion tickets, not another transport, callback registry or background load pool. Explicit reloads start new generations; readiness checks join a current flight or reuse loaded state, and unknown/pre-init-only identities do not trigger speculative RPCs. Attachment handles retain their separate uniqueness and lifetime rules.

`SessionReadiness` separates load phase, in-flight work, known/not-found/pre-init flags and peer working-state evidence. Busy is not a synonym for local handle admission or loadedness. Snapshot state is applied at the existing ordered reply position; later working-state notifications remain authoritative when the IO waiter finishes. Malformed working-state data is explicit rather than silently idle. Not-found preserves the original entity-not-found RPC error; successful explicit retry clears the marker. Pre-init changes metadata without pinning connection ownership. Lifecycle invalidation does not resurrect a detached handle.

A shared load owner retains its original asynchronous cancellation. Cancelled readiness waiters leave it running; other waiters see a typed `DaemonLoadInterrupted` if the owner is cancelled. Superseded or explicitly invalidated generations cannot publish settings/mission state. The same generation predicate is checked atomically during restored-request admission in the existing dispatcher; already admitted workers retain their normal ownership, rather than being retroactively cancelled. Full-receipt parsing and pending-request container/ID validation now precede ordered publication and share the parser used for restoration.

References: TS 0.7.0 `chunk-5UXINOXG.mjs:16094–16266,16470–16485,21888–21975,22708–22755`, the existing load schemas and native dispatcher contracts. Eleven load tests plus a dispatcher admission case cover unknown/pre-init state, coalesced/cancelled waits, stale explicit reloads, invalidation/retry, not-found, invalid full/pending receipts, busy fallback, post-reply working-state precedence, owner expiry and revoked restoration. Native waiter tests observe an actual blocked STM wait instead of relying on a scheduling delay. A failure-before regression returned settings `"invalid"` instead of `"bad-pending"` after rejecting malformed restored requests; moving shared validation before publication fixes it.

Final evidence: warning-free all-component build, all 3,373 tests (11.46s execution; 14.710s Cabal command), Ormolu/HLint, five audit regressions/reference integrity and the compiled matching README example. Receipts are `/tmp/droid-load-coordination.guOKUt/{observer-build,first-build,first-focused,validation-before,focused-after,admission-focused,build-final,suite-final,format-final,lint-final,audit-tests,reference-integrity,example}.log`. The STM admission predicate is total/nonblocking by contract. This is self-review only, not independent/live/platform/distribution certification. ## Optimistic submission delivery

`hsdk-daemon-resources-observability-mxt.4.3` adds `Factory.Droid.SessionState`, an immutable message/optimistic-submission model, and integrates it into the existing daemon context, ordered load observation, notification dispatcher and backend submission function. `submitUserMessage` uses `Core.connectionRequest` and `Client.call` with an explicit request ID; ordinary daemon turns use the same `submitMessage` implementation. There is no second transport, reader, dispatcher, callback registry or persistent timer worker.

The native public operations are `registerOptimisticSubmission`, `submitUserMessage`, `getSessionState`, `waitSessionStateChange`, `confirmOptimisticSubmission`, `cancelOptimisticSubmission` and `cancelSessionOptimisticSubmissions`. Pure model operations cover preparation, begin/finish/failure, explicit confirmation/cancellation, created-message observation, load merging and expiry. Confirmation/cancellation helpers modify local state only; actual matching notifications settle ACK-aware commands. In-flight ownership survives local overlay removal until the originating RPC finishes. A current attachment's detach clears its overlays without clearing another session or a successor lease.

Registration is idempotent for the same request/placeholder: input is retained and the display-error timer refreshed. The default is twenty seconds, separate from the thirty-second RPC/confirmation budget. Native observation uses monotonic microseconds; polling evaluates expiry and a caller-owned wait wakes at the next deadline. Expiry retains a typed failed overlay without cancelling the request. Known protocol failures retain errors; caller cancellation and other local exceptions remove overlays and are rethrown unchanged. A later valid echo remains authoritative. The model retains exact explicit input fields and generated persisted-message identity; session IDs and declared message fields are reserved against extension override. Redacted display does not sanitize explicit data.

References: published TS 0.7.0 `chunk-5UXINOXG.mjs:15493,16576–16656,21716,22014–22155,22560–22648`, plus the existing `AddUserMessageParams`, `CreateMessage` and loaded-message codecs. The native implementation deliberately uses fresh explicit message IDs for load recovery rather than the reference's text heuristic: an extracted-source probe demonstrates that unrelated newly loaded text can confirm the baselined overlay. This avoids false confirmation of repeated submissions. A missing or reassigned persisted ID is not guessed from text. The eight extracted-method checks use a fixture message sink and deterministic fixture timer; they do not execute the complete reference SDK, validate queue coordination, or replace native timer/transport checks.

### Regression coverage

The eighteen cases in `test/SubmissionSpec.hs` verify the following behavior, rather than renderer implementation details:

| Case | Observable assertion |
| --- | --- |
| Preparation | Re-registration keeps original input and renews pending status/deadline, including after expiry; negative timeouts fail. |
| In-flight ownership | Removing or failing an overlay does not permit a duplicate send or placeholder replacement until the RPC finishes. |
| Timeout and late confirmation | Expiry retains an error, a valid echo installs the real message, and later rejection cannot recreate the overlay. |
| Pure load recovery | Only a newly observed explicit ID confirms; equal text and previously observed IDs do not. |
| Echo before ACK | State confirms while the RPC reply is still withheld, and the later ACK does not recreate the overlay. |
| Correlation isolation | Foreign-session and foreign-request messages are processed without confirming the target request. |
| Legacy reply | A successful legacy object remains unconfirmed until observable display expiry; cancellation is idempotent. |
| Rejection and malformed echo | Remote rejection and malformed matching confirmation retain distinct errors without publishing a real message. |
| Cancelled sender | Local removal prevents duplicate transmission, preserves `AsyncCancelled`, and retains a later authoritative message. |
| Explicit local controls | Explicit confirmation records identity, unknown confirmation is a no-op, bulk cancellation is session-scoped, and no remote mutation is sent. |
| Custom cancellation | A custom exception delivered by `cancelWith` is preserved and removes the overlay. |
| Caller timeout | A caller-owned timeout ends waiting, clears the overlay and sends no replay. |
| Display/RPC independence | An expired overlay remains in flight, then confirms when its delayed real echo arrives. |
| Disconnect | Read failure ends the pending command; state observation and subsequent access end with closed-connection errors. |
| Wire values | Empty text/arrays, false, a large exact number, extensions, explicit message ID and fixed session/envelope identity reach the native peer. |
| Ordinary turn integration | `sendPrompt` uses the same request-correlated overlay and preserves the final streamed result. |
| Ordered load integration | Validated load receipts remove an overlay only when the explicit fresh persisted ID appears. |
| Attachment retirement | An old detached lease cannot clear a replacement's overlays or those of another session. |

The last pre-final focused run passed seventeen cases in 0.42s; the final full suite includes the additional explicit-control case. Receipts are `/tmp/droid-submissions.Ln5Pnx/`: `review-regressions-{before,after}.log`, `{build,suite,audits,integrity}-final.log`, `checks-final.log`, `reference.log`, `format-clean.log`, `lint-clean.log` and `example-clean.log`. The source probe and example are `reference.mjs` and `SubmissionExample.hs` in that directory.

The executed shared-runtime commands were `cabal build all --offline --ghc-options=-Werror` and `cabal test all --offline --ghc-options=-Werror --test-show-details=direct --test-option=--hide-successes --test-option=-j1 --test-option=+RTS --test-option=-N2 --test-option=-RTS`. **Rubric and architectural fit.** The new model is an extension of existing ownership and message types; subsequent conversation/queue behavior can extend it without a parallel session engine. Test peers replace the external daemon only. No production stub, disabled check, swallowed caller exception, missing-dependency fallback, new dependency, invented remote response or unrelated source refactor remains in this unit. The broad `try` exists solely to finalize submission state and rethrow the original exception. The message-only model does not claim full conversation, queue or renderer behavior. `Schema.Control` adds only the minimal default-input constructor for this unit; older queue-codec changes in its working-tree diff predate this work.

**Verification gaps and next work.** This is self-review, not an independent review or a verified no-history/sentinel attestation. No live Factory/CLI/daemon call, Linux/alternate-GHC execution, current independent distribution, commit or publication was performed. Those existing release gates remain open. The session-state parent still requires message/progress state, queues, child hydration, terminal restoration, authoritative cwd and pending/deferred interactions; the next child is `.4.4`. No unresolved correctness finding from this unit is being deferred into that child.

## Conversation-state development

This section retains the staged implementation record for `hsdk-daemon-resources-observability-mxt.4.4`; see [closure](#session-message-state-closure) for its accepted final status. The first slice added retained message order, parent repair, authoritative upserts/load merges, recent/role selection, removal and nonnegative-count truncation to `SessionState`. Existing daemon create/load observation calls these functions; no separate reader or session runtime was added. Parent and ordering behavior follows the published store/manager helpers at `chunk-5UXINOXG.mjs:9293–9370,18222–18253,19002–19120,20778–20984`. A verified native distinction remains explicit: equal-time leaf IDs use portable UTF-16 order, whereas the source helper uses host-locale collation. The reference probe reports `[root,a,A]` for a locale-sensitive tie; that case is not counted as cross-language equality.

The authoritative-load change removes a timestamp comparison that becomes invalid when client-observed streaming timestamps and daemon timestamps differ. A temporary restoration of the old comparison made the regression return `local prefix` at time 200000 instead of `server correction` at time 1 (`test/SessionStateSpec.hs:33–39`). The mutant failed one selected test, was reverted with `undo_last_replace`, and the restored implementation passed again. Parent repair keeps the first duplicate parent, removes self-links and cuts cycles; a single rooted history follows the newest leaf's chain, while multiple roots retain stable timestamp order. Incremental insertion respects contiguous descendants, and updates preserve an absent parent from the prior view.

The initial history slice added nine cases in `test/SessionStateSpec.hs`: authoritative replacement despite clock skew; descendant insertion and retained parents; tail inheritance without payload changes; duplicate/self/cycle repair; newest-leaf order; stable multi-root order and bounded recent selection; role/removal/truncation with pending submissions retained; terminating cyclic traversal; and UTF-16 insertion ties. At that boundary, these plus all eighteen submission regressions passed with `-Werror` (**27 tests, 0.42s**). Four extracted-reference cases, Ormolu and HLint passed. Receipts are `/tmp/droid-message-state.d9ZShJ/{first-build,history-focused,timestamp-mutant,history-restored,history-reference,history-restored-format,history-restored-lint}.log`; `history-reference.mjs` is a local extracted-helper probe, not a complete SDK run.

### Retained streaming content

`SessionState.applyMessageEventAt` now consumes the existing `DroidEvent` vocabulary for text/thinking deltas and completions, full messages, tool calls/results and retractions. Daemon intake reuses `decodeDaemonNotification`, captures clocks outside STM, and applies the immutable transition before ordinary session callbacks. The per-turn `Internal.Stream` state remains responsible for append-only delivery history and final turn results; it is not reused as a mutable conversation cache.

Provisional messages use observed peer IDs and client wall-clock timestamps. Per-kind block addresses preserve text/thinking separation and arbitrary schema-valid numeric indices without allocating array gaps. Full message/load observations reset provisional addresses. Thinking duration uses monotonic elapsed time only when an explicit duration is absent; explicit zero and false capability metadata are preserved. Pending tool calls do not invent assistant message IDs, orphan results wait for the matching assistant, and loaded authoritative tool results take precedence over cached orphans. Role-conflicting updates retain last-known history and set an explicit failure; `checkedSessionMessages` reports that failure until a validated load. Raw selectors remain available for last-known data.

Ten additional message-state cases verify independent block accumulation/completion; thinking timing and zero/false preservation; authoritative address reset; negative/fractional/large indices; role-conflict invalidation and load repair; pending calls/orphan adoption and authoritative result loading; nonduplicating embedded-result retention; retraction/replacement; native callback ordering and cross-session malformed-state isolation; and native orphan adoption/retraction. A further daemon teardown regression verifies that explicit detach joins the permission handler and produces only its correctly scoped cancellation response. No test replaces the internal decoder, dispatcher or session runtime with a mock.

References: TS 0.7.0 `chunk-5UXINOXG.mjs:18454–18535,18625–18869,19002–19120,19239–19660`; existing content/message/notification codecs. `stream-reference.mjs` extracts five matching text/completion/result/adoption/role-protection cases into a fixture message sink. It does not execute complete SDK initialization, todo processing, notification scheduling or a service. Two source distinctions remain explicit: the TypeScript assistant-text handler ignores `blockIndex`, and its embedded-result merge can repeat an already supplied result (`[a,a,b]`). Native state keeps correlated blocks and only missing results (`[a,b]`); the differing source case is recorded rather than counted as a match.

### Todo and tool-progress state

The next `.4.4` slice adds normalized todo selection and progress/phase/retry observation to the same `SessionState`. `applySessionEventAt` composes message observation with these additional transitions. Daemon intake applies the shared typed events and updates inferred working state in `SessionReadiness`, not a second cache. `expireSessionStateAt` and `nextSessionDeadline` extend the existing caller-owned expiry waiter; no timer pool, scheduler or retry engine was added.

Live todo changes require a successful reported result for a tracked `TodoWrite` call. Sequence ownership is assigned at first observation; old late successes cannot replace a newer accepted list. Reload rebuilds from confirmed history and still-pending calls, preferring the latest successful list and otherwise an unfailed pending list. Empty/invalid updates do not erase a prior selection. Normalization follows the published text/checklist, JSON-string, string-array and structured-row forms, including ECMAScript whitespace, ASCII numbering and default priority. Mixed string arrays perform field-local JavaScript numeric display coercion; retained tool inputs and other numbers remain exact.

Progress retains updates by tool ID, suppresses consecutive timestamp-only duplicates and provides stable timestamp ordering. Lifecycle rank is monotone and the first terminal meaning wins. Tool results schedule cleanup after sixty seconds; a distinct later update cancels it, whereas a duplicate does not. Expiry removes progress but retains phase evidence. Reload clears transient progress/phase/retry state. Retry-clearing event kinds match the published controller, and idle/error transitions complete only still-streaming blocks, preserving already reported thinking durations.

References: TS 0.7.0 `chunk-5UXINOXG.mjs:5084–5116,18050–18218,18532–18590,19729–19769,21210–21238,21422–21473`. Eight extracted-source cases in `todo-progress-reference.mjs` verify normalization, JSON fallback, mixed-array joining, numeric display boundaries, loaded todo selection, monotone phases and settlement. Eleven new native cases cover these domains plus live result ordering, pending-call reload survival, empty-update retention, deduplication/deadline renewal, combined expiry, retry/completion preservation, and ordered daemon progress/readiness integration.

**Executed verification.** Warning-free build, all **3,422 tests in 11.01s** (14.745s Cabal command), clean Ormolu/HLint, five audit regressions/reference integrity, eight extracted-reference checks and compiled-only `ProgressExample.hs` passed. A separate native loopback/GHCi probe exercised the real sixty-second deadline: `TOOL_PROGRESS_EXPIRY_SECONDS=60.004025`, followed by `TOOL_PROGRESS_EXPIRY_PASS`. It verified that progress expired while terminal phase remained. Its delay is intentionally outside the default fast suite; the ordinary suite still includes deterministic expiry regressions.

The field-local formatter now selects the first round-tripping decimal precision, bounded by binary64's seventeen-digit guarantee, before applying JavaScript display thresholds. No Node runtime or numeric-rounding change was introduced elsewhere. A scratch `ghc -e` display probe initially lacked the `Text` display instance; projecting to `String` corrected the probe, not library behavior.

Self-review found no production stub, missing-dependency fallback, suppressed diagnostic or duplicated working-state owner. Text fallback after invalid todo JSON is the explicitly baselined parsing behavior, not swallowed transport failure. New tests assert normalized values, ordering, preserved raw inputs and downstream state, not merely calls to a mock. The code uses the existing Aeson, base, containers and STM facilities; no new dependency was added. This is self-review and local execution, not independent review, live Factory interoperability, another platform/compiler, a current independent source distribution, a commit or publication.

### Session-message-state closure

`hsdk-daemon-resources-observability-mxt.4.4` completes the required conversation/progress state slice through the existing native owners. The staged history above is retained as evidence, not a claim that completed portions remain pending. The broader session-state parent is not complete: queue coordination, child hydration, terminals, authoritative cwd and pending/deferred interactions remain separate required children.

| Required outcome | Native implementation and acceptance |
| --- | --- |
| Loaded/streamed history | `SessionState` retains authoritative messages, partial text/thinking/tool content, orphan adoption, retractions and checked observation failure; immutable load receipts remain separate. |
| Selection | Parent/order helpers, role/recent views, filtered typed display entries, thirty-entry progressive expansion, compaction cutoff/pruning and hydration-floor metadata; native setters modify only the connection-owned model. |
| Todos | Normalization, success/sequence ownership, pending-call retention and successful/pending history reconstruction. |
| Working state | Observed and event-derived transitions remain in `SessionReadiness`; no second working-state cache. |
| Progress/retries | Monotone phases, timestamp-insensitive consecutive deduplication, stable ordering, retry-clear rules and the existing shared expiry waiter. |
| Hooks | Typed starts and reports, unknown-completion no-op, stable replacement order, hidden-data projection, monotonic lease expiry and authoritative late completion. |

Hook leases retain their raw timeout metadata. Normal deadlines use the sixty-second minimum plus thirty-second grace; expired leases become `HookLeaseExpired`, not fabricated success. Leases beyond one native wait horizon remain unscheduled but are checked exactly when observed, without expanding huge numeric exponents or narrowing the original field. Display filtering keeps assistant quotations, strips complete system tags only from non-assistant text, preserves meaningful empty persisted hook rows and excludes hidden/model-only data. Compaction retains the latest todo-bearing row and its result context; clearing a cutoff does not restore pruned cache data. A nullable compaction boundary is preserved and does not clear an existing cutoff.

Thirteen final regressions cover hook metadata/replacement and unknown completion; lease boundaries and late reports; fractional/extreme deadlines; hook privacy; role-sensitive tag filtering; empty persisted hooks; progressive doubling; enable/disable and cutoff restoration rules; todo-preserving pruning/reload counts; hydration boundaries; typed and hidden pending entries; native callback/selection/compaction integration; and malformed native hooks repaired by reload. The shared decoder now exposes `SessionCompactedEvent` rather than treating the known payload as untyped. Its addition made the exhaustive tag helper in `test/DroidSpec.hs` fail compilation; the helper gained an explicit case, not a catch-all or warning suppression.

References: TS 0.7.0 `chunk-5UXINOXG.mjs:9373–9416,19976–20035,20209–20240,20413–20460,21477–21626`. Six extracted-reference checks cover hook replacement/lease thresholds, filtering, expansion and pruning. Source distinctions are explicit: native expired leases do not invent a completed report; hidden pending inputs and hidden completion reports remain excluded; typed overlays do not manufacture wire message IDs; pending display entries keep native registration order. Earlier documented block-index, duplicate-result and portable tie-break differences remain in force.

### Queue-state delivery

`hsdk-daemon-resources-observability-mxt.4.5` extends the existing `SessionState` with five queue kinds, ordered upsert/replacement, processed-identity guards, dequeue, front restoration, clear, attachment-aware pause and load reconciliation. `QueueEntry` composes the existing `QueuedUserMessage` body with kind/observation metadata, retaining complete input options instead of reconstructing a send from rendered content.

Native facade operations expose local queue transitions, explicit sending and ACK-aware remote resolution. A successful resolution updates the original queue identity after the existing session/new-RPC-ID correlation completes. Explicit sending accepts only local entries, fixes a persisted message ID before removal, and reuses normal submission. Failure/cancellation restores paused work only while its slot remains absent and unconfirmed; a newer queue observation wins. The shared backend pauses queue observations after successful interruption, and `QueuedMessagesDiscardedEvent` uses the same model. No extra reader, transport, scheduler or automatic dequeue worker is created.

The existing ordered reply observer now passes its already captured `UTCTime` to the load consumer (`Internal.Session.callSettingsResultObserved`), rather than introducing another clock boundary. Complete queue receipts are validated before publication, and the same load generation gates settings, mission, readiness, messages and queue state. Local entries survive daemon replacement; busy drained entries remain tracked; idle uncertainty becomes paused. Fresh explicit persisted-message IDs can retire confirmed work. Source text/time matching and automatic resubmission are deliberately not copied under the approved no-implicit-replay policy.

References: TS 0.7.0 `chunk-5UXINOXG.mjs:10560–10597,14006–14225,16635–16735,19800–20006,22104,22653–22676`; existing queue/input/load codecs and RPC ownership. Five extracted-reference checks cover ordered replacement, processed enqueue, front restoration, kind filtering and attachment-aware pause. Additional probes demonstrate three intentional differences, not matching cases: source restoration can reintroduce a processed ID, source loaded-text matching can infer delivery for a different ID, and source idle reload can resubmit automatically. Native guards retain identity and explicit-action requirements.

#### Regression coverage

Twenty new `QueueStateSpec` cases, alongside the five existing queue-RPC cases, verify:

| Case | Observable assertion |
| --- | --- |
| Upsert | Replacing an ID keeps position, exact input fields and no duplicate optimistic overlay. |
| Daemon replacement | Local work remains; repeated reported IDs and processed IDs are handled consistently. |
| Front restoration/dequeue | Input order and last duplicate value are retained, confirmed IDs stay retired, and empty kind selection differs from all kinds. |
| Pause | Discardable text is removed while image, document and path attachments and end-of-loop work remain recoverable. |
| Reconciliation | Busy missing entries remain remote; idle uncertainty pauses; text/clock similarity is insufficient delivery evidence. |
| Resolution model | Placement changes preserve input and deletion retires identity without manufacturing missing entries. |
| Local facade | Queue edits send only the initial authentication exchange, no implicit load or submission. |
| Native load | Validated queue receipts replace only their scope and invalid receipts do not publish or trigger replay. |
| Stale load | Superseded receipt generations cannot overwrite the newer queue. |
| Confirmation/resolution race | An original confirmation cannot be requeued by a later reprioritization reply. |
| Explicit send | Wire fields and separate persisted identity are preserved; early confirmation prevents restoration. |
| Rejection | Unconfirmed work returns paused, while an authoritative echo wins a later error. |
| Newer queue/failed send race | Cancellation does not overwrite a newer authoritative queue receipt. |
| Cancellation identity | The original exception and generated message ID survive; late echo clears the paused row and duplicate sends do not transmit. |
| Remote-entry guard | Already daemon-backed work must be resolved rather than resubmitted. |
| End-of-loop legacy reply | Explicit remote queue placement is tracked without fabricated confirmation. |
| Busy/prepared/skip distinctions | Busy legacy sends queue, while prior optimistic preparation and skip-loop settings retain their defined behavior. |
| Rejected resolution | Local queue state remains unchanged. |
| Discard notification | Attachment retention and restored-text removal are session-scoped. |
| Interruption | Successful turn interruption pauses the queue through the existing backend owner. |

**Finding fixed.** Review found and reproduced a real ownership defect: a failed/cancelled send restored its old paused payload over a newer queue receipt accepted while the send waited (`test/QueueStateSpec.hs:175`, `restore-race-before.log`). The shared sender now restores only when the slot is still absent; the existing processed-ID guard independently rejects restoration after confirmation (`Daemon.sendQueuedUserMessage`, `restore-race-after.log`). The regression and fresh full suite pass. No assertion was weakened to obtain success.

**Architectural fit and scope.** Queue transitions belong to the existing immutable model; RPC settlement stays in `Client.call`/`awaitDaemonCommand`; generation, timestamps and publication stay in the existing load observer. The added facade helper performs an atomic model transition, not a competing runtime. Raw inputs retain exact values, omission and extensions; queue timestamps are observations rather than peer creation claims. The failure catch exists to restore owned local state and rethrow the original exception. No production stub, dependency fallback, disabled check, swallowed application exception, new dependency or unrelated source refactor remains. Existing stage/history records and unrelated work are preserved.

**Verification limits and disposition.** The no-replay, exact-identity and processed-restoration safeguards are explicit native policy, not claims that the unsafe source behaviors were reproduced. This is self-review only, without independent/no-history attestation. No live Factory/CLI/daemon call, paid operation, other platform/compiler, current independent distribution, commit or publication was performed. The queue child is accepted offline; the session-state parent advances to **5/9 children complete**, while the capability dashboard remains **11/45**. ### Child-session hydration delivery

**Historical boundary.** The records below describe their original delivery and receipts. The current-target summary freshness, terminal-status/eligibility and working-state hydration rules were subsequently revised; see [CC-15](evidence/2026-09-17/cc15/README.md) and [terminal-summary policy](evidence/2026-09-17/summary-terminal/README.md). The earlier evidence and frozen-target behavior are not retroactively changed.

`hsdk-daemon-resources-observability-mxt.4.6` supplies parent/tool linkage, child discovery, optional automatic hydration, invocation summaries and failure observations through the existing daemon connection and immutable `SessionState`. `LoadedSessionState` adds declared optional `callingToolUseId` and `subagentInvocations` fields; omission remains distinct from invalid explicit null. Existing summary codecs retain exact numbers, empty text and extension precedence.

Discovery is published before typed `ChildSessionAvailableEvent` callbacks. Manual registration and summary hydration perform no load; automatic discovery hydration defaults on but is configurable. `ensureChildSessionAttached` joins the established load coordinator, returns false for unknown children and does not grant handlers or execute a child. Accepted loads use the existing epoch and ordered receipt boundary for linkage, messages, summaries, settings and mission state. The existing sixty-second load budget is reused, not restarted by a follower.

Automatic work is registered behind a local start gate; the connection seals its job map before cancellation and joins registered jobs before completing scope cleanup (`Daemon.hs:413–444,1104–1132`). These are owned load continuations, not a second reader, dispatcher, session runtime or retry loop. Failed child loads remain observable through `sessionChildLoadError`; explicit callers receive their original exception. A real close retires linkage, but its historical invocation summary remains available. A successful silent attached close invalidates the current lease's pending load before detach, without invalidating a newer binding.

References: TS 0.7.0 `chunk-5UXINOXG.mjs:3777–3835,9442–9470,15217–15278,16124,16495–16577,21784–21795,22278–22407`; the existing load, attachment, mission and notification owners. Eight extracted-source checks cover metrics, identity validation, lookup order/isolation and bulk summaries. The native implementation ignores a conflicting availability notice as a complete parent/tool pair, rather than allowing source per-field seeding to associate a foreign tool with an established parent. Reported load metadata can still replace provisional linkage. Parent-cwd seeding remains part of the separately approved authoritative-cwd child `.4.8`; reconnect orchestration remains under advanced transports.

### Terminal-restoration delivery

`hsdk-daemon-resources-observability-mxt.4.7` implements retained terminal metadata, active selection, serialized state, unmounted buffers and attachment-owned output writers through `SessionState` and the existing daemon runtime. Raw terminal RPCs remain distinct from local metadata changes. Terminal workers belong to the daemon; this unit does not start a PTY, parse ANSI or provide a renderer.

`loadTerminals` now observes a validated `daemon.list_terminals` reply at its ordered intake position and waits for publication under a thirty-second budget. Automatic restoration defaults on for session loads, including child hydration; new-session initialization does not query terminals. The complete session-load path remains within its sixty-second boundary. Terminal-restoration tickets reject superseded requests; the existing session-load guard additionally prevents an invalidated main load from publishing terminal state. Later local metadata changes or removal prevail over an older report.

The native model retains exact dimensions, status/exit data, empty buffers and false cursor visibility. `TerminalWireTimestamp` preserves the established RFC-3339 wire value; `TerminalEpochMilliseconds` carries explicit numeric time for locally supplied snapshots. The daemon facade retains the caller's timestamp, while the compiled `saveFrame` example supplies current milliseconds, matching the source controller convenience. JavaScript date coercion remains the separate `hsdk-9j2` contract issue, not a new parser hidden in restoration.

Writers belong to an existing `DaemonSession` attachment (`Daemon.hs:1145–1183,1463`): `Core.withSessionUse` supervises their IO and the existing cleanup registry removes their registrations. A binding-local lock serializes registration and delivery; unique unsubscribe tokens protect replacements. Buffered-prefix acknowledgements retain later output and are revoked by resets, replacement or an earlier acknowledgement. Failed writes retain tracked bytes, unregister their writer, expose `TerminalWriterFailed` and preserve the original exception. Ordinary live callback failures remain isolated by the existing dispatcher, but are not hidden as successful writes. Cancellation does not reverse a partial sink effect.

References: TS 0.7.0 `chunk-5UXINOXG.mjs:16868–16955,17721–17782,19361,19670–19725,20141–20200,21239–21367`; `index-D_SzTnFR.d.ts:44942,113969–114003`; existing terminal codecs/RPCs, ordered reply observation and attachment lifetimes. Eight extracted-source checks cover metadata/order/selection, snapshot and buffer semantics, normal writer flushing and unregistration. Two additional source observations document deliberate differences rather than matches: source reload without a screen erases prior restoration data, and a failed source registration leaves its failing writer installed. Neither behavior is copied.

### Authoritative-cwd delivery

`hsdk-daemon-resources-observability-mxt.4.8` adds cwd observation to the existing immutable session model. The model distinguishes unobserved, provisionally inherited, reported and invalid state. Accepted initialization/load receipts use the existing `daemonInitialWorkingDirectory` precedence, rather than introducing another path resolver: creation uses the accepted worktree path or requested cwd; resume uses reported cwd, the established legacy worktree-path fallback, or known absence. The original receipt remains immutable.

`Daemon.changeWorkingDirectory` publishes the validated `resolvedPath` through `Client.callObserved` and the existing thirty-second connection/intake boundary. The request path, an unrelated `success: false` extension and IO waiter scheduling do not determine the retained value. The existing `WorkingDirectoryEvent` updates state before daemon callbacks; `SessionState.applySessionEventAt` now also understands it. Malformed cwd notifications retain the previous raw value as invalid and make `getWorkingDirectory` fail until repaired. Rejected/malformed RPC results leave prior observations unchanged; a later valid notification remains authoritative after cancellation.

Child discovery seeds a nonempty parent cwd only into unobserved state. Unlike source falsy-value seeding, it cannot replace a reported empty/absent value or conceal an invalid observation. This is provisional metadata, not filesystem resolution, permission, folder trust or session readiness. No local cwd setter, implicit validation/trust call or new state registry is introduced. Low-level client RPCs remain distinct from the observing daemon facade.

References: TS 0.7.0 `chunk-5UXINOXG.mjs:15160–15162,16015–16019,16248,17074–17078,21103–21110,22218–22300`; existing native receipt parser (now `attachmentReceipt`) and directory codecs. Five extracted-source checks verify store empty/null values, resolved-path precedence, rejection behavior, initialization precedence and provisional parent seeding. A separate source observation demonstrates its overwrite of explicitly empty child cwd; that unsafe inference is not counted as a native matching case.

### Pending-interaction delivery

`hsdk-daemon-resources-observability-mxt.4.9` supplies pending enumeration, subscriptions, external observation, explicit responses, inactive decisions and handler replacement. `Interaction.PendingInteractions` retains metadata and a request-local choice; it creates neither a reader nor a request worker. `Protocol.Dispatch` prepares metadata at admission, runs callbacks in its existing owned workers and sends the response through its existing writer. Immutable deferred data belongs to `SessionState`, not a second controller store.

The response choice is a `TMVar (Maybe response)`: empty means undecided, a response is a selected local answer, and `Nothing` suppresses a reply after resolution or inactivity. Scope/binding cancellation uses that same cell; it cannot replace a previously selected answer or suppression. Manual scope exit cancels only undecided requests admitted during that scope. Callback and transport effects already performed cannot be rolled back. The public response methods acknowledge local arbitration, not remote receipt.

Load restoration retains fully validated pending candidates before publication. Eligibility depends on the load generation and completion keyed by **method and request ID**. Fresh live admission explicitly clears its matching completion record; snapshot replay does not. Inactive-worker retirement waits outside intake through `rpcRequestActive`, retaining generation checks. Cleanup remains owned until it finishes. This extends existing admission and load coordination rather than introducing a second deduplicator, replay engine or scheduler (`Protocol/Dispatch.hs:208–266`; `Daemon.hs:733–746,1696–1734,1955–1994`).

Permission surfaces include execution and reported associations; questions remain execution-scoped. Association-only live/restored duplicates update the snapshot without rerunning an admitted callback. Conflicting undecided duplicates select cancellation; prior choices remain committed. Opaque tokens are local to one request/controller and cannot authorize a replacement or external observation. Inactive decisions require explicit validation and storage under execution-session/tool identity; later use requires a fresh request/token and validation again. No automatic approval replay, session resume, child execution or remote rollback is supplied.

Accepted `Daemon.logout` clears remembered authentication, pending authority and deferred data through ordered observation before returning. Rejection preserves all three. `isAuthenticated` includes connection lifetime, not a heartbeat; `connectionUser` remains the immutable receipt. The controller is closed on connection cleanup. Other retained conversation data is not represented as a live authentication assertion.

#### Source reconciliation

Authority is the pinned TS 0.7.0 bundle `chunk-5UXINOXG.mjs:10198–10215,13540–13809,14983–15122,15278–15624,15915–15923,16383–16412,17425–17675,23185–23482`, alongside the existing request/response codecs and Python adapters. Eight extracted-source checks cover association normalization/lookup/growth, permission option rejection/cancellation, preserving relayed permissions, question index validation/cancellation, deferred value/session retention and counted clearing. Bundle SHA256: `c7d07f5089088711616ccca3c2b593bbd85ea5c65252b64b86351bd577c4bde3`.

Native equivalents intentionally differ at documented safety boundaries: permission storage is session-scoped rather than assuming globally unique tool IDs; explicit surfaces and tokens constrain answers; fresh validation replaces automatic buffered approval replay/resume. Association changes are observable through snapshot waits rather than a second request callback. Source inspection disproved an attempted incoming question-response settlement path: these responses are outgoing fire-and-forget messages, not a new peer-resolution protocol. That path was removed rather than repaired with a cast or invented wire format.

The Node probe extracts the actual classes/functions and uses native `EventEmitter`; logging is inert and the error-class substitute only supports rejection checks. Tool-display normalization is outside its scope and throws if invoked. These are selected source-behavior checks, not execution of the complete SDK or real daemon. Native peers model the external WebSocket endpoint, assert response payloads and reject implicit lifecycle mutations; no internal ownership abstraction is mocked.

Read-only advisory workflow `161d9e82-4fa5-42ce-801f-c65862ce2d5f`, child `44f930e2-64d3-44eb-a53f-5af0676f72d9`, inspected installed `process-1.6.26.1`/`unix-2.8.7.0` interfaces and local macOS SDK headers. The supported `CreateProcess` interface maps only stdin/stdout/stderr; `close_fds=False` does not selectively pass a CLOEXEC descriptor. The installed communication-handle implementation leaves the child endpoint inheritable across unrelated spawns. `forkProcess` carries documented multi-capability/inherited-lock risks. Local macOS headers expose spawn descriptor actions yet no `SOCK_CLOEXEC`; ordinary socketpair-then-fcntl leaves a creation-time inheritance window. This is an unresolved route under the current interfaces, not proof that every possible native route is impossible.

Do not turn that proposal into an unsafe inheritable-FD workaround or an ignored `enableIpc` option. The required child-IPC portion remains open. Supplied-channel work is recorded separately below; in-process channels and normal-runtime integration also remain required. Safe independent work may continue while an enforceable private-descriptor route is established; no requirement is waived. ### Host-supplied IPC delivery

`hsdk-docs-package-signoff-ip3.1.1.3` implements `Transport.IPC`: a supplied JSON-text channel is adapted to scoped object send/receive actions. The host retains its connection and callback source. The adapter owns only its registrations; the existing `Protocol.withRpcChannel` owns the sole RPC reader and correlation. No descriptor, process, session runtime, authentication assertion or relay is manufactured.

`IpcMessageChannel` has availability, send, message-registration and optional disconnect-registration actions. Registration is exception-safe; returned stop actions are nonblocking/cooperative. `withIpcChannel` validates its positive message limit, retains early messages and rejects a disconnect that occurs during setup. Local close revokes the lease; peer disconnect preserves the already-received prefix. Serialized sends recheck remembered state after host completion. Host availability is not a heartbeat or permission grant.

The callback inbox and terminal failure have one shared retirement boundary. Invalid input flushes the invalid suffix and records failure in the same STM transaction; delivery cannot commit between those steps. Subscription teardown uses the existing adapter cleanup owner, including a host-thread teardown already in progress. Every stop action is attempted. An original callback/send/receive exception takes precedence over secondary cleanup failure; cleanup failure after an otherwise successful scope is reported. A partially failing host unsubscribe cannot be undone or silently retried.

#### Source reconciliation

Pinned TS 0.7.0 `chunk-5UXINOXG.mjs:12572–12731` and `index-D_SzTnFR.d.ts:108942–108947` define the supplied text channel and IPC transport. Five extracted-class checks verify unavailable setup, text/registration behavior, host send, empty disconnect reason/late-callback suppression/idempotent removal, and availability-loss error/closure. The probe stubs only UUID generation, logging and connection-error construction; it does not claim those facilities were executed. Bundle SHA256 remains `c7d07f5089088711616ccca3c2b593bbd85ea5c65252b64b86351bd577c4bde3`.

Two source discrepancies are explicit, not copied: synchronous registration messages are dropped before its connected flag is set; synchronous registration disconnect can then be overwritten by that flag. Native setup buffers the former and refuses publication after the latter. Native IO also exposes host-send completion/errors directly rather than the source transport's fire-and-forget public send. The object boundary retains exact numbers/empty/false/null values without adding an IPC envelope. Unrelated non-string host messages are excluded by the supplied typed adapter.

| 185 | The existing RPC reader correlates replies and settles a pending request on disconnect. |

**Summary.** First meaningful `-Werror` build began **20:23:00 UTC**, within thirty minutes of the **19:58:44** start. Full formatting/HLint, five audit regressions/reference integrity, five source checks and the matching compiled-only `HostIpcExample.hs` pass on macOS ARM64/GHC 9.10.3.

**Failures fixed.** The initial RPC integration fixture omitted the required `type: request` (`Schema/RPC.hs:186`), leaving its peer waiting after fixture decoding failed. `first-focused.log` records the timeout; the fixture, not the codec, was corrected. Layout and a `when` hint were corrected without suppressions.

Secondary fresh-context review `6db51072-fead-4b08-9cc4-ce8120d21362`, child `835df773-294c-441a-98a4-2b295da57ea6`, found a P1 race: queue flush and invalid-failure publication were separate transactions. The concurrent regression reproduced it naturally in `invalid-race-before.log`: expected `Left IpcInvalidObject`, received `Right (fromList [])`. No scheduling mutant was needed. `failTransport:155–161` now performs both changes atomically, while ordinary peer disconnect still drains its prefix. The regression runs bounded concurrent schedules on two capabilities and passed after the fix. Narrow follow-up `a24d2cf2-a45d-4791-b0d3-52bb448f007b` confirmed the correction by source review. The reviewer ran no tests; no parent-history sentinel was supplied, so independence is not verified.

**Architecture, scope and rubric.** Self-review verdict: **ALIGNED**. This is a callback-to-object input adapter, not another protocol processor. Queuing belongs to the transport boundary; correlation and session/permission handling remain in their existing owners. The native peer implements the external host interface and real callback/cancellation schedules, not mocks of internal SDK ownership. No production stub, hidden fallback, invented descriptor enablement, dropped assertion, disabled test, suppressed diagnostic or new dependency is present. The new transport/test module and Cabal/Main registration are required; other source files were not changed for this leaf. The initial wrong fixture and the actual reviewed race remain visible in evidence.

Exception catches in `withIpcChannel:76–86` and `preservingFailure:174–178` enforce primary-exception precedence, not success substitution; other consumers see the recorded terminal error. Scope expiry ignores late host callbacks by ownership, and malformed data is a terminal error rather than an empty object. The callback queue remains unbounded, as documented beside the existing RPC event-queue convention. The per-message limit does not certify bounded aggregate memory, and callers must keep producer/consumer rates appropriate.

**Verification gaps and next work.** No unresolved reproduced correctness finding remains in this supplied-channel leaf. ### In-process channel delivery

`hsdk-docs-package-signoff-ip3.1.1.4` supplies `Transport.InProcess`: optional lifecycle/message/close/error subscriptions, explicit event observation and wait-only pending-readiness delegation around a caller-supplied message handler. It implements no daemon or session methods. The existing `Protocol`/`Client` path constructs envelopes, correlates responses and decodes remote errors.

The source requires nonterminal runtime error events and full close codes, which the IPC-specific surface alone could not express. Callback framing/lifetime moved into private `Internal.MessageChannel`, with an explicit send-failure policy and full close data. The public IPC wrapper preserves its names, error constructors and terminal-send policy; all seventeen IPC tests remain unchanged in their assertions and pass. In-process raw sends preserve handler exceptions while retaining transport usability. The enclosing `RpcChannel` keeps its existing write-failure policy.

Scoped runtime cleanup is established before subscription acquisition and shared with explicit close through one once-only owner. `finishChannel` joins subscription retirement before runtime teardown. Waiting for that cleanup lock is uninterruptible, but supplied callbacks are not; `allowInterrupt` after releasing the lock delivers deferred cancellation. This preserves order and exception identity without blanket masking of arbitrary runtime code. Registration/teardown must remain cooperative, and no bounded shutdown claim is made for a callback that never returns.

`setPendingSessionReady` checks scope admission and delegates an opaque session ID and `STM ()` readiness gate without awaiting, starting or owning the producer. The optional hook can retain the gate; producer failure remains observable when the host awaits it. No extra load/readiness registry or automatic task is added.

The probe substitutes UUID generation, logging and the ordinary error constructor only; no live runtime is invoked. Recorded source differences are not copied: failed connect leaves subscriptions, disconnect exceptions are logged/ignored, and close during connect can be overwritten. Native scopes clean failed setup and report cleanup errors; already-closed/partial runtime cleanup remains a documented provider contract.

### Injected-session runtime delivery

`hsdk-docs-package-signoff-ip3.1.1.5` exposes normal local/daemon sessions over borrowed `ObjectTransport` actions. `DroidSessionOptions` separates local session settings from process launch options; `DaemonClientOptions` separates daemon/session configuration from a WebSocket endpoint. Existing options project into these records and existing constructors enter the same session/connection implementation. No dummy endpoint, alternate daemon client or second RPC dispatcher was added.

Authentication is explicit: `DaemonAuthenticate` performs the existing RPC, while `DaemonInheritAuthentication` requires the caller's trusted identity receipt and exact session token. Locality is separate data used only for hosted-MCP co-location checks. An unknown host rejects session-owned servers before startup. Native callback tests prove that an in-process/local transport still performs and can fail authentication unless inherited authentication was expressly selected.

Each `SessionConnection` now uses a UUID namespace plus its atomic request counter (`Internal/Session.hs:273–274,1110–1113`), avoiding deterministic request-ID reuse across logical scopes. IDs remain opaque. Physical stream ownership is still exclusive; namespaces do not solve arbitrary multiplexing or stale untagged notifications. Supplied I/O and its outer physical scope remain caller-owned, while SDK scope expires handles and joins RPC/dispatcher/session work.

The readiness hook deliberately does **not** receive the controller's full load-completion ticket. `withSessionReadyGate` creates one private gate per initialization/load RPC, registers it before dispatch and settles it after the validated receipt or original error. Later terminal restoration may itself rely on that gate. Reusing the controller ticket would therefore create a cycle. Existing load epochs continue to govern state publication/restoration independently; an old raw receipt cannot publish into a successor generation.

Authority: TS `DroidClientOptions.transport` (`index-D_SzTnFR.d.ts:24051–24054`), `connectAndAuthenticate` (`chunk-5UXINOXG.mjs:11814–11825`), explicit trusted authentication (`:15645–15655`), initialization readiness (`:15981–15994`) and raw load readiness (`:16239–16245`). The supplied daemon schemas require initialization/load tokens as strings without a nonempty refinement. Five source checks cover ordinary authentication order/exact data, authentication failure cleanup, explicit trusted mode, raw-load promise identity/order and that token constraint. Source stubs are limited to logging/status reporting and caller-owned test promises; no Factory service is invoked.

## Relay authentication delivery

Issue `hsdk-docs-package-signoff-ip3.1.2.1` supplies relay token acquisition, organization isolation and the applicable daemon authentication handoff. `Transport.Relay.withRelayConnection` owns no second socket implementation or RPC reader: it uses the existing WebSocket scope, consumes the relay control exchange, then supplies `ObjectTransport` and `DaemonAuthentication` to the existing daemon constructors. The connection-only, new-session, resumed-session and explicit-reload paths all use the shared credential getter. Binary tunneling, connection retries/readiness/hooks and telemetry remain separate required children.

The published helper at `chunk-5UXINOXG.mjs:12131–12205` sends `method = relay.authenticate`, the token, and a truthy active organization; it does not send a JSON-RPC envelope or act-as grant. `WebSocketDaemonTransport:13130–13218` fetches credentials before its ten-second authentication exchange. API keys carry their own organization context. Native bearer organization snapshots are connection-scoped; empty/absent values omit the field, whitespace remains exact, and neither locality nor a previous connection supplies a replacement. Bearer providers must continue to represent the same principal and selected organization; changing them requires a new scope. The SDK cannot infer identity from an opaque token.

`DaemonTokenProvider` supplies a fresh bearer token for initial daemon authentication and each init/load; its grant remains immutable. `DaemonInheritAuthenticationProvider` uses explicit caller-supplied identity and does not invoke its provider until init/load. Static credential constructors retain their behavior. The source load path at `16205–16228` fetches a token for every load, overrides a supplied token and rejects null rather than empty text. Native `Nothing` therefore raises `DaemonCredentialUnavailable` without cached fallback, while `Just ""` remains an explicit session token. Provider work is cancellable caller-supplied I/O outside raw RPC deadlines and readiness gates; it can run concurrently for different sessions. No periodic refresh, authentication replay or OAuth implementation is implied.

The rejection codec retains optional code/retryability, exact positive-integer delay and additional fields. Its finite-number range check precedes exact integrality validation, while the stored value remains `Scientific`; the native peer proves retention of `9007199254740993`. The source error wrapper drops `retryAfterMs`, although its schema admits it. Native recognized malformed rejections fail promptly rather than waiting for the source helper's timeout; malformed JSON and binary frames retain the WebSocket boundary's rejection policy. Other valid objects arriving before acceptance are retained in order within explicit message/encoded-byte limits, then returned through the sole application receiver. These limits are not heap-size guarantees. A fresh scope, not implicit replay, is required after retirement.

The final all-component `-Werror` build passes in **4.392s**, and **All 3622 tests passed (10.93s)** (**12.862s** Cabal command). The earlier 11.24-second full-suite receipt remains. Full formatting/HLint, five audit regressions/reference integrity, eight cached-source checks and the matching compiled-only `RelayExample.hs` pass on macOS ARM64/GHC 9.10.3.

**Findings and corrections.** The first focused run did not execute tests: `test/RelaySpec.hs` failed `-Werror` for a redundant `Data.Text` import and partial `head`/`tail` use. Explicit list patterns replaced partial access; the later expanded cases legitimately import `Text`. Formatting and `when`/`unless` hints were corrected without suppressions. A timeout fixture no longer requires proof that a write occurred before its deadline: expiration can precede dispatch; the separate cancellation test still waits for a received authentication frame before cancelling. The standalone example formatter initially lacked the Cabal project's GHC2024 context and rejected postpositive qualified imports; supplying `--ghc-opt=-XGHC2024` matched the already-used compilation language and passed. That initial failure remains in the tool receipt; no parser check was disabled. The final example takes the remote machine ID explicitly rather than relying on the generic `local` default.

Self-review then found a concrete lifetime defect in the new adapter: after an oversized send retired its underlying WebSocket, the buffered pre-authentication prefix remained readable. `buffer-retirement-before.log` records **expected `Left RelayClosed`, got `Right (fromList [("early",Bool True)])`**. The shared relay operation boundary now retires both buffered and socket I/O on failure, clears the prefix, and preserves the original exception (`src/Factory/Droid/Transport/Relay.hs:123–136`). Scope exit uses the same retirement action. The first fix failed to compile under local monomorphic inference (`GHC-83865`); an explicit polymorphic `perform :: IO b -> IO b` signature fixes the type, without casts or changed checks. `buffer-retirement-verified.log` contains the passing regression and all thirty-three focused cases.

Fresh-context review `01f421b5-9042-46ba-8e79-04b9bfd920e0`, child `2b9bcabd-b03f-40f6-8b56-5301c8b0e397`, inspected the relay module, daemon provider/shared ownership paths, cached helper/schema and tests, and reported no issue. It did not find the subsequently reproduced prefix defect. The final retirement correction and additional cases were self-reviewed/native-tested, not re-reviewed by that child. The reviewer ran no build/tests and did not verify the Git baseline. No parent-history sentinel was supplied, so independent review is not attested.

During continuation I incorrectly announced a pause based on the historical tracking amendment. The live goal was running; the notice was corrected without changing goal state or imposing a new resume requirement. The post-compaction refocus reread was delayed, rather than immediate. This was an unnecessary process detour, not a user stop or permission change.

**What the twenty-one native tests verify.** Each peer is an external wire boundary; production session, RPC, credential and transport owners remain real.

1. Bearer relay frames contain the exact organization but no daemon grant/envelope; the handed-off daemon grant and nonlocal status remain correct.
2. API-key frames omit organization and retain the daemon API-key variant.
3. Absent/empty organization values omit the field, while whitespace remains exact.
4. New connection scopes fetch fresh tokens and organization snapshots without cross-scope cache reuse.
5. Missing/empty bearer values and original provider exceptions prevent authenticated publication.
6. Rejections preserve false retryability, exact delay, message/code/extensions and redacted display.
7. Malformed recognized rejection fields, unknown codes, fractional delay and null optional values fail closed.
8. Authentication deadlines reject; cancellation after observed dispatch preserves its original exception.
9. Early valid objects are delivered after acceptance, and escaped scoped handles cannot read retained prefixes.
10. A failed send makes retained prefixes unreadable, rather than bypassing WebSocket retirement.
11. Both prelude message and byte limits reject excess input.
12. Relay auth, daemon auth and two loads use distinct wire operations and freshly fetched tokens.
13. An unavailable load token sends no cached replacement; a later explicit empty-token load remains possible.
14. All four rejection codes and absent optional fields retain their distinctions.
15. Nonpositive and extreme-exponent retry delays reject within the bounded native fixture.
16. Organization-provider and user-callback failures preserve identity and produce an observed peer close.
17. Cancellation during token acquisition preserves identity and produces an observed peer close.
18. Peer close codes, binary frames and malformed JSON remain typed transport failures.
19. Both normal create and resume constructors fetch fresh session tokens after authentication.
20. Explicit inherited authentication performs no auth RPC and fetches its token only for the session operation.
21. Invalid relay configuration precedes provider/network work, and missing/empty initial dynamic credentials precede daemon authentication.

**Stubs, swallowing and scope.** No production stub, silent credential fallback, downgraded checker or new dependency was introduced. `onException` retires the adapter and rethrows; malformed rejection diagnostics are deliberately payload-free. Test peers end on WebSocket close exceptions; assertion failures and malformed client-JSON assertions are not swallowed. The source probe evaluates the published helper, error subclasses and Zod schema using a fake socket/clock/tracing boundary and `Error` base, not a copied authentication implementation. It records the source's synchronous-send listener leak until timeout rather than reproducing that leak in the native adapter. These probes are not live service execution.

The source changes are limited to the new relay adapter, the existing daemon credential/auth/init/load seam, package/test registration, the new native tests, and required README/parity/handoff/tracker records. The full unstaged Git diff also contains earlier resource/state/transport delivery; that older work was preserved, not claimed as part of this leaf. Display redaction does not sanitize explicit fields, JSON, provider exceptions or stderr.

No live Factory/relay/daemon, alternative compiler/platform, current independent distribution, commit or off-machine protection is established. ## Binary relay tunnel delivery

Issue `hsdk-docs-package-signoff-ip3.1.2.2` supplies binary target/auth/data/error/close without treating tunnel traffic as daemon JSON-RPC. `relayTunnelTarget` builds the canonical origin-relative route; `withRelayTunnel`, `sendTunnelData`, `receiveTunnelData`, last-known openness and explicit close use the existing socket owner and shared relay authentication/prefix lifetime. `defaultTunnelWebSocketOptions` preserves verified TLS and the source thirty-second setup default. No connection retry or endpoint-wakeup workflow is implemented in this leaf.

The source is published TS 0.7.0 `chunk-5UXINOXG.mjs:12205–12380`, with `TunnelConnectionEvents`/options at `index-D_SzTnFR.d.ts:109014–109030` and methods at `111910–111922`. It constructs `/v0/computer/${computerId}/tunnel?port=…`, converts HTTP(S) origins to WS(S), authenticates before installing data handlers, forwards binary buffers/views, ignores text, and closes with code 1000/reason `tunnel closed`. Native scheme/TLS selection uses the existing separated WebSocket options/target representation. The computer ID is encoded as one path component rather than allowing separators/query syntax to alter the route; empty, `.` and `..` identities and non-TCP ports are rejected.

`MessageWebSocket` is the same physical TCP/TLS/HTTP owner before choosing object or byte semantics. `ObjectWebSocket` projects it without changing existing JSON, UTF-8, binary-rejection, code-only peer-close, normal-close-reason or serialized-decoding contracts. New raw text/binary operations preserve payloads and full UTF-8 close metadata. The once-only explicit/final cleanup shares writer/reader locks and a cleanup lock, protects only cleanup-lock joining uninterruptibly, and delivers deferred cancellation after unlocking. Existing primary-exception preservation is reused; close reason preflight counts 123 UTF-8 bytes, not characters.

The private `RelayWire`/`RelayStream` structures generalize the existing authentication and bounded-prefix retirement paths for their two actual consumers: objects and WebSocket messages. They add neither a background reader nor a second connection runtime. Binary data received before authentication is bounded and withheld until acceptance, rather than dropped as it is before the source's data-handler registration. Unrecognized/non-JSON text remains bounded before acceptance and is ignored as tunnel data; recognized malformed auth rejections still fail. A binary payload that resembles an auth response cannot authenticate. Daemon-only grants are rejected before acquisition instead of silently ignored.

Native consumption is pull I/O: receive observes peer data/close/errors, and openness is last-known local state rather than a liveness probe. The source's synthetic error event can remain nonterminal; native failed socket I/O retires the exchange. Empty bytes are data, not EOF. Write completion is not target-application acknowledgment, and frame/chunk boundaries do not define application responses. Callback/provider/cancellation exceptions retain identity; ordinary transport failures retain their existing payload-free types. Explicit fields, bytes and metadata remain sensitive despite redacted displays.

### Binary tunnel fess

**Summary.** First meaningful verification began **2026-09-11 01:04:48 UTC**, within thirty minutes of the **00:48:18** start, and passed **46 existing WebSocket/relay cases (1.15s)**. Fourteen new tunnel cases pass with them: **60 tests (1.20s)**. The warning-free all-component build passed in **10.585s**; **All 3636 tests passed (11.28s)** (**14.455s** Cabal command). Full Ormolu/HLint, five audit regressions/reference integrity, eight new extracted-class checks, the eight shared-auth checks and the matching compiled-only `TunnelExample.hs` pass on macOS ARM64/GHC 9.10.3.

**Findings and corrections.** The initial native tunnel run passed thirteen of fourteen new cases but failed the concurrent-close assertion: **expected `Nothing`, got `Just ()`**. This was an invalid fixture, not a reproduced production failure. `websockets-0.13.0.0` `receiveDataMessage` automatically sends Close acknowledgment before throwing `CloseRequest`; waiting afterward did not hold the SDK cleanup open. The cached `Connection.hs:273–302` confirms that behavior. The fixture and its acknowledgment helper now use public raw `WS.receive`, hold the ACK explicitly, and retain the original cleanup-wait and cancellation-identity assertions. The second close enters under ordinary interruptible masking so cancellation cannot win before the tested join is reached. No production workaround was added for the fixture.

Formatting failures remain in the early receipts; some multi-file formatter output interleaved, so final checks ran one file per process. ## Connection retry delivery

Issue `hsdk-docs-package-signoff-ip3.1.2.3` supplies explicit bounded retry and reconnection policies without repeating an entered session/application callback. `Factory.Droid.Retry` contains one executor for replay-safe actions and scoped connection factories; the latter reuse existing `Daemon.withConnection`, WebSocket and relay owners. Failed acquisition scopes finish cleanup before another attempt. Publication occurs before the success hook and caller body, on the caller thread, so their failures and later cleanup failures cannot restart acquisition.

The source has distinct contracts, not one interchangeable delay formula: `chunk-5UXINOXG.mjs:9718–9800` and declarations `index-D_SzTnFR.d.ts:108694–108729` describe the generic helper; `11814–11902` the connect/auth helper; `12881–12915` WebSocket retries; `13290–13300`, `13535–13609` and `15743–15863` reconnection policy/delegation. Generic `retries` counts total attempts, default three; connect/auth permits eleven attempts by default. Socket backoff starts at 500 ms, doubles and caps at 5,000 ms. Reconnection waits before its first attempt, starts at 1,000 ms, uses factor 1.5/cap 10,000 ms and adds up to 30% jitter after capping. Generic jitter instead scales to 50–100%. Native schedules use the first actual delay and millisecond units, avoiding an implicit exponent offset; the generic 250 ms/factor-2 exponential source case therefore starts at 500 ms.

Policies validate attempt counts and finite/nonnegative delay data. Delay computation caps before jitter, rounds down to whole milliseconds and checks native timer conversion rather than wrapping or silently using a short delay. Jitter uses 53 uniformly selected bits from the existing `crypton` dependency; no new package or mandatory logger is introduced. Custom delay overrides jitter after failures. Failure hooks see one-based eligible failure counts, including the last; success sees prior failures. Exhaustion recovery is an explicit callback, not a hidden default. Source success-hook failures can repeat already-completed work; native publication prevents that.

Abort is a caller-owned waiting STM reason, checked before work/publication and during backoff. It does not cancel or undo an in-flight action; normal Haskell asynchronous cancellation remains separate. Pre-aborted work does not start, and abort observed after work finishes prevents publication without replay. The source permits pre-aborted successful work to execute and retains abort listeners after a successful sleep. Native waits use scoped `race`/`threadDelay`, not an extra timer/listener registry. An initial `registerDelay` sketch was replaced before focused execution; no runtime leak was claimed as reproduced for that sketch.

`withReconnection` selects explicit local or delegated ownership. Local ownership uses the supplied schedule; delegated ownership invokes only the external scoped factory, once, without local delay or fallback to local restart. That owner supplies its own polling and I/O bounds. No machine-name/locality inference or automatic session/queue/permission replay occurs. Endpoint polling, ensure-running, active connection-state orchestration and lifecycle hooks remain separate required children.

1. Profiles preserve total attempts, zero-based delay indexing, caps, both jitter ranges and initial reconnection delay.
2. Invalid policies precede work; exponential overflow fails rather than becoming a short timer, while a valid cap remains effective.
3. Failure callbacks, custom base delays and success counts occur in source-compatible order.
4. The final eligible failure is notified and explicit recovery receives the original last cause.

6. A success-hook failure cannot repeat completed work or invoke exhaustion recovery.
7. Invalid custom delay results stop without another attempt or hidden recovery.
8. Pre-abort and abort after the last failure retain the supplied reason rather than returning a fallback.
9. Abort stops backoff/initial delay without another attempt, using an explicit first-failure barrier where needed.
10. In-flight work is not rolled back; its completion cannot publish a result after observed abort.
11. Standard asynchronous cancellation bypasses retry hooks.
12. Real relay acquisition failures close each physical scope before retry and publish exactly one successful connection.
13. A real entered relay callback stays on the caller thread; its transmitted work and failure occur only once.
14. Cleanup failure after publication does not restart the factory or caller.
15. Delegated recovery selects only the external factory without local schedule or repeated external acquisition.
16. Real daemon authentication retries before publishing one authenticated connection.
17. Abort at scoped publication releases acquisition without entering caller work or retrying.
18. A scoped success-hook failure closes acquisition without entering or replaying the caller.

**Stubs, swallowing and scope.** No production stub, weakened checker or dependency fallback was added. `trySync` deliberately retries ordinary action/setup failures according to the caller predicate while propagating standard asynchronous exceptions. Hooks, delay calculation and explicit abort failures do not become implicit retries. `retryOnAllError` is a named caller-selected recovery; its absence preserves the original failure. Native peers emit actual JSON/WebSocket frames, and the source probe evaluates cached helper/strategy code with fake clocks/logger/random/attempt boundaries rather than contacting services. The scope-factory fixtures are boundary owners, not replacements for internal SDK state.

The change comprises the new retry module, its tests/registration and required README/parity/handoff/tracker records; existing connection/session owners were composed rather than copied or changed. A scoped factory must itself be replay-safe before publication, acquire a fresh transport, invoke its callback within that scope, and await its result. The API cannot establish that property for arbitrary user `IO`; it must not wrap a session-initializing factory or a fixed borrowed stream. The compiled example places saved-session load and user work after `Daemon.withConnection` publication.

Ordinary exceptions thrown via `throwTo` cannot be distinguished from synchronous failures: custom asynchronous types require the standard async exception instance pair, a predicate that excludes them or the explicit abort gate.
