## Connection readiness delivery

Issue `hsdk-docs-package-signoff-ip3.1.2.4` supplies initial connection attempts, coalesced polling, remembered status, explicit ensure-running composition and connection/session readiness. `Factory.Droid.Connection` holds existing native daemon/relay scopes on one worker, not a second protocol reader or session runtime. User work runs through `withReadyConnection` on its caller thread. Existing session load coordination, readiness snapshots and raw init/load gates remain authoritative rather than being copied into the connection controller.

The source is TS 0.7.0 `chunk-5UXINOXG.mjs:14440–14682` (polling, pre-spawn/auth budgets, usable connection and ensure-running), `15644–15738`/`15876–15934` (authentication/logout) and `17644–17677` (status), with progress/status declarations at `index-D_SzTnFR.d.ts:113611–113650`. Native defaults retain fifteen counted polls, one-second spacing, a 120-second elapsed pre-spawn exemption and three further authentication attempts after the first observed transport connection. The effective denominator can grow and uncounted attempts can repeat their ordinal. Zero attempts returns false without work unless already ready; a negative configuration is rejected. Monotonic elapsed time avoids extending the grace period through wall-clock adjustment.

The first poll caller supplies progress/abort options; followers share its pending ticket. Cancelling a waiting thread does not cancel controller-owned acquisition, while an explicit owner abort settles the shared poll. A single pending slot is the work source, with a separate active-request owner. Publication/settlement clears both atomically. This prevents old connection teardown from consuming an unstarted poll and avoids a second queue containing already-settled work. Scope closure seals admission, settles waiters, cancels/joins acquisition and lets the existing connection scope release its resources.

`daemonConnectionPlan` and `relayConnectionPlan` preserve the existing connection/authentication paths. Transport-open markers precede authentication, allowing status to distinguish open from usable. The optional ensure-running action runs before physical acquisition, not for cached readiness or in-place auth repair; it is caller-supplied and must be authorized and replay-safe. Host-specific pre-spawn/transient-token classification is explicit rather than inferred from locality or a failed socket. Ordinary progress-observer failures remain inspectable in status without terminating the loop; observers must not await that same poll.

`Daemon.ConnectionHealth` and its IO/STM getters read existing connection/authentication state. `ensureConnectionAuthenticated` coalesces in-place repair, validates user/org against the original receipt and publishes authentication/token-provider state at the existing ordered RPC intake boundary. Auth epochs make a later logout authoritative over an older response. Failure, mismatch or owner cancellation retires the logical connection; a cancelled lock waiter does not retire the owner. Inherited authentication remains a fresh explicit host attestation. `connectionUser` is still an immutable receipt, not live authentication.

RPC channel retirement now retains its first original I/O cause atomically with the existing payload-free channel error. Normal cleanup cannot replace that cause. Readiness uses available observed close codes to retain permanent/transient failure distinctions rather than labeling every loss retryable. This extends the existing failure owner rather than installing another transport listener. Explicit cause data can be sensitive; default status/error displays remain redacted or payload-free.

## Connection hook delivery

Issue `hsdk-docs-package-signoff-ip3.1.2.5` adds request guards, settlement observation, pending inspection and connection close/error/identity metadata through the existing owners. `Protocol` retains the sole pending map and reader; `Protocol.Dispatch` retains the sole intake and server-worker owner. `Client` supplies typed per-request guard selection. Normal local handlers and daemon attachments install settlement subscriptions before initialization/loading; daemon connection subscriptions remain connection-wide. Transport kinds are descriptive data, not credentials, trust or ownership dispatch.

Sources are TS 0.7.0 `chunk-5UXINOXG.mjs:10047–10168` (request admission/count), `10377–10517` (guard/auth sequencing and lifecycle accessors), `14231–14277` (all thirty automatic-load exclusions), and `node.mjs:1696–1791` (local settlement/rejection). Eight executed-source cases and two structural checks in `hooks-reference.mjs` verify the relevant helpers without loading a live SDK, daemon or account. This is not whole-program differential certification.

The optional guard applies only to string `params.sessionId`, preserves empty strings and runs on the requesting thread before pending/writer admission. It precedes authentication waiting and the send/reply budget. Zero/negative budgets fail before guard work; arbitrary guard/provider I/O requires an external bound. Replacement/unsubscribe tokens cannot remove a successor registration. The default daemon guard uses existing `ensureSessionLoaded`, with the complete source-defined lifecycle/control-plane exclusion set; unknown sessions are not loaded speculatively. Skipping the optional guard does not skip mandatory barriers.

Authentication waiting uses the existing lock. Explicit logout bypasses that wait so an in-flight grant cannot delay revocation. The lock retains the first terminal repair failure for queued callers; failed repair retires the logical scope, so this cannot permit retry or successor adoption. The source resolves its auth promise even on failure: retaining the native originating failure is a stronger diagnostic, not a demonstrated source-contract correction. The gate waits for in-flight authentication only; it does not introduce blanket client-side authorization for every RPC. Existing pending/deferred authority checks and server authorization remain unchanged.

Settlement snapshots are admitted once at the existing terminal transition, after ordered result observation; bulk failure follows request admission order. Pending count excludes guards and accepted responses, even if the original sender has not returned. Unsubscribe affects later snapshots, not already admitted callbacks. IO delivery requires the existing active consumer, and scope exit may cancel unfinished intake; the compiled example waits for delivery before leaving scope. Ordinary observer failures are isolated, while request/guard failures propagate and cancellation identity is retained. Observers must not await later intake or initiate load/restore/detach/replace/authentication cycles that require it. Server permission/question requests are not outgoing settlements.

`Daemon.getConnectionId` reports the existing logical UUID namespace while open. `getTransportKind` exposes the adapter's explicit kind; helper constructors supply the new `ObjectTransport.transportKind` field. `onConnectionError` and `onConnectionClose` retain original causes; normal logical closure carries no invented physical close code. `receiveObjectWithClose` shares the existing WebSocket decoder/owner while retaining full close metadata; the older `receiveObject` remains code-only. Relay authentication keeps its existing setup-error projection, while application RPC close observation retains the full cause. Existing transport-open markers and remembered health/status complete the lifecycle mapping without another listener runtime.

**What the nineteen tests verify.** Fixtures use real native RPC, dispatcher, process, WebSocket and daemon/session code; only the external peer behavior is scripted.

1. Only string session IDs invoke the guard, including the empty string, on the caller thread before pending admission and mandatory gates.
2. Typed per-request skipping cannot bypass a mandatory gate or admit a rejected request.
3. A guard can perform an explicitly skipped correlated request without holding the writer.
4. Stale unsubscribe cannot remove a replacement; guard failure and zero/negative preflight admit no settlement.
5. Cancelling a blocked guard preserves cancellation identity and leaves the channel usable.
6. Result observation precedes settlement; ordinary callback failure is isolated and exact session filters do not leak other IDs.
7. An accepted reply is no longer pending even while the sender remains blocked.
8. Remote rejection, malformed response, timeout and cancellation each settle once; late/duplicate replies do not settle again.
9. Transport failure settles in admission order before error/close delivery and supports closed-error replay.
10. Normal close is once-only and unsubscribable; synchronous or async cleanup failure cannot replace the original scope error.
11. Unsubscribe does not revoke an already queued settlement snapshot.
12. Normal local initialization and turn traffic execute the configured handler through a native child peer.
13. Normal daemon calls expose logical identity, adapter kind, pending count, settlement identity and logical closure.
14. The guard runs before authentication waiting, and cancelling the waiting request does not cancel repair.
15. An authentication-settled observer can issue a correlated query without waiting on its own intake barrier.
16. A queued request retains the original failed-repair exception and sends nothing after that failure.
17. Immediate write failure and blocked-write timeout settle once and retain payload-free RPC errors.
18. Default guarding reloads a known session only when required; control-plane exclusions and attachment settlement/detach remain isolated.
19. A native WebSocket close reaches daemon observation with its exact code and reason, once.

**Stubs, swallowing, scope and documentation.** No production stub, dependency upgrade, optional missing-dependency fallback, new reader or duplicate session runtime was introduced. Silent catches are confined to documented optional observer isolation; mandatory guard/repair failures are not suppressed. The WebSocket close projection preserves an existing public API rather than replacing it. Required files are Protocol/Dispatch/Client, Daemon and local handler wiring, transport metadata/full-close projection, Connection classification, tests/registration and existing documentation/tracker records. Older uncommitted work is not attributed to this leaf. New record fields are documented with helper/default constructors. The API does not promise observer completion merely because an RPC receipt returned, or remote effect completion merely because a request settled.

## Initialization configuration delivery

Leaf `hsdk-docs-package-signoff-ip3.2.1.2` implements creation-time configuration through existing local/daemon construction, RPC and receipt owners. `Schema.Configuration` supplies `SessionConfiguration`, complete local/daemon initialization parameters, explicit daemon spawn options and validation. Four tool-policy fields reuse `Schema.Settings` helpers; enums, prompt/source/tag/mission/output/MCP representations are reused. Existing model/cwd/MCP/worktree fields keep their owners rather than acquiring competing values in the supplemental record.

Owned and injected local construction now projects optional machine identity and configuration into the same encoder. Daemon construction adds configuration, structured prompt, spawn settings and initialization timeout. Caller session IDs are validated against receipts; absent daemon IDs still receive a UUID. Reserved identity, credential and declared fields cannot be replaced by extension-map entries. Unknown extensions, explicit false/empty lists and nullable output capability retain their values. Normal validation runs before acquisition while preserving existing MCP error classes and create-only resume rejection.

A requested structured `systemPrompt` must be present in returned settings before receipt observation or handle publication. Missing acknowledgment is `DroidInvalidEvent`; this does not demand textual equality or conflate the older raw `systemPromptOverride`. Low-level Client initialization validates parameters and returns the raw result object; normal session constructors retain their required receipt validation and lifecycle publication. No full JSON Schema evaluation is implied by forwarding `OutputFormat`.

Source daemon initialization retries only wire timeouts, twice, with a stable session ID/token/parameter object and fresh RPC IDs. The default is 60,000,000 microseconds per attempt, with an optional override and a checked two-attempt aggregate initialization/restoration budget after connection/authentication. Zero sends no session init; invalid/overflowing budgets fail preflight. A wire-only deadline extension to the existing observed-call helper prevents retries of restoration, token acquisition or caller work. One existing raw readiness gate covers the attempt sequence. This relies on the source daemon's documented session-ID deduplication contract; native fake peers prove client behavior, not live-server idempotency.

References: published TS 0.7.0 `chunk-5UXINOXG.mjs:3550–3677` (tool/init/output parameters), `6870–6893` (daemon extension), `13311–13312` (two attempts/60,000ms), `15950–16038` (retry/readiness) and pinned Python `_high_level/session.py:368–411` (startup forwarding and prompt acknowledgment). `pullFromOrigin` is first-message input in the published daemon schema, not an added init field. Eight selected schemas/method checks ran from the actual cache with explicit unrelated-schema/error/logger/client boundaries; the six builder/two structural Python mapping checks also reran successfully. No live SDK or account was loaded.

## Resume/load policy delivery

Leaf `hsdk-docs-package-signoff-ip3.2.1.3` adds validated load configuration/parameters and retention through normal local, daemon, injected, explicit-reload and borrowed-attachment paths. `SessionLoadConfiguration` preserves caller intent; `prepareLoadSessionParams` produces a legal load request plus an optional post-load restriction patch. MCP configuration remains in its existing owner. Local load state resides in the local backend; daemon policy resides in existing `SessionLoadEntry` generations, not a parallel cache or reader.

Creation settings seed future load policy, with explicit load overrides applied separately from initial parameters. Omission preserves retained intent, explicit false/empty lists replace it, and nullable output distinguishes preservation, clearing and replacement. Message limits retain exact positive finite integer values. Known init-only fields and direct restriction load fields fail validation. Daemon tokens refresh on each actual load; initial unsafe-permission bypass is not automatically copied into later loads, matching the source controller. Explicit load bypass intent can be retained until replaced or the session definitively closes.

Policy selection and generation advancement share STM admission. The existing RPC writer rechecks a nonblocking admission action before physical I/O, so a superseded queued load or restriction mutation cannot escape. Guard failure retains its original exception without retiring a healthy channel. Actual transport failures retain the existing sanitized request-error contract and explicit original raw/cause access. Post-load restrictions skip only optional ensure-loaded guarding, never mandatory authentication waiting; pending restoration/readiness and user publication occur afterward. Local replacement failure restores the source session and reapplies its policy.

Local cwd uses the existing `WorkingDirectoryState` representation and ordered intake. Initial receipt prefers a validated worktree path; load prefers direct cwd and uses a legacy worktree path only when direct cwd is omitted. Successful unknown replacement state may inherit a known nonempty parent path after load succeeds. Explicit null/invalid state is not hidden by inheritance, and missing metadata is not replaced by host launch cwd. Invalid direct receipt data prevents publication; invalid recognized events retain invalid state until repaired. Ordinary cwd getters are last-observed views, not synchronization barriers or trust decisions. Daemon cwd retains its original owner without a duplicate cache.

Sources: TS 0.7.0 load schema at `chunk-5UXINOXG.mjs:3647–3677`, daemon load extension at `6877–6892`, initialization-retained fields at `16032–16040`, output preservation at `14228–14230` and load orchestration at `16185–16248`; pinned Python `_load_with_policies` and `_convert.load_cwd`. Source differences are explicit: Python supplies fallback paths while TS consumes reported cwd directly; native uses reported/legacy/inherited/unknown states and preserves explicit null. Native rejects unsupported known load fields instead of silently stripping them, and honors explicit low-level message-selection values while defaulting ordinary daemon loads to full messages.

## Operation refinement reconciliation

`hsdk-runtime-refinements-tfo` is resolved against the approved functional SDK scope, not an exhaustive 1.205.0 schema target. Historical notes said worker progress, settings, permission and system-prompt refinements were unimplemented or blocked on newer internals; current code and source-backed tests establish those operational contracts. No new runtime implementation was necessary. The [refinement disposition](parity.md#runtime-refinements-operational-contracts-and-optional-markers) now identifies required paths separately from unused backend/disk schemas.

Published custom-model CRUD uses dedicated upsert/summary request/result schemas (`chunk-5UXINOXG.mjs:6382–6395,6463–6474,10729–10754`), not `ManagedCustomModelSchema` or its model-registry predicate. The native API reports invalid rows intentionally and never substitutes unvalidated managed-file data for that contract. Published automation create/update likewise use their own RPC parameter schemas (`5565–5602,5753–5776,11280–11385`), not engine tool-input refinements. Loop/automation tool-input and managed-disk array schemas occur only as declarations in the inspected runtime; the newer worktree-profile save operation is absent from the pinned runtime, guide/README and Python client surface.

This classification follows upstream routing, not mere absence in the Haskell implementation. The old managed-model `authMode`/registry mismatch remains true for optional disk-schema work, but does not block these SDK operations. No credential helper was executed, trust filtering was weakened, model registry invented, or codec/test removed. Existing worktree reports and other represented schema data remain intact. The original issue description is retained in its notes as historical context rather than erased.

## Standalone stream delivery

**Summary.** `hsdk-docs-package-signoff-ip3.2.2.2` supplies `Factory.Droid.Stream` through the existing stream reducer. The normal local/daemon `runEventTurn` now uses that same feed and collector instead of its private queue/loop. This adds no protocol reader, transport, session owner or implicit interrupt. Current macOS ARM64/GHC 9.10.3 verification passed **3,779 tests (11.21s)**, **13.04s** for Cabal, and the warning-free all-component build passed in **4.12s**. Full Ormolu/HLint, five reference-audit tests/integrity, eight extracted TypeScript tracker checks and two exact compiled README examples pass.

### Contract and abstraction fit

A scoped feed accepts typed events or existing local/daemon notification envelopes. It validates matching terminal identity, retains unknown events, fails malformed known events and admits one caller-thread consumer. Immutable terminal/failure observations are separate from callback consumption. Ordinary failures drain the accepted prefix; feeding asynchronous exceptions raises them unchanged. Close retires feeding/waiting and drops undelivered frames without claiming remote completion, revoking credentials or owning caller subscriptions. The enclosing caller brackets its source. The optional cooperative deadline covers the action and callbacks; zero runs no caller setup and negative budgets fail validation.

Normal session submission, listener-before-send ordering, original `DroidError` mapping, outer timeout and invalidation remain with `Internal.Session`. Its existing STM lifetime check is passed into the shared collector. Tool-name enrichment remains a projection alongside the original event, not a mutation of payloads. A reported zero duration wins; absent duration uses monotonic time at terminal admission. Exact usage, extensions, structured output, message corrections/retractions and append-only text behavior remain in the shared accumulator.

The pure `StreamState`, scoped decoders, `stepStream`, `eventToolName` and `summarizeStream` replace mutable tracker-class construction. Manual summaries accept explicit reason/duration, preserve absent usage, use the latest observed usage when omitted and prefer an explicit override. This was completed after noticing that the wire-only feed could not represent the TypeScript helper's completion without usage. No zero counters or fake peer completion were introduced. `Internal.Output.adaptOutputData` factors the existing decoder for wire results and manual summaries; no second decoder or schema validator was added.

A native terminal receipt remains inspectable after later callback failure or scope timeout. Python's iterator timeout clears its result instead. The native action still fails and closes; retaining an immutable observation is not reporting successful consumption or remote rollback. This distinction is explicit in the README and parity matrix. Manual summaries are not wire receipts and do not weaken existing completion codecs.

### Tests and retained failures

`StreamSpec` registers **31 new cases**. They verify pre-consumption results; complete/partial filtering; all completion reasons; zero/reported/elapsed duration; session/turn filtering; empty IDs and required daemon routing; missing terminal IDs; late/duplicate terminal exclusion; malformed versus extension payloads; correction/retraction text; tool-name correlation; independent wire/local output outcomes; explicit-output precedence and decoder failure; first failure retention; fed async identity; callback failure; blocked-consumer cancellation; competing consumers; close/wakeup/idempotence; escaped handles; absent/zero/explicit pure usage; pure decoding/enrichment/artifact order; manual output decoding; zero/negative budgets; timeout/source cleanup and failure inspection; callback deadlines; simultaneous terminal/error/close admission; native dispatcher integration and unsubscribe/borrowed ownership; dispatcher failure; and redacted displays. Parameter variants live within these cases; case count is not a coverage target.

First execution began **13:46:12 UTC**, within ten minutes of the **13:36:49** start, and passed 192 existing local tests in **5.83s**. The changed shared-runtime check began **13:52:10** and passed 204 local/injected cases in **5.90s**. Initial standalone execution passed 26 cases. The review's coverage notes prompted timeout-inspection and simultaneous terminal/error/close checks. The race fixture initially failed compilation with **GHC-83865** because its local gate helper specialized to `IO Bool`; an explicit `IO a -> IO a` signature fixed the fixture.

The added timeout check then failed with **expected `Just (Just DroidStreamTimedOut)`, got `Nothing`**. Consumer cancellation had already closed the feed, so the scope owner's attempted error feed was ignored. The timeout owner now records the failure directly if no earlier terminal outcome exists, even after logical close (`src/Factory/Droid/Internal/Stream.hs:194–208`; regression `test/StreamSpec.hs:260`). The same assertion passes; it was not weakened. The simultaneous race check is at `test/StreamSpec.hs:283`. A provisional pure-output test only forced evaluation; before execution it was replaced by an explicit invalid-output assertion. Five HLint hints and local formatting differences were corrected without changing warning policies. The obsolete user-skill path lookup failed; the installed pi-subagents skill was read instead.

Eight source groups execute the fingerprint-verified TypeScript tracker: absent usage/zero duration; latest all-zero usage; explicit usage override; artifact filtering versus text; tool-name correlation; separate structured output; interruption/spec handoff; and first terminal error. No Factory module initialization, CLI, credential helper or live service was invoked. Native exact-number/extension guarantees are retained rather than copying JavaScript numeric rounding or dropped credit fields. Both `StreamFeedExample.hs` and `StreamSummaryExample.hs` match their README blocks and compile with `-fno-code -XGHC2024 -Wall -Werror`; neither was run.

## Legacy stream delivery

**Summary.** `hsdk-docs-package-signoff-ip3.2.2.3` implements separately selected Python low-level idle completion. `withDroidLegacyStream` uses the same result-indexed feed, accumulator, queue, consumer, deadline and close path as ordinary turns (`Internal.Stream.hs:181–384`). Its private completion policy cannot produce an explicit-turn result from an idle observation. The public `DroidIdleCompletion` carries only latest optional usage. Final macOS ARM64/GHC 9.10.3 checks passed **3,795 tests / 12.59s**, **15.62s** Cabal test and **9.44s** warning-free all-component build, full formatting/HLint, five audit tests and reference integrity. One new and two existing README examples compile and match exactly; none was run.

### Contract and verification

Initial Idle is ignored; any non-idle working state arms the first subsequent Idle. The terminating Idle follows accepted frames before consumption returns. Explicit completion events never terminate legacy iteration or supply its usage. Legacy selection admits assistant/thinking deltas, created tool uses, results/progress, usage, working-state transitions and errors. Ignored events do not alter correlation. No fabricated success, duration, reason, ACK or remote interrupt is exposed; normal turns still require exact terminal identity. Full precise usage and extensions remain intact, including absent and zero values.

The enclosing caller owns notification subscriptions and physical resources. The compiled example installs notification and close listeners before submission and releases both with brackets. Legacy observations have no turn identity: drain unrelated notifications and serialize work for the selected session. Error notifications are data; source failures are exceptions. Native known malformed payloads fail rather than adopting Python's silent skipping. Existing prefix/error, async identity, cooperative timeout, single-consumer and immutable-inspection guarantees remain shared.

`legacyStreamTests` registers sixteen checks (`test/StreamSpec.hs:334–508`); each asserts behavior rather than merely forcing a value:

| Check | Observable requirement |
| --- | --- |
| Every non-idle state | All five states arm completion; repeated initial Idle is excluded and missing usage stays absent. |
| Text without activity | Text does not arm the Idle boundary; the waiting scope expires. |
| Explicit completion | Missing/matching/foreign terminal IDs are ignored by legacy policy and do not supply usage. |
| Normal stream | Working-state transitions cannot replace a matching explicit completion. |
| Latest zero usage | Last all-zero counters/credits and exact extension values are retained. |
| First boundary | Later usage, activity and Idle cannot overwrite the admitted observation. |
| Session routing | Foreign daemon activity cannot arm a stream; explicit empty session IDs remain exact. |
| Legacy filtering | Created-tool names survive ignored raw/tool-call updates and enrich results/progress. |
| Malformed state | Invalid known state fails after the accepted text prefix, rather than silently hanging. |
| Source failure | The accepted prefix precedes the original exception; later Idle is not admitted. |
| Error notification | A reported error remains an event, without asserting a turn outcome. |
| Cancellation | The waiting consumer retains its asynchronous exception and releases its source bracket once. |
| Deadline inspection | The timeout cause remains observable after consumer cleanup. |
| Invalid budgets | Zero/negative budgets enter no caller setup and report their distinct errors. |
| Mixed families | Thinking, text, two tool uses, a result for the second tool, progress for the first, usage and Idle retain exact order and names. |
| Dispatcher ownership | Real in-memory RPC dispatch feeds the legacy response; scope exit unsubscribes without closing the borrowed channel. |

The source probe executes the pinned Python `receive_response` AST and original stream dataclasses through an explicitly isolated, already-converted-event fixture boundary. Six groups exercise initial Idle/order/absence, latest zero and terminal cutoff, immutable name backfill, unmapped conversion output, cancellation and early close; a separate structural check verifies that explicit completion has no converter branch. These are **iterator-control checks, not Pydantic/SDK codec or live execution**. Native notification fixtures exercise the actual Haskell decoder; the source probe imports no Factory module or credential helper.

### Full fess and limitations

The increment changes `Internal.Stream`, its public reexports, `StreamSpec` and test registration, plus required README/parity/development/handoff/tracker records. It adds no dependencies, reader, writer, connection owner or alternate collector. Existing source, assertions and unrelated uncommitted work remain. Checked categories found no production stubs/mocks, availability fallback, swallowed failures, warning suppression or invented completion. The intentional legacy event filter is source semantics, not error suppression; malformed known payloads still fail. The source probe's fixture boundary and compile-only examples are not interoperability evidence.

Retained diagnostics: Ormolu requested a parenthesis layout change; full HLint found **`Redundant void`** in the later mixed-family test. Both were corrected without suppression or assertion changes. The first broad suite passed **3,795 / 11.71s** before the lint failure; final results above are a separate receipt. Generalizing the feed initially left an overly broad unknown-event comment and README sentence; both now distinguish explicit and legacy policy. The post-closure administrative child check initially used issue-show's `id` instead of dependency-list's `issue_id` and failed with **`KeyError: 'id'`** before export. The actual JSON shape was inspected and the check corrected; all seven children and the unchanged parent were then verified. `tracker-first-failure.log` retains the diagnostic; SDK assertions and issue scope were not changed.

**Contract and tests.** The README records the complete five-kind table: deferred-after-interrupt is steering/non-reviewable; paused is queued/reviewable/1; daemon-discardable is steering/reviewable/0; daemon-end-of-loop is queued/reviewable/1; compaction-deferred is queued/non-reviewable. **Contracts and architecture.** The [public examples and projection table](../README.md#pure-content-and-inspection-helpers) and [parity mapping](parity.md#utility-contracts-and-native-equivalents) state each boundary. Content construction preserves supplied source order and data but does not replace normal attachment validation. Equality ignores only the top-level timestamp (`Schema.Notifications.hs:77–80`), without changing existing progress deduplication/deadline policy. Descriptive machine labels create no transport or authentication authority.

The null diff for this work is small public projections in the owning schemas, shared arithmetic/predicates in existing consumers, and direct native tests. The scoped diff has that shape. Typed wire models and raw read-only inspection have distinct contracts; the latter does not round-trip data through a permissive substitute model. Native `Exception`/`SomeException`, `toException`, `throwIO`, typed SDK failures and existing asynchronous-failure tests supply error conversion without arbitrary-value stringification.

## Saved-session file scanning delivery

**Summary.** `hsdk-f20.1` closed/verified offline at **20:14:19**, giving the saved-session parent **1/3** completed children; dashboard remains **17/45**. It provides the read-only file layer in `Factory.Droid.Discovery`, rather than treating daemon catalogs as local discovery. Public operations obtain the default root, scan a caller-selected directory, read one unarchived `.jsonl` file and read root favorites. `DroidSessionFile` retains exact filename/header/settings data, typed projections, physical line count and actual modification/status-change times. It does not fabricate a creation timestamp or pretend that cross-directory selection is complete. The [mapping](parity.md#saved-session-file-scanning) records the reconciled Python/TypeScript behavior; the [README example](../README.md#saved-session-file-observations) uses real public APIs.

**Ownership and abstraction review.** One private observed-file fold (`Discovery.hs:192–213`) uses the existing POSIX facilities and standard bracket ownership. It checks regular-file device/inode identity after nonblocking/CLOEXEC acquisition, reads 64 KiB chunks up to the opened file's observed size, and rejects premature EOF. It does not chase appends or retain later message bodies. Header/settings/favorites memory remains proportional to their observed content; there is no global cap or immutable multi-file snapshot. This prefix-counting contract differs from Input's strict attachment snapshot/size checks, so those owners were not replaced by a shared policy framework. No session runtime, watcher, process, credential provider or scheduling abstraction was added.

Stable symbolic links remain supported, including targets outside the selected directory. The supplied path is not a sandbox. Header IDs/cwd do not choose other files; settings are filename-derived. Only missing and permission-denied I/O is suppressed as expected discovery absence (`Discovery.hs:215–218`); other I/O errors and asynchronous exceptions propagate. Raw objects and standard exceptions remain sensitive despite the observation's redacted `Show`.

**Native verification.** The first `-Werror` library build began **19:37:14 UTC**, **14m22s** after the **19:22:52** start, and failed only on a shadowed local `parts` binding. Renaming that binding retained the diagnostic policy. The first registered focused run (**19:47:05–19:47:25**) passed **24 new tests / 0.14s**. The final all-component warning-free build took **10.48s**; the combined scanner/unchanged-attachment check passed **58 tests / 0.17s** (**3.72s** Cabal). Five additional complete scanner runs passed all 24 cases each (0.12/0.13/0.20/0.18/0.18s), exercising the asynchronous filesystem boundaries repeatedly. Later invalid-UTF-8 and split-multibyte-header additions passed all 24 cases again (**0.12s**, ending **20:07:17**). Changed-file Ormolu/HLint and `git diff --check` pass. The exact README example compiles with `-fno-code -XGHC2024 -Wall -Werror` and passes format/lint; it was not executed. Only macOS ARM64/GHC 9.10.3 execution is established.

**Meaning of all 24 tests** (`test/SavedSessionSpec.hs`):

| Case | Actual assertion |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | The default root is `.factory/sessions` beneath the platform's current home directory. |
| 2 | Direct scans select case-sensitive `.jsonl` entries in filename order without descending into project subdirectories. |
| 3 | Missing and non-directory roots produce no observations. |
| 4 | Filename identity, metadata, exact extensions and observed modification/change times survive parsing. |
| 5 | Empty filename-derived ID and empty title/owner/cwd values remain literal. |
| 6 | Malformed JSON, invalid UTF-8, absent required fields and a blank first line are rejected. |
| 7 | Nonblank legacy title overrides modern title unchanged, while blank/invalid legacy fields retain the appropriate fallback. |
| 8 | Invalid optional owner/cwd/mission values use tolerant projections without losing the original header. |
| 9 | Physical counting follows CR/space/tab rules, retains VT/FF records and counts non-JSON trailing lines. |
| 10 | Multibyte headers and line counts remain correct across multiple read chunks. |
| 11 | A header without a final newline has zero messages. |
| 12 | First mission metadata, including empty IDs, and complete settings extensions are retained. |
| 13 | Valid header role wins, and missing first mission metadata does not merge a later tag. |
| 14 | Missing/malformed/invalid optional settings do not remove an otherwise valid header. |
| 15 | Every tested truthy archive value hides the file despite invalid unrelated tags. |
| 16 | Null, false, zero and empty archive values do not hide the file. |
| 17 | Favorites retain only exact string IDs, including empty text, and tolerate missing/malformed files. |
| 18 | Missing entries, directories and FIFOs are not consumed as regular session streams. |
| 19 | Stable regular symlinks, including outside-directory targets, work; dangling links are skipped. |
| 20 | Permission behavior follows actual process privilege; this UID 501 run exercised denied reads, while a root run checks its privileged behavior rather than pretending to test denial. |
| 21 | A NUL-containing path raises the expected payload-free user I/O error rather than returning absence. |
| 22 | An active reader has CLOEXEC/nonblocking flags, preserves custom cancellation identity and leaves no descriptor for the file. |
| 23 | Truncation after the first read prevents publication of a partial observation. |
| 24 | Appended records beyond the captured file size do not enter the count. |

These fixtures use real temporary files, permissions, symlinks, FIFOs and sparse regular files. `/dev/fd`, descriptor stat identity and a positive read offset establish the in-flight boundary for cancellation/truncation/append tests; no internal reader is mocked and no sleep is treated as proof of admission. Stale descriptor errors during that test-only enumeration are ignored, not application failures. The raced pre-open replacement guard is reviewed by construction; no deterministic replacement-race execution is claimed.

**Reference evidence.** Seven groups execute extracted, fingerprint-verified TypeScript file helpers and their actual cached Zod schemas; five execute original Python `_parse_session`, `_favorites`, `SavedSession` and `ensure_aware` definitions from the pinned commit. All I/O is against fresh fixtures; neither SDK is initialized and no live service is called. Explicit differences cover legacy-title precedence, malformed optional fields, strict UTF-8 versus TypeScript replacement, archive handling when tags are invalid, and VT/FF counting. The file layer retains raw cwd while the Python high-level result resolves it. Native exact-number preservation is not a JavaScript precision claim. Creation-time semantics are not inferred from the source's aware-datetime assertion.

## Saved-session timestamp checkpoint

**Status and scope.** `hsdk-f20.3` remains **in progress/partial**: macOS execution is established; Linux bridge compilation/execution awaits a compiler image. No child or parent closure is claimed. `DroidSessionFile.sessionFileBirthTime` now records optional reported birth time, and `sessionFileCreatedAt` exposes the SDK-compatible status-change fallback only when birth is absent. The raw provenance remains observable. Both references distinguish their available birth field from a ctime fallback; Python's stat interface can omit birth data that Linux statx can provide. The [mapping](parity.md#birth-time-checkpoint) states the resulting native contract.

**Implementation and abstraction review.** The platform bridge uses Darwin `fstat` or Linux descriptor-only `statx`, with the returned `STATX_BTIME` bit checked before reading the value (`cbits/file-birth-time.c`). It reports absent support as zero status, not a zero timestamp; ENOSYS/EOPNOTSUPP are unavailable cases, while other errors retain errno. Darwin reports its native `st_birthtimespec` value, including zero. Types/layouts come from platform headers, not copied struct offsets. The installed `unix` interface neither supports this operation on macOS nor exposes the Linux returned mask needed for presence. The small bridge fills that specific gap; it is not a replacement filesystem library.

`Internal.FileTime.fileBirthTime` uses safe FFI, retries EINTR through the standard errno helper, and converts signed 64-bit seconds/nanoseconds directly to POSIX/UTC time without floating-point rounding. The existing regular-file fold takes an explicit metadata observer after acquisition/identity checks and before streaming; only primary session files request birth data. Settings/favorites retain a unit observer. No path is reopened, descriptor lifetime extended or other state owner introduced. A compatibility ctime projection does not relabel the optional birth field.

**Execution evidence.** Work began **20:24:40 UTC**. The fresh unsupported-API probe ran **20:30:45–20:30:47**, reporting `haveStatx=False` and `getExtendedFileStatus: unsupported operation (Operation is not supported)`; this was not concealed with a ctime default on macOS. The native C probe passed **20:37:09**, matching Node bigint birth nanoseconds exactly and retaining EBADF for an invalid descriptor. The linked Haskell/API conversion independently matched the actual Node value at **20:53:05**. A separate probe compiling the actual internal Haskell source and C bridge verified Haskell EBADF propagation at **21:03:47**; it is a component probe, not a second implementation.

All 24 prior scanner cases passed after integration (**0.13s**, **20:41:48**). Four new tests then passed with them (**28 / 0.35s**):
- Reported birth availability is explicit; the macOS test requires the native field, while unavailable platforms verify the labelled compatibility projection.
- Changing file metadata advances status-change time without replacing a reported birth timestamp.
- Pure projection checks preserve present zero, negative, fractional and large values; absence alone selects ctime. These injected record values test projection, not the C conversion.
- Path replacement after reading starts retains the original inode's title and timestamp observation, rather than querying the replacement path.

At the native-ABI integration boundary, **3,858 tests passed / 12.88s** (**17.34s** Cabal), including all 24 scanner and four timestamp cases. The warning-free all-component build took **15.15s**; full-tree formatting/HLint, five audit tests and reference integrity passed, ending **21:00:16**. C also compiled independently with `-std=c11 -Wall -Wextra -Werror -pedantic`. Cabal's source list contains both the C bridge and internal Haskell module. Source inclusion is not an independently built/tested distribution; other GHC/platform jobs and live interoperability remain uncertified.

**Linux evidence boundary.** Read-only context inspection established an existing local Unix-socket Docker daemon running Linux/aarch64. Its sole cached image, `ghcr.io/c4illin/convertx:latest` (`b515b04bfd25`), was inspected using an ephemeral, read-only, capability-dropped, network-disabled `/bin/sh`, without mounting source or launching the application. It reported Linux `7.0.12-linuxkit aarch64`, but no cc/gcc/clang; no suitable host Zig compiler was available either. This establishes a local Linux runtime, not bridge execution. Permission to download an official GCC image was requested; no image was pulled and no compiler/SDK execution was invented. Linux mask/absence/error branches therefore remain unverified despite the documented [statx API](https://www.man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/statx.2.html).

## Saved-session selection delivery

**Summary.** `hsdk-f20.2` closed/verified offline at **22:14:51**, giving the saved-session parent **2/3** completed children; dashboard remains **17/45**. It implements `listDroidSessions` in `Factory.Droid.Discovery` and re-exports it through `Factory.Droid`. `ListDroidSessionsOptions` covers caller/default root and cwd, outside-cwd scanning and optional limits. `DroidSavedSession` wraps the complete file observation, adding only normalized cwd and favorite membership; metadata, raw values and birth-time provenance are not rebuilt or discarded. The [selection mapping](parity.md#saved-session-selection) and [compiled facade example](../README.md#saved-session-file-observations) state the public contract. The Linux timestamp check remains a separate, unresolved `.3` requirement; this is not saved-session parent acceptance.

**Architecture and reconciliation.** Existing directory/file readers and descriptor ownership are reused. Cwd is captured once; both canonical filesystem and lexical path meanings are retained because the pinned SDKs generate different keys and legacy matches for symlinks, missing suffixes and backslashes. The algorithm selects those defined layouts rather than sniffing SDK identity or inventing a workspace cache. Root favorites are read once. Legacy filtering precedes exact newest-ID selection; modification time sorts descending, then code-point ID/path order resolves ties. Final limits cannot truncate away a unique result behind duplicates. Zero avoids filesystem work after validation; negative limits reject rather than adopting TypeScript's unbounded interpretation. This is a read-only query, not a restore, trust, session-load or execution operation.

**Native checks.** Work began **21:17:56 UTC**. Reused-source/API reconciliation ended before implementation at **21:29:31**; the first changed build/check (**21:31:56–21:32:49**) passed **28 existing scanner cases / 0.34s**, within thirty minutes. The first 23 selection cases exposed a real bug: a file supplied as the root caused `file/.favorites: getFileStatus: inappropriate type (Not a directory)` rather than no sessions. A root directory check now precedes all descendant reads, in both selected/outside modes. The same 23 cases passed **0.15s** by **21:47:43**, with the failed receipt retained.

A reviewer identified insufficient proof that zero limit avoids I/O. A new regression uses a self-referential symlink root: zero returns empty, a positive-limit control actually fails on the root, and zero with a NUL option still fails validation. All **24 selection cases passed with two Tasty workers / 0.10s**. Final full integration passed **3,882 tests / 12.74s**, **16.13s** Cabal, warning-free all-component build **13.11s**, full-tree Ormolu/HLint, five audit tests and reference integrity (**22:01:25–22:03:22**). The explicit formatter list covers **162 Haskell files**. Three exact file/timestamp/listing README examples compile together; the new example also passes format/lint. Only macOS ARM64/GHC 9.10.3 execution is established.

**What each of the 24 new cases verifies** (`test/SavedSessionSelectionSpec.hs`):

| Case | Actual assertion |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | Facade listing combines project/legacy records, retains observations and obtains favorites only from the root. |
| 2 | Legacy cwd is filtered while selected-project stored cwd remains descriptive. |
| 3 | Outside-cwd mode includes root files and one level of prefixed directories, not ordinary or nested directories. |
| 4 | The newest duplicate wins with exact original metadata/settings preserved. |
| 5 | Same-time duplicate IDs choose a deterministic path. |
| 6 | Same-time IDs use code-point order, including BMP/astral values that differ under UTF-16 ordering. |
| 7 | Sub-millisecond modification differences are not rounded away. |
| 8 | A newer foreign-workspace duplicate cannot mask an eligible selected observation. |
| 9 | Archived/malformed newer copies do not hide a valid older copy. |
| 10 | A unique record beyond more than twenty duplicate candidates still satisfies the final limit. |
| 11 | Omitted, zero, finite and large limits yield the correct sorted prefix. |
| 12 | Zero performs no root lookup, positive-limit control does, and zero does not bypass NUL validation. |
| 13 | Negative limits fail before path checks, and invalid cwd/root options do not normalize into usable paths. |
| 14 | Missing roots and roots that are files yield no sessions in both workspace modes. |
| 15 | Default home-root/current-cwd behavior works in an isolated native self-peer. |
| 16 | Relative root and empty cwd resolve against the captured process cwd. |
| 17 | An explicit empty root does not select the default home root. |
| 18 | Tilde options are literal relative paths rather than implicit home expansion. |
| 19 | Canonical legacy matching recognizes a symlink alias while retaining the resolved view. |
| 20 | Missing cwd suffixes discover both stored-directory conventions. |
| 21 | Parent segments across symlinks discover both baselined interpretations. |
| 22 | Backslash key variants, root-workspace spelling and empty filename ID remain supported. |
| 23 | NUL stored cwd cannot match or normalize, while the original raw value remains inspectable. |
| 24 | Listing cancellation preserves the original exception and closes the active file descriptor. |

Default/relative/empty/tilde cases execute the actual SDK through a native self-peer with explicit cwd and a minimal HOME-only environment. Parent-global HOME/cwd is not mutated; the peer is not a Factory process. Existing temporary-file and descriptor helpers are shared from `SavedSessionSpec`, rather than replacing the reader with mocks. The NUL filesystem-call avoidance is additionally checked by source inspection; no general I/O tracer is claimed.

**Same-fixture reference evidence.** Ten TypeScript groups execute the original async `listSessions` and file/schema helpers. Ten Python groups execute the original public async `list_sessions`, thread dispatch, parser and dataclass, replacing only its default-root provider with the fixture root because Python has no sessionsDir option. The linked native API then passes those same ten filesystem scenarios. The probes demonstrate—not merely assume—the path-convention differences, negative-limit difference, root-file behavior, favorites and TypeScript's duplicate-oversampling shortfall. Native union results and complete limiting are asserted explicitly. No SDK module initialization, credentials or live service is involved; no synthetic backend replaces filesystem behavior.

**Full fess.** `selection-first.log` retains the root-file failure; production validation was fixed without weakening the expectation or widening exception suppression. Reviewer `4f6daac0-330b-43dc-ae5d-de3f02358f3e` reported the zero-I/O coverage gap as P2; the new failing-root/positive-control test addresses it. Workflow `41fd82cd-e5ef-4130-9303-fc9525d9768a` completed successfully. This was static secondary review without a history sentinel or reviewer execution; the final regression was self-reviewed and executed by the parent. One HLint composition hint was corrected with standard `(>=>)`; no rule or assertion was suppressed.

The scoped before-copy/source/test/registration diff was reviewed. Checked categories found no production stub, fixture substitution, vacuous shape-only test, credential loading, swallowed asynchronous error, availability-based implementation fallback, second file owner, speculative SDK hierarchy or unrelated runtime change. Lexical path normalization and the two directory-key encodings represent demonstrated source semantics, not identity-based dispatch. Optional path metadata remains descriptive; no filesystem containment, immutable snapshot or hard kernel-I/O deadline is promised. Nonzero queries may read every candidate to preserve complete deduplication/limit behavior. Only Discovery/facade code, tests/peer registration and required documentation/tracker records changed.

## Observability integration

Logging, metrics, tracing, shared-path instrumentation and privacy/failure handling were completed as one batch under `hsdk-docs-package-signoff-ip3.3.1`: all five children and parent closed **23:38:12**, dashboard **18/45**. The goal and hierarchy were not revised. `Factory.Droid.Observability` provides optional typed sinks, scalar-attribute normalization, explicit serialized errors, immutable trace context and shared failure isolation. Transport decoration retains the I/O owner and in/out/out_failed records without default payloads. RPC tracing merges only present fields and rechecks physical write admission after provider I/O. Request/remote-error, connection and process paths use existing owners, exact monotonic duration/counter events and no mandatory backend.

The [README](../README.md#observability) documents explicit local/borrowed/daemon configuration, timing meanings, reentrancy constraints and privacy boundaries. Local telemetry uses dedicated entry points rather than breaking comparable session settings or overloading mutable user-interaction handlers. Daemon options carry the same observation configuration. Scope/operation outcomes say returned/failed, not remote success. No automatic exception-body, argv, credential, stderr, environment or trace-value serialization is introduced; caller-supplied messages/attributes/errors/renderers remain explicit sensitive-data surfaces.

Verification: **22 new observability cases** exercise scalar/exact/empty data, sink/provider failures, standard async exceptions, primary-error/cleanup preservation, transport directions, real local/daemon paths, trace merge/revalidation, timing, remote-error counters and credential/payload non-disclosure. Existing RPC/hook checks passed with them (**75 focused / 0.38s**); the final observability/mission check passed **32 / 0.07s**. Final integration: **3,904 tests / 13.25s**, **24.19s** Cabal test; warning-free all-component build and full-tree formatting/HLint passed. The compiled example is not a live run.

Compact fess: the first test build omitted `sendPrompt`'s callback, then used an incorrect fixture expectation; both were corrected against the existing API/fixture. A later integration run exposed a pre-existing cancellation-peer race: its WebSocket handler caught framed close errors but not plain TCP broken pipe. The fixture now recognizes only resource-vanished I/O as the same expected disconnect; other errors and all cancellation/publication assertions remain. Failed receipts are retained. Remote error responses now receive a separate payload-free log/counter rather than being confused with successful operations. Formatting/lint diagnostics were fixed without suppression. Shared paths were self-reviewed; no new reviewer, source inventory, goal revision or per-helper report cycle was run. Reference: pinned TS node.mjs:438–767,2358–2504,2677–2732 and declarations :4540–4579; Python logging sites.

Evidence is in `/tmp/droid-structured-logging.jg1S2E7F/` (failed/focused/batch build-test logs and final tooling), with behavioral assertions in `test/LoggingSpec.hs`. Verification remains macOS ARM64/GHC 9.10.3, not Linux, live interoperability or an independently tested current sdist. Linux timestamp compiler-image approval and the other existing release gates remain open. No commits, publication or live service calls occurred.

## Native hosted validator integration

`hsdk-zpf` now uses an owned native worker rather than the rejected safe-FFI boundary or the restricted Haskell validator. `MCP.Validator` passes exact schema/value JSON over bounded stdin/stdout to the locked Rust `jsonschema` 0.55.1 engine. Each job uses the existing process owner, an empty environment, closed unrelated descriptors and closed stderr. Definition compilation precedes server publication; input and successful structured output use the same validator. Invalid instances remain tool errors, infrastructure failures remain explicit, and error results retain their existing output-schema exemption. The [README](../README.md#hosted-haskell-tools) covers installation, configuration and the pure-construction/IO-compilation distinction. Local/embedded resources are supported; implicit HTTP/file retrieval remains disabled and formats remain annotations.

Self-review reproduced a dependency failure that the initial semantic vectors missed: `^(a|aa)+\1b|a+$` rejected 28 `a` characters, while its negation incorrectly accepted them after the backend exhausted its backtracking counter. The worker now relies on its owned wall-clock deadline instead of that counter. A narrowly modified, MIT-licensed copy of the dependency terminates on remaining regex engine failures, and the worker terminates on panics; errors cannot be inverted into success. The engine itself still supplies all regex/schema semantics. [VENDOR.md](../native/schema-validator/VENDOR.md) records the original archive hash, sole source patch and restriction to this one-request process. No reference checkout or upstream service was modified.

Verification: **31 focused hosted/worker checks /8.54s**, then **3,917 tests /20.85s** in the full suite (**24.02s** Cabal); warning-free all-component build **6.81s**, full formatting/HLint and five audit tests/reference integrity passed. The batch adds **13 tests** to the preceding 3,904-test boundary and revises two restricted-profile expectations to verify the formerly rejected semantics. The exact updated README example compiles with GHC2024/Wall/Werror without contacting Factory. Three Rust tests pass; locked offline Cargo installation produces a working standalone binary, and its embedded notices match the shipped file byte-for-byte.

The ten native-worker tests verify, respectively: compilation versus instance invalidity; exact decimal/large-number/regex/NUL/evaluated-property/kind pairs; escaped and percent-encoded references; refusal of external retrieval; missing-worker/zero/negative configuration errors; hostile compilation timeout and reaping; original `AsyncCancelled` identity and reaping; valid backtracking and its negation; actual regex-execution timeout and reaping; and engine exhaustion as failure even under negation. Both deadline cases require an actually executed worker and finish within two seconds with a 500ms budget. The five changed hosted cases verify exact pointer selection, ref siblings/evaluated fields, invalid-definition rejection before publication, actual HTTP input/output enforcement with handler nonexecution on invalid input, and explicit missing-worker failure. Existing authentication, raw/typed/rich-result, ownership, concurrent close, handler-finalizer and session-replacement cases remain intact.

Compact full fess: no replacement schema walker, mocked successful validator, silent fallback, suppression or scope expansion was introduced. The exact `Object`/unconstrained-object-schema check is valid by construction, independent of worker availability; server definition compilation still checks deployment. Ordinary handler failures retain sanitized tool errors; validator errors and async exceptions are not swallowed. Failed receipts include an initial 100ms timeout that preceded exec, an overly narrow test selector, missing test import/format/lint mistakes, a license-checker regex that crossed line boundaries, and the reproduced backend false results. All were corrected without lowering assertions. The first 3,917-test run also failed opening the unnecessary `/dev/null` sink; five unchanged focused reruns did not reproduce that OS error. The worker now uses the existing closed-stderr process API instead, and the final full suite passes. The transient OS cause was not established.

Licensing: the former Haskell `jsonschema-0.3.0.1` is absent from the current Cabal plan; its conflicting declarations remain historical evidence, not a permission claim. `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.txt` covers all **65** resolved dependency/version pairs across four macOS/Linux target graphs. Two workspace crates inherit the JSONSchema MIT license; the SIMD notice is pinned to the commit in the published crate metadata. Source copying and the narrow patch retain the MIT notice. No outreach was required. Graph coverage is not platform execution.

The unpacked source archive independently builds the worker in **16.68s** and the warning-free Haskell package, then passes **3,917 tests /21.80s** (**25.31s** Cabal) using that worker; its three Rust tests and reference/attachment checks also pass. The initial Haskell build reached test module90/94 before the 240s tool timeout; no compiler remained active, and the resumed build completed in18.01s. Archive SHA256: `a8d2568d3ad44ec5bda5ff2f6be630be55a7ad7ae57f3468b2269ae31b83f326`. **366** source/test/schema/native/manifest files and the resolved Cabal package/version sets match the working tree. Later handoff/acceptance/NOTICE edits are not in that archive. The equality checker initially assumed `cabal.project` was an archive member; it is development configuration, and the corrected check compares the actual manifest and resolved dependencies. Haddock and `cabal check` complete, retaining known dependency/Generic-link and missing-source-repository advisories.

This is self-review and parent execution, not an independent reviewer attestation. Only macOS ARM64/GHC 9.10.3/Rust 1.98.1 execution is established.
