## Toolchain policy

The development shell uses **GHC 9.12.4**, Cabal3.16.1.0, Ormolu0.8.0.2 and HLint3.10 from nixpkgs revision `5545adfad2e98de106a5544ca7067e03010410bd`. The default and `ghc912` shells use the same bare compiler; Cabal resolves dependencies against the existing pinned index-state. Consumers use the declared library bounds rather than a compiler-specific freeze file.

On 2026-09-13 the user selected stable **GHC9.12** instead of the former9.10/9.12/9.14 proposal. SDK CI now checks9.12.4 on macOS/Linux, and `base >=4.21 && <4.22` records the supported compiler series without a dependency-bound waiver. The lint job also selects9.12.4; its pinned tools resolve separately with `--ignore-project` and the same index-state. A local offline dry-run verifies that solver plan, not a fresh build of the tools or an executed GitHub job. Historical compiler receipts below are not current release certification.

Use `GHC2024`, explicit public export lists, ordinary `IO`, algebraic data types and established libraries. Aeson supplies JSON instances; `time` supplies Gregorian validation. The JSONL channel uses `process`, `unix` and `bytestring`; request correlation and scoped callback dispatch reuse `async`, `stm` and `containers`, within the Cabal bounds. Transport ownership remains explicit; no general transport class is added. The scope is recorded in [the parity inventory](parity.md).

The foundation choices were checked against the [GHC release index](https://www.haskell.org/ghc/index.html), [GHC edition documentation](https://downloads.haskell.org/ghc/9.10.2/docs/users_guide/exts/control.html), [Cabal documentation](https://cabal.readthedocs.io/), and the maintained [Aeson](https://hackage.haskell.org/package/aeson) and [typed-process](https://hackage.haskell.org/package/typed-process) packages. Release facts were consulted on 2026-09-05; they are not assertions of indefinite compiler support.

## Local checks

Enter the development shell, or provide the corresponding tools through an existing Haskell installation. Without the shell, install the native worker as described in the README and make its executable available on `PATH`:

```sh
nix develop
cargo fmt --manifest-path native/schema-validator/Cargo.toml -- --check
cargo test --locked --release --manifest-path native/schema-validator/Cargo.toml
cabal build all --ghc-options=-Werror
cabal test all --test-show-details=direct
python3 scripts/check_attachment_files.py
cabal haddock all
python3 scripts/check_haddock_urls.py dist-newstyle
cabal check
find src test examples -name '*.hs' -exec ormolu --mode check {} +
hlint src test examples
python3 scripts/reference_schemas.py
python3 -m unittest discover -s scripts -p 'test_*.py' -v
git diff --check
```

Use `nix develop path:.` while the new flake remains untracked. No files need to be staged merely to obtain the development shell.

The library enables warnings but does not impose `-Werror` on downstream consumers. Development and CI builds add it explicitly. Ormolu is the sole Haskell formatter. HLint checks are not disabled to conceal defects.

The package-scoped Haddock settings in `cabal.project` use versioned Hackage dependency URLs and include internal-module pages needed by generated links. Those implementation modules remain outside the public Haskell API. The project file is included in source distributions for standalone verification; dependency consumers retain their own project configuration. Pass the actual build directory to `check_haddock_urls.py` when using a custom `--builddir`. The check rejects unresolved URL templates and missing package-local files, not missing anchors or unavailable external sites.

## Source distribution

The source distribution includes schema fixtures, provenance, documentation, audit scripts and the native worker with its lockfile, worker-local dependency and notices. Verify an unpacked distribution as well as the working tree:

```sh
cabal sdist
scratch=$(mktemp -d)
tar -xzf dist-newstyle/sdist/droid-sdk-0.1.0.0.tar.gz -C "$scratch"
(
  cd "$scratch/droid-sdk-0.1.0.0"
  cargo build --locked --release --manifest-path native/schema-validator/Cargo.toml
  export PATH="$PWD/native/schema-validator/target/release:$PATH"
  cabal build all --enable-tests --ghc-options=-Werror
  cabal test all --enable-tests --test-show-details=direct
  python3 scripts/reference_schemas.py
)
```

Retain the temporary directory when investigating a failure. Distribution validation is independent of any planned release; no upload, commit or push is implied.

## Conformance evidence

The enum tests compare every constructor with the corresponding literal in the unmodified shared schema, reject unknown values and incorrect JSON types, and check `toJSON`, `toEncoding` and decoding consistency. Schema-derived golden checks are required in addition to round trips, because mutually incorrect encoders and decoders can agree with each other.

Model tests in `test/ModelsSpec.hs` check every declared catalog field, both availability branches, required fields, null rejection, defaults, precise numeric values, extension preservation and reserved-key precedence. Fallback metadata and mission model settings retain enum types and distinguish omitted flags from explicit false.

Token-usage tests in `test/UsageSpec.hs` check both cumulative and last-call shapes against their schema field lists, including both occurrences of the inline last-call definition. Golden fixtures and numeric properties cover precise decimals, large integers, required fields, omission, type rejection and extensions.

Content tests in `test/ContentSpec.hs` cover all seven content variants, both document-source variants and the restricted tool-result array. Full/minimal goldens, schema field/literal checks and negative cases cover typed script context, MIME types, required signatures, nonnegative durations and extension precedence. Cache tests exercise every block variant through the same serializers and confirm that nested tool-result items retain their ordinary schema.

Session metadata tests in `test/SessionSpec.hs` cover identifier parameters, nonempty tags, optional string-valued metadata, worktree metadata, saved-message snapshots, sandbox reports and tool overrides. Worktree-reference checks include every Unicode scalar value against a JavaScript-derived exclusion oracle, plus length and structural cases; U+2028/U+2029 handling was corrected after that sweep exposed a mismatch. The test suite itself remains Haskell-only.

Message tests in `test/MessagesSpec.hs` cover all 37 fields shared by ordinary and cached messages, typed hook records, inline enums, nullable phases and exact numeric values. RPC tests in `test/RPCSpec.hs` distinguish absent payloads from explicit null, require nullable failure IDs to be present, and preserve the broad base-response shapes rather than infer success from a schema name.

Tool tests in `test/ToolsSpec.hs` compare full/minimal objects and enums with the supplied schema, enforce nonempty successful patch results, preserve both file-path spellings, and exercise nested diff coordinates, string arrays and precise numeric options. Open, non-nullable record checks are shared with content tests through `test/SchemaTest.hs`. No tool is invoked by these tests.

Script tests in `test/ScriptSpec.hs` compare all six schema alternatives with full/minimal goldens, reject unknown fields and invalid result-location combinations, and exercise nonempty identifiers and task lists. They also verify that the recursive script-value schema is exactly Aeson's JSON `Value` domain. Common result tests preserve acknowledgement literals, Boolean success flags and open empty-object fields.

Source tests in `test/SourcesSpec.hs` cover all 16 provenance variants, branch-specific reserved fields, absent/null/present metadata, and all diagnostic text limits with ASCII and non-BMP characters. A separate local GHC check confirmed that `coerce` cannot change a `BoundedText 100` into `BoundedText 32`; same-bound coercion compiled.

Host tests in `test/HostSpec.hs` cover UUID syntax and spelling preservation, exact version literals, required nonempty registration IDs, precise timestamps and extensions. Model tests compare every supplied compaction selector and custom-selector regex edge case; Bedrock request metadata remains a typed string map.

Core notification tests in `test/NotificationsSpec.hs` exercise text, turn, usage, state, title, retraction, directory, compaction, structured-output, error and child-session payloads. They validate nested message/usage records and required nullable fields, and keep idle-state events separate from explicit turn completion. The existing Aeson enum-options helper is shared rather than duplicated.

Tool/hook notification tests in `test/ToolNotificationsSpec.hs` cover shared tool-result and hook-output serialization, full/minimal fields, progress and retry enums, permission selections and batched IDs, and the distinct started/completed hook contracts. The tests validate data only; no permission changes, commands or retry actions are performed.

Loop tests in `test/LoopSpec.hs` validate inclusive interval bounds, integral/nonnegative counters, required nullable next-run timestamps and open extensions. Session-control tests in `test/ControlSpec.hs` cover 24 parameter/result definitions, including ordered text/image user content, user-only append constraints, queue variants, fork tag differences and typed rewind lists. These codecs do not perform the operations they describe.

Interaction tests in `test/InteractionSpec.hs` cover all eleven tool-confirmation detail alternatives, questionnaires, collected answers and batched permission requests. They reuse tool-use/outcome/patch-operation codecs, verify branch-specific extensions and nested record validation, and preserve independent outer/inner discriminators without treating parsing as a permission decision. Permission-result refinements still require authoritative version-matched source.

Context tests in `test/ContextSpec.hs` exercise aggregate and detailed snapshots, exact numeric values and the shared skill/droid entry shape with distinct location domains. Discovery tests in `test/DiscoverySpec.hs` exercise catalogs, command metadata, skill resources, disabling-source variants, editable settings levels and identity metadata. The existing interaction enum checks now share `enumSchemaTest` with these tests.

MCP tests in `test/MCPSpec.hs` cover registry/tool/status data, stdio/header wire bodies, authentication notifications and name/remove/toggle/submission parameters. They check typed input-schema containers, exact counts, intrinsic user settings, shared listing/notification encoding, default annotations without normalization and redacted record `Show` instances. They do not perform authentication, spawn servers, validate operational HTTP headers or exercise network clients.

Mission tests in `test/MissionSpec.hs` cover features, handoff/verification/test/feedback reports, worker and subagent summaries, and five independent notifications. They preserve nullable worker identifiers, exact numeric domains and untrusted report content without executing commands or treating reported status as proof. Runtime-normalized worker progress and dependent snapshots remain unimplemented.

Automation tests in `test/AutomationSpec.hs` cover list/read/delete wire inputs, shared read/delete structure, execution locations and nonempty IDs without applying older input trimming. They perform no scheduling, lookup or mutation.

Daemon workspace tests in `test/DaemonWorkspaceSpec.hs` cover trust reports, file-read/write/list/search bodies, session-scoped transfers, directory changes and setup progress. They exercise redacted records, declared integer/number distinctions, default annotations, independent trust flags and shared directory-validation shapes. `test/Main.hs` now loads the daemon schema once alongside the shared/local snapshots.

Terminal tests in `test/TerminalSpec.hs` cover base/session-scoped bodies, creation outcomes, required nullable PIDs, screen state and the complete data/exit notification union. `test/TimestampSpec.hs` checks RFC 3339 syntax/calendar boundaries, offsets, leap-second position, lowercase separators and unbounded fractional spelling; historical/announced leap-second occurrence validation remains tracked in `hsdk-w8y`. No PTY or terminal process is exercised by these codecs.

Worktree tests in `test/WorktreeSpec.hs` cover closed management/file-content inputs and open profile/managed-worktree/cleanup/archive reports. They test scalar reuse, UUID spelling, Unicode limits, integer/number distinctions and unknown-field rejection. The common record-shape tests now serve both open and closed records without removing previous checks.

`test/ProcessSpec.hs` provides thirteen offline process-exchange checks. A private `--jsonl-peer` mode in the test executable provides a real child process, with a test-only PID handshake followed by protocol 1.205.0 fixtures. Checks cover acknowledgement, streamed deltas, explicit completion, byte-split UTF-8/coalesced frames, stderr backpressure, CRLF, malformed/non-object/invalid-UTF-8 frames, byte limits, EOF/truncation, callback failure and cancellation. Shutdown checks cover cooperative exit, ignored SIGTERM, nonpositive grace, natural-exit races, full-pipe writes and cancellation during the grace period. Each launched peer is checked for termination and reaping.

`withJsonLinesProcess limit grace config action` takes the frame-byte limit and then the SIGTERM grace in microseconds. It owns its pipes and child process, overrides configured stdio, and preserves the caller's other process settings. Cleanup sends SIGTERM, polls for exit during positive grace, then sends SIGKILL if the owned PID remains and reaps it before closing the pipes. Cancellation during grace also takes the force-and-reap path; asynchronous exceptions are deferred during that final step. There is no absolute kernel-exit deadline or descendant management. Callers must finish their send/receive threads before leaving the callback. Stderr is discarded and failures expose only fixed categories; framing/I/O errors must end the exchange. The low-level transport does not choose Droid arguments, attribution, authentication, retries or exit-code classification; `Factory.Droid` supplies scoped local startup separately.

The resistant-child regression failed against the preceding checkpoint after 5.02 seconds. A second regression, using repeated small frames rather than a single large write, exposed a buffered stdin flush that blocked `hClose` before escalation and replaced cancellation with a cleanup failure. Termination now precedes pipe closure, and `process`'s existing `ignoreSigPipe` helper suppresses only EPIPE when closing that terminated child's stdin. Unbuffered mode alone did not resolve the failure.

A five-second self-kill watchdog prevents leaked fixture processes when cleanup is broken; a separate two-second assertion prevents that watchdog from being mistaken for successful parent cleanup. The final thirteen-test group, including small and large full-pipe writes, passed thirty consecutive runs with four test workers and two GHC capabilities.

Repeated tests exposed an EOF cleanup race with the initially evaluated `typed-process` 0.2.13.0: cancellation of its exit waiter was followed by a second `waitForProcess` reporting no child. The final implementation uses one reaping path through `process`, without suppressing the failure. Thirty consecutive focused runs passed after this change.

`test/MetadataSpec.hs` adds 43 schema-backed checks for SDK metadata, version syntax, attribution branches, extension preservation and trace fields. `test/EnvelopeSpec.hs` adds 81 checks for complete envelopes, schema intersections, typed results, nullable IDs and overlapping response unions. The original process transcript now decodes full envelopes rather than only bare bodies.

`test/ProtocolSpec.hs` provides 26 channel/result checks. A native peer interleaves notifications, a server request and reversed replies; deterministic in-memory peers cover duplicate IDs, remote errors, missing payloads, malformed envelopes, EOF, deadlines, cancellation, writer backpressure and scope cleanup. Typed result tests distinguish absent, null and invalid payloads, preserve remote error precedence and verify that local decoding failure leaves the channel usable. IDs are supplied by the caller and must remain unique for the connection. The event queue has one consumer and is currently unbounded; slow-consumer overflow policy is not inferred from the reference SDKs.

Review identified two terminal-state defects in the channel: post-poison frames could still enter the event queue, and reader failure could be mislabeled as write failure during sender startup. Both regressions failed before the fixes. Dispatch checks terminal state in the same STM transaction as delivery; send-error handling preserves the recorded terminal cause without blocking cancellation.

`test/DispatchSpec.hs` adds 16 checks for the opt-in generic dispatcher: native callbacks with concurrent requests, ordered listener snapshots, idempotent removal, successor-safe handler replacement, in-flight snapshots, duplicate IDs, response identity/context, reentrant requests, redacted handler failure, terminal replay and owned-worker cleanup. Sibling-finalizer tests require concurrent cancellation rather than sequential cancellation that could deadlock. An independent read-only implementation review found no issues. All 55 process/protocol/dispatcher checks passed thirty consecutive runs with four test workers and two GHC capabilities.

`test/ClientSpec.hs` adds 130 checks for 31 statically defined local operations and 45 named request/response aliases. Every binding is checked against its schema method/response links, required parameters, result decoding, error precedence and exact outbound request. A native peer verifies model discovery and directory-change messages. Tests also protect caller metadata/extensions, omitted protocol signals, explicit deadlines and redacted configuration. All 185 process/protocol/dispatcher/client checks passed twenty consecutive parallel runs.

`MethodRequest` uses nominal method and parameter roles. Compiler probes at `/tmp/droid-method-roles.jLHLsn` accept same-type coercion and reject both method substitution and coercible parameter-wrapper substitution. These are compiler checks, not merely JSON round trips. Independent review found no issues in the new aliases and operation bindings.

Wire coverage comprises 93 shared, 177 local and 47 daemon definitions, plus inline types. Broader schema and SDK parity remain backlog rather than requirements for the local workflow.

Offline tests must not contact Factory services or require credentials. Live CLI/daemon checks, external mutations and cost-bearing requests require explicit authorization. Unavailable platform or live evidence must be reported as unavailable, not counted as passing.

## Historical lower-level results

Verified locally on Apple Silicon macOS with GHC 9.10.3, Aeson 2.2.4.1 and time 1.12.2:

- Library and tests compile with `-Werror`; all 2,680 Haskell checks pass, including 185 process/protocol/dispatcher/client checks.
- Five reference-audit regression checks pass.
- Ormolu and HLint pass; actionlint validates the CI configuration.
- An unpacked source distribution builds and passes both test suites.
- The README expressions produce the documented values in GHCi.
- Haddock covers the current public modules. It also warns about unavailable dependency documentation (`vector`) and generated `Generic` representation links; these warnings have not been suppressed.
- `cabal check` succeeds with a source-repository metadata warning. This worktree has no configured remote, so no repository URL has been invented.

These results do not establish Linux execution, the additional compiler configurations, or live Droid compatibility.

## Owned child IPC implementation and fess checkpoint

The launch-control work now provides an owned JSONL IPC channel beside ordinary stdin/stdout. `withJsonLinesProcessIpc` uses the existing JSONL implementation, process reaper and borrowed session constructor; the C-only `factory-droid-launcher` performs a private descriptor handoff and execs the selected target under the same PID. There is no persistent broker or second session runtime. This is implementation and macOS evidence, not final platform acceptance of `hsdk-docs-package-signoff-ip3.1.1.2`.

### Contract and implementation

The baselined TypeScript `enableIpc` path retains stdin/stdout/stderr and adds an IPC descriptor (`node.mjs` 3049–3059, 3110; `node.d.ts` 196). The Haskell scoped constructor expresses that capability without copying the JavaScript transport class. Explicit launch preparation retains the existing ordinary → sanitizer → trusted environment ordering. The helper reserves fd 3 and the two Node channel variables; the IPC stream otherwise carries caller-selected JSON objects, not invented Factory methods.

On macOS, a child-only Mach bootstrap handoff transfers fileports and restores the original bootstrap before target exec. `fileport_makefd` publishes descriptors with CLOEXEC under the kernel FD-table lock; the retained XNU source and native bridge checks support that property. The parent bootstrap is not changed. Linux uses a CLOEXEC control socket and `SCM_RIGHTS` reception with `MSG_CMSG_CLOEXEC`; that branch is not yet executed here. Its `posix_spawn_file_actions_addclosefrom_np` dependency was introduced in [glibc 2.34](https://lists.gnu.org/archive/html/info-gnu/2021-08/msg00001.html). Linux execution and libc-baseline acceptance remain required; older-libc support has neither been demonstrated nor silently waived.

One startup timeout encloses validation, encoding, lookup, borrowed-stderr preparation and handoff. A masked ownership cell retains native or adopted `ProcessHandle` ownership across the timeout result; `finallyPreserving` and the existing reaper perform cleanup. The callback is outside that deadline. OS spawn/filesystem calls and final reaping can delay asynchronous delivery; no hard real-time termination claim is made. Stderr follows the ordinary process contract, including blocking-mode export, caller ownership, stdout aliases and writable duplex handles. GHC's existing writable-handle selection and all-halves device update facilities supply the latter behavior.

### Reproductions and independent review

Two initial fault fixtures failed before their PID marker: a non-protocol launcher caused Mach receive to report `MACH_RCV_TOO_LARGE` before the private handshake. A private C test peer now completes the actual bootstrap, records its PID and resists SIGTERM before stalling. The timeout and cancellation assertions were retained, not replaced with startup-failure expectations.

Two independently reproduced defects were corrected before the first integration boundary: borrowed stdout lost child stderr because file actions replaced fd 1 before copying it; a full borrowed-stderr pipe escaped the startup timeout because flushing preceded that timeout. The same tests failed before the fixes and passed afterward.

The first full privacy check also encountered an IPv4 descriptor already present before IPC creation after hosted HTTP tests. That observation did not establish an owned-IPC leak. The check now runs in a fresh Haskell process; its inner 100 unrelated execs still use `close_fds=False` during 20 owned launches. Deliberately clearing CLOEXEC on the owned IPC socket makes that isolated check fail on the inherited AF_UNIX descriptor. The mutation was removed, the C source matched its original checksum, and the complete build/test suite was rerun.

An independent read-only audit used native Pi `context:fresh`; the canonical parent-history verifier returned `PARENT_HISTORY_ABSENT` at 18:37:33 UTC. Its initial orchestration failed unrelated no-staged-files acceptance bookkeeping; the same fresh child session was revived with the coordinator's verified empty-index evidence. The audit inspected source and receipts but did not execute tests. It found two medium-severity stderr defects: read-side selection rejected a writable `DuplexHandle`, and nonblocking borrowed descriptors were exported without ordinary process normalization. Both were then reproduced by the coordinator and corrected. The added checks preserve duplex use in both directions, compare ordinary/IPC fd flags, transfer all 256 KiB of stderr and retain the borrowed writer. Post-audit fixes were reviewed and executed by the coordinator; no second independent execution claim is made.

### Tests and evidence

Each case in `test/OwnedIpcSpec.hs` has a distinct observable check:

| Case | Behavior verified |
| --- | --- |
| Exact stdio/IPC objects | A real child preserves exact Aeson values, empty/Unicode argv, cwd, empty environment value and captured stderr across separate channels while an IPC read is pending. |
| Ordinary session integration | The existing session runtime initializes over stdio while the separate IPC channel remains usable; the child is subsequently reaped. |
| Invalid configuration | Invalid frames, argv/environment, unsupported process settings and missing launcher/target produce the named failures before callback publication, and option display is redacted. |
| Process flags | A native target reports the expected process group and session identity for the requested flags. |
| Callback failure | The original `FixtureFailure` survives scoped cleanup and the observed PID disappears. |
| Caller cancellation | `AsyncCancelled` survives cancellation of the published scope and its child is reaped. |
| Startup timeout | An executed, bootstrapped, SIGTERM-resistant helper times out and is reaped within the asserted bound. |
| Startup cancellation | Cancelling the unpublished helper preserves exception identity and reaps its recorded PID. |
| Borrowed file stderr | Child stderr reaches the file and the caller can append after scope exit. |
| Duplex stderr | A writable FD-backed duplex handle is accepted, all stderr arrives, and both directions remain usable by the caller. |
| Nonblocking stderr | Ordinary and IPC launches both give the native target blocking stderr, deliver all 256 KiB and preserve the borrowed writer. |
| Stdout alias | Child stderr reaches the caller's original stdout before the caller's own suffix. |
| Full borrowed pipe | A real full pipe reaches the startup deadline during flush without closing the borrowed handle. |
| Callback lifetime | Caller work may outlast the startup budget without being cancelled by it. |
| Blocked IPC I/O | The worker reaches a blocked read or write before cancellation; exception identity, bounded cleanup and PID reaping are checked. |
| Concurrent descriptor privacy | A fresh Haskell process observes clean before/after scans and rejects inherited owned descriptors in 100 unrelated execs during 20 owned launches. |

At the post-audit boundary, the warning-free all-component build and **3,935 tests passed in 29.79 seconds**. Full Ormolu/HLint, five reference-audit tests and schema-integrity checks pass. The exact README example compiles without contacting Factory. A separately compiled **Node v22.23.2** peer passes real stdio/IPC/stderr exchange and reaping for false, zero, empty, NUL, Unicode and nested values; arbitrary-precision preservation remains a Haskell-peer check, not a JavaScript Number claim. The focused process/IPC/validator boundary passes **50 checks in 7.89 seconds**.

Receipts reside under `~/Products/droid-sdk-haskell/owned-ipc-20260912/`. `post-audit-integration.log` and `post-audit-tooling.log` retain exact command lines and results; `audit-stderr-before.log`/`audit-stderr-after.log` retain the independent findings' reproductions and fixes. The audit report is under `~/Products/droid-sdk-haskell/wg-owned-ipc-20260912/audit/report.md`. These local paths are supporting receipts, not portable installation requirements.

An earlier current-IPC source archive independently built and tested its own Rust worker and Haskell package, passing 3,932 tests and matching 370 source/test/native/schema files. It predates the audit fixes; the replacement archive requires its own verification. Haddock completed with the retained dependency/Generic-link warnings, and `cabal check` returned success with the existing `[no-repository]` advisory. Neither diagnostic was suppressed.

### Fess disposition and remaining scope

No production stub, mock engine, disabled assertion, warning suppression or duplicate protocol runtime was introduced. Header availability selects only declarations of the same fileport ABI; a missing executable raises an explicit error rather than selecting another implementation. IOExceptions at process boundaries become payload-free failures; asynchronous exceptions remain exceptions. `finallyPreserving` keeps the initiating cause when terminal cleanup also fails. Native final disposal remains best-effort for exceptional close/Mach-release errors; no fault-injection coverage of every OS resource-exhaustion or disposal failure is claimed.

The C source/header, launcher, process module, native/Haskell peers, Cabal/test registrations and API documentation are required by this capability. Other dirty-tree changes belong to earlier work. The retained-sdist review diff also contained older hosted-MCP prose; that spillover is not attributed to IPC. Generated direnv state and relocated build products follow the user's execution policy, not an SDK API change. The earlier `--max-jobs 0` blocker and repeated permission request were agent errors; `de --max-jobs 4` completed the already-authorized Nix environment. Cabal's cached-archive refusal was handled by verified local sources with the network transport disabled, without changing package versions.

The two independent correctness findings are resolved by parent reproduction and verification. Linux/libc execution, the broader compiler matrix, rare conversion/disposal fault injection, updated distribution evidence and authorized Factory interoperability remain separate evidence limits. No live Factory call, image pull, source publication, Factory account/folder-trust change or commit was performed. The generated local direnv configuration was allowed as part of the requested environment regeneration. The full functional goal remains open.

## Compiler matrix checkpoint

This section preserves the earlier compiler investigation. The later user-approved [toolchain policy](#toolchain-policy) selects GHC9.12; references below to the old default,9.14 gate and shell names are historical.

The proposed compiler matrix remains GHC 9.10.3, 9.12.4 and 9.14.1; this checkpoint adds actual macOS ARM64 evidence without weakening dependency bounds or removing a CI target. `flake.nix` retains the default GHC 9.10.3 development shell and exposes bare `ghc912` and `ghc914` verification shells. The patch-specific `ghc9124` package is used because the shorter Nix `ghc912` alias resolves to 9.12.3 in the pinned package set. Each compiler is checked through the regenerated project direnv environment, with build products under `~/Products/droid-sdk-haskell/compiler-matrix-20260912/`.

Bare GHC 9.12.4 reports `base-4.21.2.0`. Its ordinary Cabal dependency graph resolves without overrides and builds all SDK components with `-Werror`; **3,935 tests pass in 32.21 seconds** (`ghc912-verified.log`). The resolved graph includes Aeson 2.3.1.0 and process 1.6.30.0, exercising the declared ranges rather than injecting the default shell's precompiled libraries. `tested-with` now records 9.12.4 alongside 9.10.3; it does not assert Linux execution.

Bare GHC 9.14.1 reports `base-4.22.0.0`, but strict solving fails through `tls -> serialise-0.2.6.1 -> base <4.22`. Trying the older TLS versions already permitted by this SDK does not remove that dependency. The current [Hackage revision](https://hackage.haskell.org/package/serialise-0.2.6.1) retains the restriction. [Upstream issue 373](https://github.com/well-typed/cborg/issues/373) reports successful builds with relaxed bounds, but neither a report nor compiler availability establishes this package's compatibility. No `allow-newer`, cryptographic downgrade, source-bound patch or matrix reduction was introduced. GHC 9.14.1 remains unresolved.

### Timing-fixture correction and fess

The first 9.12.4 suite passed 3,934 of 3,935 cases; the regex deadline expired before the temporary launcher's PID marker. This was the same fixture assumption previously observed after a 9.10.3 rebuild, not evidence of a new compiler failure. Installed process source forces the fork path for `close_fds=True` and closes descriptor numbers up to `_SC_OPEN_MAX`; the observed soft limit was 1,048,575. A controlled native-program comparison measured about 101–103 ms with that closure loop versus 1.6–1.8 ms without it. The production close policy was not changed.

A separate real-worker probe measured newly written launcher starts at 314–1,198 ms, compared with 104–109 ms on immediate second use. The two deadline tests intend to time engine work, whereas the API correctly permits the whole deadline to expire during startup. Their shared fixture now first obtains a successful response from the real worker, verifies reaping, and removes the preflight PID file. The timed invocation must create its own new marker. The original **500 ms deadline**, **two-second total bound**, expected `ValidatorTimedOut` and PID-reaping assertions remain intact. No assertion is skipped and no production timeout is moved.

The changed compilation-timeout case still checks a hostile schema against the original deadline after fixture deployment is established. The changed regex-timeout case still checks the hostile pattern against that deadline and a fresh PID marker; it does not claim that a cold unique launcher must start within 500 ms. The preflight itself verifies real schema validation and cleanup rather than manufacturing a ready flag. `test/ValidatorSpec.hs` is the only Haskell file changed by this compiler checkpoint.

The initial additional Nix shell preloaded development libraries and unnecessarily built their test-dependency trees. Its owned Nix client was stopped and the verification shells were corrected to bare compilers. Missing solver-selected source archives were fetched as temporary Nix fixed-output inputs, checked against Cabal's exact source hashes, and atomically made available in the existing Cabal cache without overwriting archives. Build-time HTTP transport was disabled. That one-off provisioning manifest and flake attachment were removed after use; their snapshots remain under `source-provisioning/` in the receipt directory, not in shipped configuration. The compiler shells and verified `tested-with` entry are permanent, justified changes.

No production stub, fallback implementation, warning suppression, schema policy change, weakened TLS setting or relaxed package bound was added. Formatting and lint pass for the changed timing fixture. The first cache-publication command misread the single-field `cabal path` output and was corrected before copying sources; no archive was overwritten. Original dependency-resolution and timing failures remain recorded. This is parent execution plus a self-fess checkpoint, not a new independent audit of the complete compiler matrix. The later Linux execution is recorded below; GHC 9.14.1 and the unanswered terminal timestamp policy remain open.

## Linux execution checkpoint

GNU/Linux ARM64 execution was obtained without pulling a compiler image or using a remote build. The optional `linuxChecks` Nix shell provisions public Linux GHC 9.10.3, Cabal, GCC, Rust/Cargo and supporting tool closures while retaining a native host compiler. Their 167 store paths, approximately 5.1 GiB, were mirrored under `~/Products/droid-sdk-haskell/linux-checks-20260912/` and checked for identical NAR hashes. The full host store was not exposed. The already-cached image `sha256:b515b04bfd25298a5cdc775b2fcd48b9399bab658ce13e2598b65df1b16098c8` supplied the Linux container environment, with its application entry point replaced by the verified Nix tools.

Execution uses `--pull=never`, no networking, read-only root/store, dropped capabilities, no-new-privileges and the unprivileged caller UID. Writable state is limited to the copied SDK source/build workspace and temporary filesystem. Only public Hackage index data and 119 checksum-verified archives were copied into an isolated Cabal cache; the Linux worker builds from Nix-vendored sources. No Factory credentials or host Cargo/Cabal configuration were mounted. These are local execution facilities, not SDK runtime dependencies.

The native C checks execute on Linux 7.0.12-linuxkit/aarch64. IPC stdio/stderr/message transfer, CLOEXEC, same-PID adoption/reaping and exec failure pass. The birth-time binding matches direct `statx` seconds/nanoseconds, remains unchanged after modification-time edits and reports EBADF; birth time is present on the exercised filesystem. The Linux Rust worker builds and passes its three tests. The full Haskell package builds with `-Werror`, then passes **3,935 tests in 37.62 seconds**, five reference audits, integrity checks and package check (`linux-full-final.log`). After the fixture changes, macOS/GHC 9.12.4 also passes **3,935 tests in 32.10 seconds** (`macos-regression.log`).

### Failure-driven fixture corrections

The first Linux suite reported nine failures. Four fixture assumptions, rather than a substitute SDK implementation, were corrected:

| Observation | Correction and retained contract |
| --- | --- |
| `/tmp` was mounted `noexec`, so generated validator launchers were unavailable. | The existing fixture now respects configured `TMPDIR`. The mount was not relaxed, and executable availability, validation, deadline and reaping assertions remain. |
| An explicitly empty child environment selected a non-UTF-8 argv decoder. | Only the IPC self-peer re-decodes its raw argv as UTF-8 after entering peer mode. The exact Unicode assertion and deliberately empty environment remain; production argument encoding is unchanged. |
| A fresh Haskell runtime already had two inheritable control-pipe descriptors before any process use. | The isolated privacy peer marks only its pre-existing baseline CLOEXEC before allocating IPC resources. It still checks every unexpected descriptor, and all 100 inner inspector execs retain `close_fds=False`. No newly allocated IPC descriptor is ignored by number or kind. |
| The blocked-write cancellation completed, but the deliberately tiny TCP receive window stalled the peer's final closure drain. | Stage tracing established the ordering. A post-cancellation window increase was tried and reverted when it did not resolve the timeout. The fixture now uses a 64 KiB receive window and a 16 MiB message with a 32 MiB message limit, explicitly observes the sender thread blocked before cancelling, and retains `AsyncCancelled`, peer-side EOF/reset and the original ten-second bound. |

The descriptor correction was tested adversarially: removing `MSG_CMSG_CLOEXEC` in the isolated copied C source made the test fail on newly inherited pipe and socket descriptors. The source was restored and compared with the canonical C file; all seventeen targeted IPC/WebSocket checks then pass in 2.36 seconds. The fixture does not make newly allocated IPC descriptors safe on behalf of production code. Both the failure and restoration are retained.

Production transport, validation, timestamp and permission semantics were not altered by these corrections. The modified Haskell files are `test/Main.hs`, `test/ValidatorSpec.hs`, `test/OwnedIpcSpec.hs` and `test/WebSocketSpec.hs`; additional Nix configuration supplies the actual execution tools. A 75-second combined build/test tool call expired before the full suite result; the completed separate run is the passing receipt, not that timeout. Exact commands, stage diagnostics, mirrored-store checksums and negative/positive results reside in the Products directory above. The Linux source snapshot incorporates explicit copies of the canonical fixture changes and requires a fresh archive check before being described as an unchanged unpacked distribution.

The original fresh-context auditor completed this bounded Linux review with the verified history-isolation provenance retained, finding no blocking regression or weakened substantive assertion. ## Approved timestamp and attribution contracts

On 2026-09-13 the user approved retaining strict timestamp strings and omitting unsupported optional SDK-language metadata. This settles the recorded policy questions without inventing a Haskell enum member or a JavaScript Date compatibility layer. The existing runtime already implements those choices; this batch changes its metadata Haddock explanation, adds focused tests, and reconciles current documentation and issue evidence.

**Behavior checked.** `test/DaemonTerminalClientSpec.hs` exercises real native WebSocket/RPC decoding: three spellings retain offsets, submillisecond precision and leap seconds; thirteen invalid/coercive inputs are tested independently in each timestamp field. Both listing and restoration reject them with `RpcInvalidResult`, retain a seeded terminal entry, and permit a subsequent resize request. The fixture also asserts absent default `_meta` and the truthful `haskell-sdk` debugging caller. The existing local launch test verifies ordinary upstream labels are removed before the native child initializes; existing metadata tests preserve omission/null distinctions and reject an invented Haskell enum value.

`test/TimestampSpec.hs` now makes the documented occurrence boundary executable: a syntactically positioned future leap string is retained without asserting that an IERS event will occur. Existing calendar, offset and invalid-position checks remain. RFC 3339 section 5.7 distinguishes occurrence announcements from grammar; required terminal APIs retain observations rather than generate leap events. MCP modification times separately reject second 60. The existing worktree-profile codec remains unchanged. No occurrence catalog or forecasting facility appears in these required SDK operations.

**Fess and evidence.** Parent self-review found no new runtime stub, fallback, swallowed error, suppression or unrelated source change. These are new checks of retained behavior, not a claim to have repaired a previously failing timestamp implementation. The peer supplies the selected wire contract, not a live Factory response; the older TypeScript coercion mismatch remains explicit. Initial Ormolu import-order/layout findings were corrected, and the failure log is retained. The focused baseline passed 306 tests; the expanded focus passed 310 in 0.42s, including the native launch case. The all-component `-Werror` build and all 3,941 tests pass in 31.53s; changed-file Ormolu/HLint, five audit tests and reference integrity pass. The shipped example builds; README code blocks and public signatures are unchanged. Commands, logs and the scoped source diff are under `~/Products/droid-sdk-haskell/contract-policy-20260913/`.

**Verification limits.** This is parent execution/source review, not a fresh independent review, new Linux/compiler run, live test or independent archive build. The existing archives predate these added tests. Remaining release gates are unchanged: required compiler/architecture evidence, authorized live checks, unresolved package/Haddock metadata diagnostics and final distribution sign-off. No commits, publication, new dependencies or compiler/cache expansion occurred.

## September 14 SDK authentication source correction

At the user's explicit request, the authentication investigation checked current Python source at `6d06b4e613aab3990cf2ced469b5c4e80053e52e` and the integrity-verified published TypeScript SDK 0.9.1. This is a targeted authentication comparison, not a replacement of the frozen functional baselines. Python's `_high_level/_client.py` passes `api_key` through the owned CLI's `FACTORY_API_KEY` environment; `transport.py` supplies Python SDK attribution. Current TypeScript's `createTransport` uses the same environment channel with TypeScript attribution. Its daemon `connectToDaemon` sends an `apiKey` credential, whereas 0.7.0 routed that option through the WorkOS `token` field. The earlier 0.7.0 daemon rejection was therefore not a sound comparison for SDK-key validity.

The current daemon schema keeps `caller` a free debugging string, but requires `metadata.sdk` exactly when `caller` is `sdk`. That metadata accepts only `typescript` or `python`. A prototype that changed the Haskell caller to `sdk` without metadata was rejected by the actual CLI with `RpcParseError`. It was fully reverted, together with its changed fixture expectations and launch-label prototype. The same current SDK schema definitions accept the retained `haskell-sdk`/`apiKey` shape and reject both partial SDK attribution and an invented Haskell language value. Four source-extracted schema checks verify these distinctions without a network request. The first checker incorrectly assumed the private schema was exported; its import failure is retained. No compatibility branding, schema relaxation or authentication bypass was adopted.

Direct SDK executions initially used the supplied file privately: Python 0.4.0 and TypeScript 0.9.1 each attempted one bounded HELLO through installed CLI 0.218.1. Python returned `error_during_execution`; TypeScript returned `401 status code (no body)`. Current TypeScript daemon authentication also failed against both installed CLI 0.217.0 and 0.218.1. These were failures of the tested SDK configuration, not proof that the key was inactive in the user's working application. The request for a working SDK comparison was later superseded by the newly supplied key and successful checks below. Two descendants of each earlier local reference run were still observed immediately after scope return and absent on later inspection; both original observations remain, with no forced cleanup or exhaustive-census claim.

Receipts are under `~/Products/droid-sdk-haskell/sdk-auth-20260914/`. Every Haskell source/test file was restored byte-for-byte to the pre-investigation archive; the restored all-component `-Werror` build and public-probe rebuild pass. Prototype-only four-test success is not authentication evidence. The prototype integration run passed 3,940 of 3,941 tests but exposed an intermittent appended-record observation failure at `test/SavedSessionSpec.hs:204`; a correctly invoked focused check passed afterward. That unresolved finding is tracked as `hsdk-fn4`. Later bounded diagnosis did not reproduce the failure; temporary tracing was removed byte-for-byte, and the restored full suite passed 3,941 tests in 29.07 seconds. That pass does not establish a fix. The intermediate direct-executable retry lacked Cabal's data-file setup and did not run a test. No source-distribution refresh or independent review followed from the diagnostic investigation.

## September 14 saved-session observer correction

The intermittent `hsdk-fn4` count was caused by the fixture's intrusive cursor observation, not by changing the scanner's size limit. A native reproduction read an unchanged 16 MiB sparse file containing one header newline while another thread called `lseek(fd, 0, SEEK_CUR)`. One of 300 reads returned that newline twice, the second at logical byte 4,098: the reader had replayed its prefix without any append. The retained [Apple XNU source](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/main/bsd/vfs/vfs_syscalls.c) shows the relative seek reading `fg_offset` and subsequently writing the value back even for a zero offset. The downloaded source and its digest are retained with the reproduction.

`test/FilePosition.c` now queries offset metadata through `proc_pidfdinfo` on macOS and `/proc/self/fdinfo` on Linux. The latter opens its metadata descriptor with `O_CLOEXEC` and closes it after reading. `SavedSessionSpec.descriptorsFor` uses this test-only bridge; the library's scanner, snapshot budget, message-count assertion, cancellation identity and descriptor-cleanup checks remain unchanged. The added position test observes zero, 65,537 and 4,294,967,297 twice, checking the bridge's values and 64-bit representation without requiring a petabyte-scale filesystem offset. The macOS `proc` link is confined to the test component.

Verification under GHC 9.12.4 with `-Werror`:

- Original native seek observer: **1/300 failures**, with the failing prefix replay retained.
- Non-seeking native observer: **0/300 failures**, **5,227,002 observations**, with nonzero observer execution required in every trial.
- Affected scanning/selection tests: **53/53** on macOS and Linux.
- Complete suites: **3,942/3,942**, macOS **31.31s**, GNU/Linux ARM64 **36.86s**.
- Touched Haskell formatting/HLint and native C warning checks passed.

Sources, logs and the unmodified initial reproduction are under `~/Products/droid-sdk-haskell/append-snapshot-20260914/`. The Linux development source was refreshed from the current tree after its previous contents were retained intact as `src-before-append-snapshot-20260914`; no historical unpacked release was overwritten and no new tool/image provisioning was needed.

Scoped self-review found no production scanner workaround, weakened assertion, mock, skipped check, suppression or fallback. The native stress result is observed evidence, not exhaustive proof for every kernel or schedule. These checks are parent execution, not a new independent review. The original failure and unsuccessful early probes remain recorded; they are explained by the reproduced cursor mutation rather than erased by later passing tests.
